       APPENDIX A: A COMMON OPERATOR BEYOND THE OBSERVED CATALOGUE
17                                                                                                 17

18     A coefficient vector checked at finitely many tasks need not work at a new task. 18
19   The constructive NBO has a stronger route when the economic primitives provide                19

20   joint regularity. We state the necessary regularity on states and contexts rather than        20

21   assuming successful extrapolation by a fitted nine-coefficient readout.                       21

22     Let r = (s, x ) range over an invariant compact subset Kt of R pt , where s contains        22

23   declared economic contexts and any endogenous preference variables. Actions and               23

24   transitions are defined on this augmented domain. Suppose the primitive moduli,               24

25   Hausdorff-continuous feasible menus and measurable repairs in Theorem 2 hold                  25

26   jointly in r, with constants Lt , Dt , KtA . Let an effective cover have St nodes and         26

27   radius ht . At each node obtain its own-future minimized label with lower error −et           27

28   and upper error Dt k t + et . Repair displacement is bounded by KtA ∥r − ri ∥1 + ζ t .        28

29                                                                                                 29

30                                                                                                 30

31                                                                                                 31

32     Qian QI: qiqian@pku.edu.cn                                                                  32

1        T HEOREM 1—Population construction with one shared neural circuit: The single               1

2    function                                                                                        2

3                                                                                                    3

4
                                   f t (r ) = min{yti + Lt ∥r − rti ∥1 }                             4
                                            i ≤ St
5                                                                                                    5
     has an exact affine–ReLU circuit, a measurable original-owner actor and the bounds (6)
6                                                                                                    6
     uniformly on Kt . Consequently (7), with the joint-domain constants, holds for every initial
7                                                                                                    7
     context and every admissible evolution remaining in those domains. Contexts may be
8                                                                                                    8
     generated endogenously and adaptively. No independence assumption on their deployment
9                                                                                                    9
     sequence is needed for this uniform deterministic assertion.
10                                                                                                   10
         The circuit uses O( pt St ) nonzero scalar operations and weights, with absolute-value
11                                                                                                   11
     gates and a balanced minimum tree of depth O(log St ). If the joint domains have effective
12                                                                                                   12

13
     ℓ1 covering numbers at most Ct h−kt , this yields St ≤ Ct h− kt
                                                                t . It is the explicitly shared      13
     joint-domain circuit, not a collection of task-specific networks. The original-owner and
14                                                                                                   14
     repair obligations remain necessary.
15                                                                                                   15

16                                                                                                   16

17       This is a constructive population theorem, not a claim that the small feature               17

18   maps in the historical experiments satisfy its approximation bound. The cover- 18
19   ing dimension k t , the ambient readout cost pt , acquisition, integration and label            19

20   work are charged separately. A low intrinsic covering dimension must be estab- 20
21   lished for the declared economic family. The same cone also has a native min-plus               21

22   implementation: exact equivalence of two encodings is not neural superiority.                   22

23                                                                                                   23

24       P ROPOSITION 2—Acquisition coverage under persistent exploration: Choose M                  24

25   deterministic radius-h balls whose centers cover K. If, before each acquisition i, every such   25

26   ball has conditional probability at least ah > 0 of containing the next node, then after n      26

27   acquisitions the probability that the acquired nodes fail to form a radius-2h cover is at       27

28   most M(1 − ah )n ≤ M exp(−nah ). Thus n ≥ [log M + log(1/δ)]/ah is sufficient. The              28

29   assertion permits an adaptive acquisition law satisfying the conditional lower bound; it        29

30   does not silently replace such a law by IID sampling. On the coverage event, Theorem 1          30

31   applies with ht = 2h, provided the own-future labels and primitive moduli have their stated     31

32   simultaneous certification.                                                                     32

1                            A.1. From approximation to predictor work                                 1

2                                                                                                      2
       The certificate’s validity and a proposal’s value are different. Consider a complete
3
     finite action menu at a query, with true objective Q and estimates having simultane- 3
4                                                                                                      4
     ous radius at most e. Select a minimum estimated objective and close only when its
5
     upper endpoint does not exceed every competitor’s lower endpoint. Let ∆ be the                    5

6
     true best-to-second-best gap, with ∆ = 0 for ties.                                                6

7                                                                                                      7

8      P ROPOSITION 3—A distributional work consequence of uniform accuracy: The                       8

9    preceding test closes whenever ∆ > 4e. Suppose, uniformly over the relevant policy-induced        9

10   query laws, the tie probability is at most p0   and Pr(0 < ∆ ≤ z) ≤ Czα for z > 0. If proposal    10

11   and certification work are at most h(e) and a protected fallback uses at most W+ , then           11

12                                                                                                     12
                              EW ≤ h(e) + W+ min{1, p0 + C (4e)α }.
13                                                                                                     13

14   If the always-conventional comparator has expected work at least W− , a positive saving           14

15   follows when the displayed upper bound is below W− . Comparing two upper bounds is                15

16   not sufficient. The assertion is componentwise for nonnegative operation ledgers, or for a        16

17   separately declared nonnegative resource-price vector. It is not a wall-clock assertion without   17

18   a valid mapping from the ledger to time.                                                          18

19                                                                                                     19

20
       Uniformity over induced query laws is essential: a margin observed under a                      20

21
     baseline state distribution does not establish the bound after the policy changes. 21
22
     The tie term cannot be discarded. A representation-specific empirical comparison                  22

23
     must measure h(e), achieved accuracy and the relevant query distribution for each                 23

24
     representation. The constructed neural circuit provides an explicit member of this                24

25
     comparison; protected fallback alone supplies no evidence about neural training                   25

26
     reliability. Proofs are in Supplement 9.                                                          26

     APPENDIX B: A CCURACY ALLOCATION WITH ENDOGENOUS SUCCESSOR COST
28                                                                                                     28

29     The separable allocation in Proposition 19 applies when its coefficients are fixed in- 29
30   dependently of the allowances being optimized. A dense successor circuit generally                30

31   has a size that depends on its own allowance. Charging that size at the preceding                 31

32   date couples the two choices. We now optimize this dependency explicitly.                         32

1    At the optimizer, the Karush–Kuhn–Tucker multipliers make these bounds equal. For any          1

2    candidate, the choice ν = 0 and λ = maxt {− gt /wt } supplies a valid, possibly loose, lower   2

3    bound. With integer exponents and rational inputs and candidates, every check is rational      3

4    arithmetic. For other exponents, certified enclosures of the function and derivatives are      4

5    required.                                                                                      5

6      Put P = max j ∑t a jt . Rounding each allowance down by less than a factor two preserves     6

7    feasibility and gives                                                                          7

10   The exponent is the total degree of a coupled term, not merely the exponent of the date at     10

11   which that term is paid.                                                                       11

12                                                                                                  12
       The proof in Supplement 13 includes attainment, strict convexity and the cer-
13                                                                                                  13
     tificate. Convex duality and geometric programming are established techniques
14                                                                                                  14
     (Boyd and Vandenberghe, 2004, Boyd et al., 2007); their role here is to preserve
15                                                                                                  15
     the successor dependency in the NBO’s original accuracy account. The certificate
16                                                                                                  16
     bounds the optimum of an explicitly stated resource upper model. It is not a lower
17                                                                                                  17
     bound on the work of every algorithm solving the economic problem.

24                                                                                                  24
     For a dense successor account with label exponent p and evaluation exponent s, the adjacent-
25                                                                                                  25
     date term has degree p + s. Thus a valid polynomial horizon account charges p + s, rather
26                                                                                                  26
     than silently holding the successor cost constant. Worsening regularity, growing cover
27                                                                                                  27
     dimension, bit lengths and setup remain explicit inputs; none is bounded by this corollary
28                                                                                                  28
     without its stated hypotheses.

     6

1    The equal allocation costs 52. The current numerical appendix gives a rational                       1

2    feasible allocation, its independently recomputed lower certificate and its gap. The                 2

3    example isolates the accuracy dependency algebraically; its resource units are not                   3

4    fitted runtime measurements. It also provides a regression test that rejects a negative              4

5    multiplier, an infeasible budget or a forged lower bound. In the original economy                    5

6    the same construction applies only after its coefficients have been derived from the                 6

7    actual label, action, integration and stored-circuit accounts.                                       7

8                                                                                                         8

9
         Policy and resource certificates. Qualification determines which policy outputs                  9

10
     meet the economic accuracy requirement. The coupled program determines how a                         10

11
     specified implementation distributes the corresponding dated allowances. These are                   11

12
     different optimizations. A tight resource certificate cannot replace an original-law                 12

13
     policy certificate, and a tight policy certificate does not determine hardware time. 13

14
     Keeping both accounts explicit permits constructive improvement without a claim                      14

15
     of dimension-free or universally faster computation.                                                 15

17       APPENDIX C: P ROSPECTIVE CHOICE AND ECONOMICALLY INTERPRETABLE                                   17

18                                           COMPARISONS                                                  18

19       Exact ranking on a completed catalogue and preference before a new workload                      19

20   are different claims. We connect them by a population selection bound and a                          20

21   separately frozen evaluation, retaining the original investment economy and every                    21

22   comparator.                                                                                          22

23                                                                                                        23

24       P ROPOSITION 6—Population regret for a fixed service menu: Condition on con- 24
25   struction of m complete services, each with original-law cost in [0, H ]. For n independent          25

26   new workloads, obtain valid pathwise cost enclosures [l ji , u ji ]. Write l¯j , ū j for endpoint   26
                       p
27   means and qn = H log(2m/δ)/(2n). Select the service minimizing λū j + p · w j + f j ,               27

28   with a fixed tie rule. Here λ ≥ 0, p ≥ 0, and the nonrandom resource and fee inputs are              28

29   declared separately. With probability at least 1 − δ, simultaneously for every such price-fee        29

30   vector, its expected objective regret is at most                                                     30

31                                                                                                        31
                                        λ{2qn + max(ū j − l¯j )}.

                                C.1. The frozen independent evaluation
8                                                                                                         8

9      The prospective protocol preceding the retained evaluation fixes the nine com- 9
10   plete two-date investment controllers: the zero controller and both ReLU and Bern- 10
11   stein readouts, under both legacy and calibrated qualification, at acquisition sizes                 11

12   eight and sixteen. Coefficients, complete action tables, constraints and original                    12

13   continuous innovation laws are unchanged. There are 8,192 calibration bins and                       13

14   131,072 distinct evaluation bins, with forty bits per coordinate. Selection uses the                 14

15   smallest calibration conditioned upper-endpoint mean, with lexical ties, and is fixed                15

16   before evaluation.                                                                                   16

17     Both independently written whole-bin evaluators run on every policy and bin;                       17

18   rational midpoint fixtures check their hulls. The two estimators are cash cost and                   18

19   analytically conditioned cash cost. Each stream has 36 unordered pairs for each                      19

20   estimator. The empirical-Bernstein error account used in the original study as- 20
21   signs 4 exp(−11) to each interval, yielding 288 exp(−11) < 0.005 per stream and                      21

22   576 exp(−11) < 0.01 jointly. No fitting-success event is inferred. The 139,264 paired                22

23   workload rows count once, rather than once per policy or per comparison. Re- 23
24   constructing the frozen streams during the current build adds no independent                         24

25   observations.                                                                                        25

26     Calibration selects the fine calibrated Bernstein controller. In the retained evalu- 26
27   ation, its upper expected-cost regret is less than 0.000573, below the prespecified                  27

28   1/256 noninferiority margin. Seven of its eight comparisons have negative upper                      28

29   endpoints. Its comparison with fine calibrated ReLU crosses zero and supports                        29

30   equivalence at that margin, not strict basis superiority. The resolution comparison                  30

31   within calibrated Bernstein is signed. The crossed basis-by-qualification effect is a                31

32   separate factorial estimand, not a change in the underlying economy. Full endpoint                   32
     8

                                    C.2. Uniform price-fee sensitivity
26                                                                                                              26

27       Let X be a finite set of feasible service assignments, including the original capacity                 27

28   constraints. Let D be a nonempty compact polytope containing their joint normal- 28
29   ized cost vector. For an assignment x, let wx be its complete resource vector, λ ≥ 0                       29

30   the value of normalized policy loss, p ≥ 0 the resource prices, and f the assignment                       30

31   fee vector. All coordinates refer to explicit units. Put hD (v) = supc∈D c · v.                            31

32                                                                                                              32
                               NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                               9

1      P ROPOSITION 7—Uniform purchase region: Assignment x is optimal for every cost                1

2    vector in D exactly on the region                                                               2

3                     \                                                                              3
              Rx =         {(λ, p, f ) : λhD ( x − y) + p · (wx − wy ) + f · ( x − y) ≤ 0}.    (5)
4                                                                                                    4
                     y∈X
5                                                                                                    5
     This region is polyhedral. Rational support-function upper certificates give an independently
6                                                                                                    6
     checkable sufficient region. Uniformity over a compact polytope of price-fee inputs can be
7                                                                                                    7
     checked at its vertices. The supporting equalities give the breakpoints at which a guaranteed
8                                                                                                    8
     choice can change. A point outside the sufficient region is not automatically evidence that
9                                                                                                    9
     the candidate is inferior.
10                                                                                                   10

11     For a single selected service and comparator k, a simultaneous cost upper bound               11

12   ubj,k gives the transparent sufficient condition                                                12

13                                                                                                   13

14                                 f bj − f k + p · (wbj − wk ) ≤ −λubj,k .                          14

15                                                                                                   15
     The numerical appendix prints every selected-service breakpoint, including neg-
16                                                                                                   16
     ative ones. In particular, the fine-versus-coarse calibrated Bernstein policy com-
17                                                                                                   17
     parison supports a positive extra-fee allowance even at zero computation price,
18                                                                                                   18
     whereas a comparison whose upper cost endpoint is positive cannot justify a posi-
19                                                                                                   19
     tive fee solely from policy quality. A resource-driven choice is distinguished from
20                                                                                                   20
     a policy-cost-driven one. Application-specific prices, energy costs and monetary
21                                                                                                   21
     scales require independent calibration; none is manufactured from the normalized
22                                                                                                   22
     tolerance or the favorable timing repetition.
23                                                                                                   23

24     Evidence identities. The inherited experiments have distinct observation units. 24
25   The retained R67 description records 212 service returns and 48 neural fitting                  25

26   warnings; the later training, validation, reuse and vector catalogue records 188                26

27   complete processes. The attained-policy factorial study instead has nine controllers            27

28   and its own independent path rows. The current complete-menu calculation has                    28

29   2,548 qualification certificates and no new fits. Counts from different catalogues,             29

30   including the 336-service description in the advisory report, are not added together            30

31   or assigned to this evaluation without a source-matched manifest. The finer-bin                 31

32   experiment does not erase the older adverse timing and horizon results. Every                   32

4    APPENDIX D: A FROZEN CONTINUATION COMPARISON UNDER RECURSIVE RISK                          4

5                                                                                               5
       We apply the preceding construction to d = 2 at both declared risk levels. There
6                                                                                               6
     are four arms: a fitted oblique ReLU continuation, an additive linear-spline con-
7                                                                                               7
     tinuation, a projected-quadratic continuation, and conventional online evaluation
8                                                                                               8
     of the original one-date risk Bellman problem. The last arm pays for that calcula-
9                                                                                               9
     tion; it is not supplied with a free optimal-value oracle. Every fitted label is itself
10                                                                                              10
     computed from the original terminal primitive and a paid all-action certificate. The
11                                                                                              11
     terminal continuation is the known g, so this last-date fit is consistent with the
12                                                                                              12
     own-future construction rather than training against an externally supplied optimal
13                                                                                              13
     continuation.
14                                                                                              14

15                                                                                              15
                           D.1. Frozen design and common accuracy
16                                                                                              16

17     Each risk level has 1,024 training states, 128 calibration states and 2,048 evaluation   17

18   states. The three streams are domain-separated before execution. States are drawn          18

19   from the declared uniform 20-bit dyadic initial-state distribution; the state dynamics     19

20   and innovations between decisions remain continuous. Each fitted arm uses the              20

21   same labels, 32 fixed nonlinear features, an unrestricted affine part and nonnegative      21

22   nonlinear coefficients. Least squares uses the same solver tolerance and iteration         22

23   cap; readouts are stored on a 30-bit dyadic grid. The source, serialized coefficients      23

24   and calibration selector are checked before evaluation. No fitting or reselection          24

25   occurs on the evaluation stream.                                                           25

26     The selector minimizes calibration mean cost-interval midpoints with a fixed             26

27   lexicographic tie rule. It chooses online risk-DP at both risk levels. All twelve          27

28   pairwise comparisons across the two levels were specified in advance, including            28

29   those not involving the selected arm. The independent observation is an initial state,     29

30   not a shock bin or a repeated solver query.                                                30

31     A common own-continuation gap is insufficient for a fair policy comparison. 31
32   We require both an own-continuation gap at most 1/64 and an original-optimum               32
                               NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                          11

1    complete-policy loss bound at most 1/16 at every queried initial state. Fitted arms                        1

2    pay for the extra original-continuation lower witness in Theorem 13; online risk-DP                        2

3    reuses its already computed witness. Integration may refine from 128 to 4,096 bins,                        3

4    but the proposed first action cannot change. All failures are retained rather than                         4

5    silently replaced by conventional actions. The terminal controller is common to                            5

6    every arm and has the uniform allowance proved above.                                                      6

     Each fit uses the same 1,024 labels and 32 nonlinear features plus an affine part. The quadratic feature
17                                                                                                              17
     matrix has rank six, not 35; the experiment matches nominal features and labels, not independent
18                                                                                                              18
     parameter rank. The residual enclosures cover every state in the continuous square.
19                                                                                                              19

20     The ReLU fits have the smallest training RMSE and the narrowest whole-square                             20

21   residual brackets in both cases. These are distinct observations: training errors alone                    21

22   do not give a whole-domain certificate. The quadratic design has effective rank six                        22

23   although all three fitted arms have 35 stored coefficients; matching feature counts is                     23

24   therefore not matching approximation spaces or statistical degrees of freedom. No                          24

25   rank-matched representation superiority is inferred from this comparison.                                  25

27                    D.2. A common-policy comparison of certificate accounts                                   27

       Let [l, u] be the verified whole-square bracket for V1 − f , E = max{|l |, |u|}, and

     δ( x ) the own-continuation all-action gap. Two valid original-optimum bounds for
30                                                                                                              30
     the same complete policy are
31                                                                                                              31

32           Bnorm ( x ) = δ( x ) + β(2E + ε 1 ),         Bsigned ( x ) = δ( x ) + β(u − l + ε 1 ).             32

1    Monotonicity and cash invariance prove these inequalities directly. The joint local- 1

     Cost enclosures here contain numerical uncertainty only. Simultaneous sampling intervals for all

22                                                                                                      22

23                                                                                                      23

24     The estimand is the initial-distribution mean of each complete policy’s original                 24

25   nested-risk value. It is not average realized cash expenditure or a monetary welfare               25

26   estimate. The fixed margin 1/16 equals four fifths of the maximal first-date invest- 26
27   ment charge h(1/4) = 5/64 in this normalized model. It is a declared numerical                     27

28   economic tolerance; judging it small for a particular application requires an external             28

29   economic calibration.                                                                              29

30     All twelve simultaneous empirical-Bernstein intervals lie strictly inside                        30

31   [−1/16, 1/16]. Thus all four complete policies are equivalent at that prespecified                 31

32   tolerance on the declared initial distribution. None of the intervals excludes zero,               32
     14

1    so none establishes strict superiority. In particular, the narrower ReLU residual                           1

2    bracket does not translate here into an identified improvement over online risk-DP. 2
3    The numerical mean-cost brackets in Table D.III are not themselves sampling                                 3

4    confidence intervals; the full paired intervals and their sampling components are                           4

5    reported in the appendix.                                                                                   5

6      The numerical component includes original-law integration, terminal observation                           6

7    and optimization, and outward arithmetic. Initial-state discretization is part of the                       7

8    declared evaluation distribution, not an unreported approximation to a continuous                           8

9    uniform initial distribution. There is no transition-law substitution by a state grid,                      9

10   and therefore no hidden state-aggregation allowance in these costs. Sampling and                            10

11   numerical uncertainty are combined as proved in the supplement. The protocol                                11

12   does not permit changing the margin or sample size after examining the evaluation                           12

13   outcomes.                                                                                                   13

     Every arm processes all 2,048 initial states. Timings include first-action proposals, qualification, the
29                                                                                                               29

                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                            15

1      In the completed execution, the fitted arms require approximately 11.2–12.3             1

2    seconds per 2,048-state risk-level workload, compared with approximately 30.8–32.5        2

3    seconds for online risk-DP. These measurements include the extra original-optimum         3

4    witness required of the fitted arms. Preparation for the entire two-risk-level, six-fit   4

5    comparison takes approximately 8.79 seconds. A separately coded rational audit            5

6    takes approximately 89.98 seconds and checks the continuous-risk kernels and              6

7    retained evidence; that verification is a separate paid campaign cost, not zero-cost      7

8    validation.                                                                               8

9      These measurements identify a benefit of stored continuations on this workload,         9

10   not a uniquely neural advantage. Spline and quadratic continuations obtain similar        10

11   execution reductions, and the fitted arms are not rank matched. The counts identify       11

12   the repeated terminal computations avoided by storage; they do not identify the cost      12

13   of every machine instruction. The reported timings are from one x86-64 execution. 13
14   Two earlier tool-interrupted starts are recorded separately, without recoverable          14

15   complete cost ledgers. Accordingly the table is not a claim about the aggregate cost      15

16   of every attempted run, nor a cross-architecture, energy or asymptotic scalability        16

17   result.                                                                                   17

18     Whole-bin screening excludes between 738 and 746 of 4,096 candidates at α = 1/2,        18

19   and between 2,053 and 2,062 at α = 3/4. After paying for unpruned original-value          19

20   bounds and the strict test, the screened calculation is slightly slower in every          20

21   displayed case. The exact unchanged tail values validate the exclusions; they do          21

22   not establish a computational gain. No-cut rows and all denominators are retained         22

23   in the appendix. The experiment therefore separates a valid geometric certificate,        23

24   the amount it removes and the net value of constructing it.                               24

25     The two-date scalar-control experiment does not replace the inherited two-control,      25

26   two-innovation horizon frontier. Proposition 14 now gives explicit fixed-accuracy         26

27   resolutions and a horizon-uniform construction for both the scalar and original           27

28   vector economies. The general NBO construction and joint-domain theorem remain            28

29   available under their hypotheses, while a full independent largest-vector imple- 29
30   mentation remains a separate numerical obligation. This distinction preserves the         30

31   general research program without borrowing scalability evidence from a different          31

32   problem.                                                                                  32

19   A zero projection radius is removed; if all radii vanish the mean is z+ . Consequently P a h is   19

20   a finite exact rational calculation for rational primitives, states, actions and weights. For n   20

21   trajectories and at most K neurons per date, its arithmetic-operation count is O(nTKd),           21

26     The operation count does not bound rational bit complexity. Near-zero projected                 26

27   radii can also make a naive floating evaluation unstable; directed rounding or                    27

29   moment catalogue checks the rational identities against an independently written                  29

30   polygon integral. It is a conditional-integration experiment, not a new trained-policy            30

31   cost comparison. The formulas do not imply that the achieved residual oscillation                 31

1                   E.3. Qualifying a fitted continuation without a Bellman tree                      1

2                                                                                                     2
       Uniform qualification need not query V ∗ . Suppose Qth is Lipschitz in state and
3                                                                                                     3
     action with constants L x,t , L a,t , and ht has constant Lh,t , all in ℓ1 . In the investment
4                                                                                                     4
     capacity simplex, an action feasible at y can be repaired continuously at x with
5                                                                                                     5
     displacement at most κ ∥ x − y∥1 , where κ = 1/(8d). Thus a valid residual Lipschitz
6                                                                                                     6
     constant is
7                                                                                                     7

8                                     Lr,t = L x,t + κL a,t + Lh,t .                                  8

9                                                                                                     9
     Let a finite state cover have fill radius ρ, and let [l j , u j ] enclose rth at every cover
10                                                                                                    10
     point. Then
11                                                                                                    11

12                            min l j − Lr,t ρ ≤ rth ( x ) ≤ max u j + Lr,t ρ                 (13)    12
                               j                              j
13                                                                                                    13
     throughout the cube. At a cover point, a feasible action mesh of fill radius s gives
14                                                                                                    14
     a lower infimum bound by subtracting L a,t s from the smallest action-value lower
15                                                                                                    15
     endpoint; the smallest upper endpoint is an upper bound. Exact moments can
16                                                                                                    16
     supply those action endpoints. None of these steps requires convexity of h or a
17                                                                                                    17
     true-value curvature minorant for Qh .
18                                                                                                    18
       For Nt state-cover points and At actions per point, this qualification costs
19                                                                                                    19
     O(∑t Nt At Kt+1 d) arithmetic operations, plus certification of the moduli and any
20                                                                                                    20
     actor-specific greedy-gap bound. Its cover can still be expensive in high dimension.
21                                                                                                    21
     The result replaces a recursive optimal-value oracle by an explicit approximation-
22                                                                                                    22
     and-qualification tradeoff; it does not make that qualification free. The residual-
23                                                                                                    23

24                                                                                                    24

25                                                                                                    25

              APPENDIX F: F ITTING A CONTINUATION FOR QUALIFICATION
29                                                                                                    29

30     A continuation used to evaluate a policy should be fitted to the error that de- 30
31   termines the evaluation cost. The residual-cover bound in (13) supplies such an                  31

32   objective. We now make its optimization constructive. The future continuation is                 32

16   hθ ) ≤ W (θ ). Minimizing (14) is a finite linear program with 2K + 2 variables and 2N + 16
17   2K inequality constraints, apart from variable bounds. For probability vectors p, q on the      17

18   nodes, set λk = 2ρLk and                                                                        18

                 D( p, q) = p⊤ y+ − q⊤ y− + 2ρC − ∑ Bk |[ F ⊤ (q − p)]k | − λk + .

21                                                                                                   21
     Then D( p, q) ≤ infθ W (θ ), with equality for some p, q. Thus any feasible readout and exact
22                                                                                                   22
     probability vectors certify an achieved optimization gap of at most W (θ ) − D( p, q).
23                                                                                                   23

24     The proof is in the Technical Supplement. It constructs the primal constraints                24

25   and dual support function explicitly. Rationalizing a solver’s proposed readout                 25

26   and probability vectors gives a checkable certificate; a convergence flag is not the            26

27   certificate. Label intervals, features, moduli, coefficient bounds and the cover remain         27

28   part of the numerical contract.                                                                 28

29     Applying the theorem backwards gives a fitted continuation at every date. Its                 29

30   achieved ranges Wt , together with the terminal oscillation and the actual imple- 30
31   mented greedy-gap bounds, can be substituted directly into Theorem 8. This con- 31
32   nects the attained fitting objective to both original-optimum regret and the range              32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                               21

1    of the original-law cost observation. Each readout minimizes its stated one-step              1

2    qualification bound conditional on the fixed future. It does not minimize the joint           2

3    multi-date range, the true policy loss, or a hidden-feature objective. The next back- 3
4    ward step recomputes its labels and moduli from the continuation actually returned. 4
5      The label construction in (13) uses exact conditional moments for a shallow ReLU            5

6    future and a verified continuous-action cover. Its work is charged in addition to the         6

7    linear program, coefficient rationalization, modulus calculation and dual check. A            7

8    conventional basis with the same number of coefficients receives the same labels              8

9    and the same objective; it uses its own valid feature moduli. This is a comparison            9

10   of qualified approximations, not an assertion that different bases span the same              10

11   functions or that a neural encoding has an intrinsic advantage.                               11

14                                                                                                     14
          P ROPOSITION 11—Affine-exact qualification: The moduli in (16)–(17) give valid
15                                                                                                     15
     residual enclosures and the residual-oscillation upper bound (18). The objective is convex and
16                                                                                                     16
     admits a finite linear-programming representation. For every common affine continuation
17                                                                                                     17
     h( x ) = a + ux1 + vx2 , whenever its two coefficient representations are feasible, both moduli
18                                                                                                     18
     equal max{|u|, |v|}. Thus, for common labels, node cover and target modulus, the two
19                                                                                                     19
     qualification objectives agree on this affine function.
20                                                                                                     20

21        P ROOF : A ReLU coordinate has the displayed cumulative slopes on its four affine            21

22   intervals. The dual norm of ℓ1 is ℓ∞ , so their largest coordinate magnitude gives                22

23   the state modulus. A Bernstein derivative is a convex combination of 4(θij − θi,j−1 )             23

24   with degree-three Bernstein weights. This proves its bound. Residuals change                      24

25   by at most ( G + L)ρ between a node and a covered state, proving the enclosure                    25

26   and its oscillation bound. Each term in (18) is a maximum of finitely many affine                 26

27   functions. For an affine function, the ReLU coefficients are (u, 0, 0, 0) and (v, 0, 0, 0);       27

28   the Bernstein coefficients are (u/4, u/2, 3u/4, u) and the analogous vector for v. All            28

29   relevant slopes or derivative controls are u or v.                                     Q.E.D.     29

30                                                                                                     30

31     This calibration does not identify the two non-affine function classes, equalize                31

32   every numerical conditioning effect, or remove all conservatism from Bernstein                    32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                   23

1    controls. It isolates a particular qualification difference. For example, the old co- 1
2    efficientwise modulus charges ux1 as |u| in its ReLU encoding but as 10|u| in its                   2

3    Bernstein encoding. The calibrated modulus charges both as |u|. The factorial exper- 3
4    iment below retains the old fits and evaluates the new fits separately; the calibration             4

5    is not an ex post correction of their recorded costs.                                               5

          P ROOF : If b weakly displaced aC , the readout would be feasible for its switch
4                                                                                                    4
     problem and hence F (θ ) ≥ DCb ≥ DC . This contradicts F (θ ) ≤ U + ϵ < DC . Couple
5                                                                                                    5
     the attained and alternative controllers with the same initial state and innovations.
6                                                                                                    6
     On a certified initial cell their first actions agree, their next states agree, and their
7                                                                                                    7
     fixed terminal-policy actions agree. Their cash costs are identical there. Elsewhere
8                                                                                                    8

10     The theorem does not replace failed switch certificates by successful ones: a non- 10
11   positive surplus is unresolved. Nor does a terminal-label certificate automatically             11

12   propagate through newly fitted label problems at a longer horizon. The numerical                12

14   the first-date action and the terminal actor is common.                                         14

29                                                                                                   29
     [l jk , u jk ] for J π j − J πk . Apply Theorem 12 separately to their fixed-label sublevels,

1    simultaneously for every pair of readouts in those sublevels. No union bound over                 1

2    an uncountable optimizer class is needed: the extension is deterministic on the                   2

3    original finite-policy event. When both covers are entirely stable the interval is                3

4    unchanged. Sharp controller-choice and deployment-allocation results in Section S                 4

5    can therefore be applied directly to these qualified optimizer classes after the stated           5

6    enlargement. This transfers attained-cost evidence without identifying numeri- 6
7    cal fitting success with either population training reliability or a new economic                 7

13     A qualification tolerance need not preserve every acquired action to preserve                   13

14   an economic conclusion. The bound in Theorem 12 charges the full cost range on                    14

15   each uncertified cell. We now retain the particular actions that remain possible and              15

16   the direction of their economic effects. This gives a cost guarantee for an entire                16

17   optimization sublevel, including approximately optimal readouts, without treating                 17

18   an inconclusive switch certificate as an observed policy failure.                                 18

30     The cellwise extrema relax the common-coefficient restriction and need not be                  30

31   attained by one readout. The theorem is consequently not a sharp joint characteriza- 31
32   tion of the optimizer class. Its useful feature is different: it can retain an identified        32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                              27

1    economic comparison when some actions remain uncertified and the binary Hqϵ                    1

2    allowance is too large. The original Bellman optimum and the constructive accuracy             2

3    results are unchanged. A reference expected-loss upper bound can also be enlarged              3

4    by vϵ ; the actionwise comparison itself is not a new bound obtained by replacing              4

5    the original optimum with a menu optimum.                                                      5

6      For the executed two-date economy, the conditional contrasts are computed from               6

7    the original stage costs, transitions and fixed terminal actor. Common innovations             7

8    preserve their actual marginal laws. Centered polynomial differences retain cancella- 8
9    tion, and rectangle queries enclose every crossed acquired cell. The final innovation          9

10   is integrated analytically. Appendix AT proves whole-bin validity, deterministic               10

11   refinement convergence under vanishing arithmetic error, and the work bound. 11
12   It also separates integration accuracy from the independent statistical interval to            12

13   which (26) is applied.                                                                         13

          APPENDIX I: E CONOMICALLY SUFFICIENT OPTIMIZATION ACCURACY
16                                                                                                  16

17     The qualification objective and the economic loss have different units. A numeri- 17
18   cal stopping rule should therefore be connected to the decision it supports, rather            18

19   than chosen by equating their tolerances. The actionwise transfer supplies this                19

20   connection for every tolerance in an interval, not only for a finite list of optimizer         20

21   outputs.                                                                                       21

22     For construction j, fix the labels, attained value Uj , action-switch bounds and             22

23   continuation policy of Theorem 13. Fix e j > 0 and enclose every action with D jCb ≤           23

24   Uj + e j . Write [v−         +
                        j ( ϵ ), v j ( ϵ )] for its class-cost band. The only possible changes of   24

25   this band on [0, e j ] occur at                                                                25

26                                                                                                  26

27                       K j = {0, e j } ∪ { D jCb − Uj : 0 < D jCb − Uj ≤ e j }.                   27

28                                                                                                  28

29   At a knot the weak switch inequality is already active. Thus the band is constant              29

30   on each interval between successive knots, with the left endpoint included and the             30

31   right endpoint excluded; the final endpoint is evaluated separately if it is itself a          31

32   switch knot. No interpolation across a knot is permitted.                                      32
     28

                                  Fj (θ ) ≤ Uj + ϵ,         Fk (ϑ ) ≤ Uk + η

     has cost difference in [ L jk (ϵ, η ), Ujk (ϵ, η )], simultaneously for all tolerances in the stated
19                                                                                                          19

20                                                                                                          20
     additional charge, and E jk (m) certifies a cost difference strictly within the margin for the
21                                                                                                          21
     entire pair of readout classes.
22                                                                                                          22
       These two sufficient regions are downward closed in their tolerance coordinates and are
23                                                                                                          23
     finite unions of knot rectangles with the stated endpoint conventions. Their maximal extent
24                                                                                                          24
     relative to the supplied actionwise bands can be obtained without a further optimizer call or
25                                                                                                          25
     cost observation. In particular, if a feasible numerical output has a verified primal–dual gap
26                                                                                                          26
     at most ϵ, it belongs to the corresponding class and may be returned whenever its gap pair
27                                                                                                          27
     is in the required region. Choosing the stopping tolerance after examining the simultaneous
28                                                                                                          28
     intervals does not require an additional probability allowance.
29                                                                                                          29

30     P ROOF : As a tolerance increases, its possible-action set expands. Hence v−
                                                                                  j is                      30
                         +
31   nonincreasing and v j is nondecreasing. Each set changes only at the displayed                         31

32   switch knots, where equality retains the newly possible actions. Theorem 13 gives                      32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                29

1    (27)–(28) for every readout and every tolerance by the same deterministic argument. 1
2    The lower endpoint decreases and the upper endpoint increases in either tolerance. 2
3    This proves downward closure and the finite-rectangle representation, including                 3

4    the strict economic tests at their boundaries.                                                  4

5      If d j ≤ inf Fj is a verified dual value and Fj (θ ) − d j ≤ ϵ, then Fj (θ ) ≤ inf Fj + ϵ ≤   5

6    Uj + ϵ. The same argument applies to k. All statements therefore hold for any                   6

7    stopping output satisfying these verified inequalities on the original event. Nothing           7

8    in this implication requires a union bound over the outputs or tolerances.           Q.E.D.     8

9                                                                                                    9

10     A boundary of a sufficient region is not an identified failure boundary of the                10

11   true optimizer class. The cellwise relaxation can combine actions that no common                11

12   coefficient vector selects. Nor does a strict region necessarily include its supremum           12

13   tolerance: at an exact switch knot, the added action can destroy the desired strict             13

14   economic inequality. Appendix AU gives an exact finite algorithm, checks both                   14

15   sides of every knot, and separates this stopping guarantee from a distributional                15

16   claim about neural fitting success.                                                             16

18    APPENDIX J: C OMMON READOUTS AND ECONOMIC POLICY QUALIFICATION                                 18

19                                                                                                   19
       An approximately optimized continuation is one function. Its coefficients deter-
20                                                                                                   20
     mine the action in every observation cell. Separate action-switch tests deliberately
21                                                                                                   21

22                                                                                                   22

23                                                                                                   23

24                                                                                                   24
     ring the original NBO qualification account to an economic decision. The attained
25                                                                                                   25

26                                                                                                   26
     unchanged.

25     Removing all joint-event restrictions gives independent cell simplices. Their sup- 25
26   port is the sum of the cellwise maximal weights. Adding valid restrictions can only               26

27   reduce that support. Full-menu exclusions retain a subset of the alternatives ad- 27
28   mitted by a reference-only comparison, proving the second ordering. Intersections                 28

29   preserve containment because each input bound contains the same cost difference. 29
30     Subtract the two instances of (33) and add the attained-policy interval to obtain               30

31   (34). On the attained-cost event this entire argument is deterministic and holds                  31

32   for every valid collection of inequalities. Taking any number of allowances or                    32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                            33

1    any coefficient class therefore requires no additional probabilistic event. Finally,        1

2    F (θ ) ≤ d + ϵ ≤ infΘ F + ϵ ≤ U + ϵ.                                               Q.E.D.   2

3                                                                                                3

4
       A positive lower endpoint in (34) supports replacing A by B for every additional          4

5
     normalized charge below that endpoint. A strict interval contained in (−m, m) gives         5

6
     equivalence at the declared margin m. These statements concern actual policy costs          6

7
     throughout the stated optimization classes. They do not identify a probability that         7

8
     an unrestricted fitting algorithm will enter those classes.                                 8

9                                                                                                9

10
                      J.3. Qualification with one common coefficient vector                      10

11     The fixed-label calibrated objective is                                                   11

12                                                                                               12
               F (θ ) = max(yi+ − ϕiT θ ) − min(yi− − ϕiT θ ) + 2ρ G + max |rrT θ | .
                                                                                   
13                       i                   i                            r                      13

14                                                                                               14
     A winner or joint-winner event adds linear inequalities dTj θ + c j ≤ 0. The Numer-
15                                                                                               15
     ical Appendix derives an exact lower certificate from two probability vectors, a
16                                                                                               16
     derivative-budget multiplier and nonnegative event multipliers. All inequalities
17                                                                                               17
     in a joint event use the same θ and the same qualification objective. A positive
18                                                                                               18
     coefficient-box support separation certifies an empty event; a solver’s infeasibility
19                                                                                               19
     flag alone does not.
20                                                                                               20
       The complete-menu calculation and joint-event calculations can be stopped at any
21                                                                                               21
     finite budget. Unchecked events add no exclusion, and inconclusive calculations
22                                                                                               22
     retain the existing bound. Conversely, a valid new exclusion can immediately
23                                                                                               23
     improve the economic assignment problem without any additional policy-cost
24                                                                                               24
     observations. The arithmetic and integration costs required to obtain that exclusion
25                                                                                               25
     remain separately chargeable.

      APPENDIX K: S HARED POLICY PATTERNS AND SHARP OCCUPATION BOUNDS
28                                                                                               28

29     A common continuation restricts an entire contingent plan, including its pre- 29
30   scriptions at states that are not reached. Pairwise exclusions need not retain that         30

31   restriction after the occupation variables are relaxed. The following construction          31

32   preserves the complete pattern before relaxing the state distribution. It strengthens       32
     34

1    Theorem 6, rather than replacing the original Bellman problem with an unrelated                            1

2    finite-policy benchmark.                                                                                   2

6    with the stipulated tie convention. This is a finite set even when the parameter set                       6

7    is infinite. For validity, it is sufficient to retain a finite superset Σϵ containing every                7

8    actual pattern and satisfying all previously certified conflicts. A numerical search                       8

9    that finds some patterns does not establish such coverage. Conversely, asserting that                      9

10   a retained pattern is attained requires a qualified parameter witness; a fractional                        10

11   solution is not that witness.                                                                              11

     In particular, exact finite-state transitions and advantages give the exact class-cost extrema,
16                                                                                                     16
     attained by qualified patterns. More generally, any refinement sequence for which the dis-
17                                                                                                     17
     played E tends to zero yields convergence of both bounds to the corresponding true extrema.
18                                                                                                     18
     The stated transition-width condition, not refinement alone, licenses this conclusion.
19                                                                                                     19

20     The full proof appears in Supplement 12. It does not assume that the cell process of            20

21   the continuous economy is Markov. Conditional cell-to-cell probabilities exist under              21

22   each fixed policy at each date; the uniform enclosures contain those probabilities                22

23   regardless of the within-cell distribution. Disaggregation prevents an optimization               23

24   relaxation from buying one parameter pattern initially and a different pattern after              24

25   observing its induced state.                                                                      25

26                                                                                                     26

27     Why complete patterns add information. The two-date example in Supplement 8                     27

28   has two qualified signs of one shared coefficient. One sign visits the right-hand                 28

29   state and then selects its free action. The other sign selects the costly action there but        29

30   never visits that state. Every actual policy therefore has cost zero. The pair-conflict           30

31   flow relaxation permits mass 1/2 on the right-hand state and its costly action; its               31

32   upper bound is 1/2. Disaggregation by the two attainable patterns gives upper                     32
     36

1    bound zero. This strict improvement requires no new policy-cost sample, changed          1

2    economic primitive or successful nonconvex fit.                                          2

3                                                                                             3

4      Computation and scope. The number of complete patterns can be exponential in           4

5    the number of dated cells. Enumeration is therefore a finite exact implementation,       5

6    not a general polynomial-time claim. One may begin with an outer covering of             6

7    parameter space, retain every unresolved pattern and refine only economically            7

8    material branches. Every discarded branch needs a valid exclusion for the actual         8

9    own-future qualification functional. Missing a branch invalidates the bound; re- 9
10   taining an unresolved branch only weakens it. The state-flow width in (37) and the       10

11   global-pattern relaxation are distinct approximation costs. This distinction preserves   11

12   both the original continuous economy and the original-optimum policy sandwich            12

13   while making the added qualification work explicit.                                      13

14                                                                                            14

              APPENDIX L: O RIGINAL - LAW QUALIFICATION UNDER TRANSPORT

                                       APPROXIMATION
17                                                                                            17

18     The occupation bounds retain a complete policy and its induced state distribution. 18
19   Their cellwise convergence result, however, requires shrinking transition-probability    19

20   widths. Moving a deterministic successor by an arbitrarily small distance need           20

21   not make those widths small. We develop a complementary certificate that tests           21

22   the transition approximation against the actual stored continuation. It applies to       22

23   singular as well as diffuse transition laws and does not require a continuous greedy     23

24   actor.                                                                                   24

25     Work in the original finite-horizon expected-cost model, with compact metric           25

26   state spaces, nonempty feasible action correspondences, bounded costs and terminal       26

27   cost g. All kernels below are probability kernels with finite first moments. Minima      27

28   may be replaced by infima provided a measurable feasible approximate minimizer           28

29   is supplied. Write B0 = 1 and Bt = ∏s<t β s , where β s ≥ 0. A parameter θ determines    29

30   all stored continuations f t,θ and a complete feasible policy πθ . Parameters, actions   30

31   and continuations can be shared across dates and economic contexts; the state            31

32   includes every endogenous variable needed by the original model.                         32
                                    NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                                  37

30   Thus any common-parameter qualification class for which Rθ ≤ ε is a class of complete                                   30

31   policies with original-law loss at most ε. The result permits changes at every date and all                             31

32   corresponding changes in occupation. It requires neither a fixed continuation after the first                           32

1    date, independence of deployment contexts, nor Lipschitz continuity of the implemented              1

2    policy.                                                                                             2

3      The certificate is invariant to replacing each stored continuation by f t,θ + k t,θ , where the   3

4    shifts are state independent, and shifting its approximate action values consistently. If the       4

5    numerical transition is obtained by a measurable successor quantizer qt with d(y, qt (y)) ≤         5

6    ht almost surely, then et = ht is valid even when the two kernels are mutually singular. Its        6

     In particular, the transport part vanishes along any refinement for which this explicit
11                                                                                                       11
     sum tends to zero. No total-variation convergence is required. Convergence of the full
12                                                                                                       12
     certificate additionally requires the displayed residual, action-search, terminal and arithmetic
13                                                                                                       13
     contributions to vanish.
14                                                                                                       14

15                                                                                                       15
       The full proof is in Supplement 14. It compares the candidate and the original
16                                                                                                       16
     Bellman optimum separately to the same stored continuation, before subtracting
17                                                                                                       17
     their bounds. Applying a baseline occupation law to a different complete policy is
18                                                                                                       18
     neither necessary nor valid. The use of a uniform continuation modulus is essen-
19                                                                                                       19
     tial: a small transport distance alone does not control integration of an arbitrary
20                                                                                                       20
     discontinuous value function.
21                                                                                                       21

22     What the representation contributes. For the constructed affine–ReLU continua- 22
23   tions, a valid Lipschitz modulus follows either from the cone construction or from                  23

24   verified layer norms. The resulting Kt+1 prices transition approximation in the                     24

25   policy certificate. The exact same function represented by a native minimum circuit                 25

26   has the same mathematical guarantee. The result identifies the role of the neural                   26

27   continuation without making a method-neutral verifier evidence of universal neural                  27

28   speed. Wasserstein robustness and finite-model approximation have established                       28

29   analyses (Zhou et al., 2024, Saldi et al., 2015); our use is the uniform, translation- 29
30   invariant qualification account for shared NBO continuations and their complete                     30

31   original-law policies.                                                                              31

17   When fixed, verified continuation moduli and primitive resource estimates yield a                17

18   positive-monomial upper model in (η, e), Theorem 4 applies to its positive-weight                18

19   coordinates. Coordinates with zero budget weight require separate caps and treat- 19
20   ment; they do not satisfy that theorem’s positive-weight premise. A modulus de- 20
21   pending on an optimized parameter is not held fixed without a class-uniform upper                21

22   certificate. This preserves the cost of transition precision rather than hiding it in the        22

23   number of acquired states.                                                                       23

24                                                                                                    24

25
                              L.2. Nonlinear and strategic evaluations                                25

26     For a cash-invariant monotone one-step risk evaluation, the same signed recur- 26
27   sion applies when a separately established bound on the risk-evaluation discrepancy              27

28   replaces bt . Wasserstein distance and cash invariance alone do not imply such a                 28

29   bound. General monotone Lipschitz aggregators use the absolute-error recursion                   29

30   of Proposition 7, with their own continuation moduli, rather than an unsupported                 30

31   linear expectation identity. For games, the transport account enters each fixed- 31
32   opponent best-response certificate separately. Preference states and temporal selves             32
     40

1    retain the augmented-state and unilateral-deviation interpretation already stated in                 1

2    Section 5.1. The new result supplements, rather than removes, the original diffusion,                2

3    recursive-preference and game constructions.                                                         3

4                                                                                                         4
       A singular-transition diagnostic. Take one decision date, β = 1, terminal cost
5                                                                                                         5
     g( x ) = x on [0, 1], and actions 0 and 1. Action 0 has zero immediate cost and true
6                                                                                                         6
     successor h > 0; action 1 costs h/2 and has successor zero. The numerical kernel
7                                                                                                         7
     places both successors at zero. With f 0 = 0 and f 1 = g, its greedy choice is action 0, its
8                                                                                                         8
     residual span is zero and its true regret is h/2. Omitting the transition charge would
9                                                                                                         9
     falsely certify zero regret. The transport certificate is 2h and converges to zero, while
10                                                                                                        10
     the total-variation distance for action 0 is one for every h > 0. Appendix BA gives
11                                                                                                        11
     exact rational checks. This diagnostic is not a new observation in the investment
12                                                                                                        12
     experiment.
13                                                                                                        13

     2021). Here the observations are verified, continuous-law NBO cost contrasts, not
22                                                                                                        22
