8    Each endpoint may also be intersected with the corresponding average predictable support              8

9    endpoint. The assertion holds at every almost surely finite stopping time. If the complete            9

10   controller pair and initial-law/reset contract stay fixed, then ∆i = ∆, even when the qualified       10

11   continuation changes predictably between episodes. Otherwise the target is the average                11

12   conditional cost difference, not the last controller’s cost.                                          12

13                                                                                                         13

14     The proof applies conditional Hoeffding bounds to each endpoint and Ville’s                         14

15   inequality to a finite collection of nonnegative supermartingales. It requires no                     15

16   independent or identically distributed deployment contexts. It does require the                       16

17   stated conditional transition laws. Within-episode cache adaptation is allowed only                   17

18   when the controllers are treated as history-dependent algorithms and the greedy- 18
19   gap qualification covers every memory state that can occur. Repeating the same                        19

20   program identifier while carrying a different cache state does not establish a fixed                  20

21   conditional target.                                                                                   21

22     The implementation uses a finite dyadic collection of λ j and exact rational upper                  22

23   bounds for the logarithms. These give conservative, computable versions of (43). 23
24   It reserves the support and target identity before accepting an episode. A failed                     24

25   numerical evaluation consumes the full previously qualified support interval and                      25

26   its actual work; it is not deleted after inspecting its outcome. A failure of the support             26

27   certificate itself stops qualification.                                                               27

28                                                                                                         28

29     C OROLLARY 20—Costed replacement after optional stopping: For a fixed target                        29

30   pair, let [l N , u N ] be the interval at a finite stopping time N. Let a declared deployment count   30

31   be m ≥ 1, a one-time replacement and qualification charge be F ≥ 0, and the incremental               31

32   per-deployment charge be c, all in the same units as the policy cost. On the simultaneous             32
     42

1    event of Theorem 19, the net incremental cost lies in                                           1

2                                                                                                    2

3                                 [l N + F/m + c, u N + F/m + c].                                    3

4                                                                                                    4
     Consequently u N + F/m + c < 0 certifies strict net improvement. Inclusion of [l N , u N ] in
5                                                                                                    5
     a declared equivalence band certifies equivalence at that band, not strict superiority. New
6                                                                                                    6
     adaptively selected controller pairs may be qualified in separate epochs with conditional
7                                                                                                    7
     error allocations whose sum is at most the overall error budget.
8                                                                                                    8

9                                                                                                    9
       A wall-clock reading is not a monetary charge. Measured setup, validation,
10                                                                                                   10
     rejected evaluations and successful retrievals are retained separately; prices are
11                                                                                                   11
     explicit decision inputs. The number of qualification episodes is not automatically
12                                                                                                   12
     the number of deployments over which a setup cost is amortized. These distinctions
13                                                                                                   13
     let the original investment decision use a valid stopping rule without silently
14                                                                                                   14
     changing either its estimand or its units.
15                                                                                                   15

       APPENDIX N: O RIGINAL - LAW QUALIFICATION OF CONSTRUCTED POLICIES
17                                                                                                   17

18     A continuation can have a verified fitting objective without its implemented                  18

19   policy having an identified economic advantage. The distinction is resolved by                  19

20   evaluating the same complete policy that the construction returns. We now connect               20

21   the qualification-optimal readouts of Section F to independent original-law cost                21

22   observations. Neither the fitted future nor the acquired actor is replaced during               22

23   evaluation. The comparison concerns the constructed NBO policies, rather than a                 23

24   different recursive controller supplied with the same neural label.                             24

                                            103           37    21097
19                       [0, H ],      H=       (1 + β ) + β2 =       .                    (48)   19
                                            64            16    4096
20                                                                                                20

21   A paired difference has the signed support [− H, H ]. Negative common-path gains             21

22   are not clipped. No original-optimum value query is required to compute either               22

23   observation for a frozen acquired actor.                                                     23

24     An initial or innovation bin is a continuous cell, not a point mass. The evalua- 24
25   tor encloses every true state in the bin, every acquired cell reached by that state          25

26   enclosure, and every resulting feasible action. When an enclosure crosses an acqui- 26
27   sition boundary, all intersected actions enter the continuation enclosure. A second          27

28   implementation evaluates the same primitives using integer-directed arithmetic               28

29   and expanded polynomials. The reported endpoints are the hull of the two valid               29

30   enclosures, intersected with the proved support. Arithmetic agreement is a check             30

31   on the implementations; validity follows from the enclosure rules and the primitive          31

32   identities, not from agreement alone.                                                        32
     44

1                             N.2. Precision and the decision criterion                               1

2                                                                                                     2
       The following statement applies to any fixed finite collection of qualified policies,
3                                                                                                     3
     not only to the two feature bases used below. All construction information is fixed
4                                                                                                     4
     before the independent evaluation rows are drawn. Dependence among policies on
5                                                                                                     5
     a common row is allowed.
6                                                                                                     6

7         T HEOREM 21—Original-law cost qualification: For each of K declared estimands,              7

8    let Zi be its original-law observation on independent row i, and suppose a deterministic         8

9    full-bin calculation returns                                                                     9

10                                                                                                    10
                              a ≤ Li ≤ Zi ≤ Ui ≤ b,           i = 1, . . . , N.
11                                                                                                    11

12   The inclusion may instead hold for the original conditional expectation within the sampled       12

13   bin. Put R = b − a, let L̄, Ū denote sample means, and let s2L , sU
                                                                        2 be the unbiased endpoint    13

14   sample variances. For N ≥ 2 and ℓ > 0, define                                                    14

15                       s                              s                                             15
                             2                              2
16                         2s L ℓ      7Rℓ                2sU ℓ      7Rℓ                              16
                   rL =           +           ,    rU =         +           .
17
                            N       3( N − 1)              N      3( N − 1)                           17

18   With probability at least 1 − 4Ke−ℓ , simultaneously for the declared collection,                18

19                                                                                                    19

20                                     EZi ∈ [ L̄ − r L , Ū + rU ].                          (49)    20

21                                                                                                    21
     Before rounding the final endpoints, its width is exactly
22                                                                                                    22
                                        s           s
23                                            2          2                                            23
                                           2s L ℓ     2sU  ℓ     14Rℓ
                            (Ū − L̄) +           +          +           .                    (50)
24                                           N          N      3( N − 1)                              24

25                                                                                                    25
     Upward square roots and outward final rounding preserve coverage. The first term includes
26                                                                                                    26
     all retained numerical and acquisition uncertainty; the middle terms reflect observed end-
27                                                                                                    27
     point dispersion; the last term is the finite-support penalty. A declared full-width target is
28                                                                                                    28
     met only when their computed sum meets it.
29                                                                                                    29

30     The proof in the Technical Supplement applies the empirical-Bernstein inequality               30

31   of Maurer and Pontil (2009) to the lower and upper endpoint variables. The statis- 31
32   tical inequality is established work. The contribution here is its application to the            32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                    45

1    original-law costs of the actual constructed actors, with executable whole-bin obser- 1
2    vations, independent arithmetic and an explicit precision account. Refining only a                   2

3    simulation bin cannot remove the finite-support or sampling terms. Conversely, an                    3

4    inaccurate uniform regret certificate need not prevent an accurate comparison of                     4

5    two actual policy costs.                                                                             5

6      Precision, equivalence and improvement are different conclusions. For a cost- 6
7    difference interval [l, u] and a predeclared margin δ, equivalence requires −δ < l ≤                 7

8    u < δ. Noninferiority of the first policy requires u < δ; strict superiority requires                8

9    u < 0. An interval can meet its width target and fail equivalence because it identifies              9

10   a material difference. These decisions are made from exact endpoints, not from                       10

11   rounded table entries.                                                                               11

12                                                                                                        12

13
       C OROLLARY 22—Priced selection and adoption: On the simultaneous event of                          13

14
     Theorem 21, let [ L j , Uj ] qualify the cost of policy j, and let w j be its declared nonnegative   14

15
     resource charge. A minimizer bj of Uj + w j satisfies, for every policy j,                           15

16                                                                                                        16
                                     Jbj + wbj ≤ Jj + w j + (Uj − L j ).
17                                                                                                        17

18   The inequality holds for every assignment of resource prices on the same event. If the gain          18

19   from replacing an installed controller is in [l, u], then m deployments, monetary scale v ≥ 0,       19

20   qualification charge q ≥ 0 and replacement fee f ≥ 0 give expected net benefit in                    20

21                                                                                                        21

22                                   [mvl − q − f , mvu − q − f ].                                        22

23                                                                                                        23
     In particular, when l > 0 and v > 0, every m > (q + f )/(vl ) is certified beneficial.
24                                                                                                        24

25                                                                                                        25
       P ROOF : The first assertion follows from Jbj ≤ Ubj , the minimizing property, and
26                                                                                                        26
     Uj ≤ Jj + Uj − L j . These deterministic inequalities hold for all nonnegative prices
27                                                                                                        27
     once all cost intervals cover. The second assertion is multiplication by mv and
28                                                                                                        28
     subtraction of q + f .                                                                    Q.E.D.
29                                                                                                        29

30     The resource charges in this result must be specified, not inferred from normalized                30

31   losses. An already paid construction and a prospective purchase have different                       31

32   charges. When only a shared qualification service has been timed, charging that                      32
     46

1    entire service to a proposed replacement is conservative; inventing a per-policy       1

2    marginal qualification time is not.                                                    2

3                                                                                           3

4                                                                                           4
                          N.3. The constructed-controller experiment
5                                                                                           5

6      The fixed catalogue consists of the four previously constructed NBO actors and       6

7    the admissible zero-investment incumbent. ReLU and degree-four Bernstein con- 7
8    tinuations each have nine coefficients and are constructed on eight- or sixteen-cell   8

9    coordinate covers. At a fixed cover, the two bases use the same acquisition con- 9
10   tract and primitive information. The actor arrays, actual fitted futures and exact     10

11   optimization certificates are unchanged. The zero-investment policy supplies an        11

12   economically interpretable installed reference; it is not substituted for the strong   12

13   conventional query competitors in the preceding study.                                 13

14     Before execution, the protocol fixes N = 524,288, forty-bit continuous bins, the     14

15   sample stream, the full-width target 1/256, and the separate two-sided equivalence     15

16   margin 1/256. All five marginal costs and ten paired differences are reported          16

17   for both observations in (45)–(46). Thus K = 30 and ℓ = 10 give a joint failure        17

18   bound below 1/100. Reusing a row for another controller or estimator does not          18

19   increase the independent sample count. The declared independence model concerns        19

20   the simulated rows; a fixed pseudorandom seed establishes reproducibility, not         20

21   mathematical independence.                                                             21

22                                                                                          22

23     Outcomes of the fixed precision design. Across both estimators, 20 of the twenty     23

24   paired intervals meet the declared full-width target. Their widths range from          24

25   0.000936 to 0.002285. Exactly 4 meet the separate equivalence criterion. Neither a     25

26   failed width target nor a failed equivalence comparison is omitted.                    26

27     The conditionally integrated comparisons identify a positive gain over the zero- 27
28   investment incumbent for 4 of the four constructed policies. These are gains of        28

29   complete implemented controllers, not reductions in a qualification objective.         29

30     At the two matched acquisition resolutions, the ReLU-minus-Bernstein compar- 30
31   isons identify 2 lower-cost ReLU outcomes, 0 higher-cost outcomes, and 0 unre- 31
32   solved signs. This finite comparison is not a universal representation ranking.        32
                                NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                         47

24     The construction and the evaluation now refer to the same complete controllers. 24

26   determine the adoption regions in Corollary 22. Comparisons between the feature                             26

27   bases identify only what their paired intervals support. The earlier unresolved and                         27

29   the Numerical and Development Appendix.                                                                     29

31   action rectangle. Additional rational midpoint checks test predetermined rows. The                          31

32   phase ledger separates controller loading, lookup preparation, binary64 evaluation,                         32

1    integer evaluation, exact statistical arithmetic, output and the complete parent        1

3    without replacing its original clocks. This evaluates the two-date constructed-policy   3

7    APPENDIX O: Q UALIFICATION , REPRESENTATION , AND ATTAINED ECONOMIC                     7

8                                           COST                                             8

9                                                                                            9
       The preceding attained-controller experiment used a coefficientwise qualifica-
10                                                                                           10
     tion bound. Its ReLU–Bernstein ordering is an observation about those specified
11                                                                                           11
     constructions. We now examine whether that ordering persists after the two bases
12                                                                                           12
     receive the same qualification charge on their common affine functions. This directly
13                                                                                           13
     tests a source of representation-dependent conservatism while preserving the origi-
14                                                                                           14
     nal nonlinear investment economy and the Neural Bellman Operator construction.
15                                                                                           15

16                                                                                           16
                            O.1. A prospective factorial comparison
17                                                                                           17

18     The design fixes two bases, two qualification rules and two acquisition resolutions. 18
19   The eight constructed controllers and the installed zero controller are evaluated on    19

20   the same original-law paths. Each basis has nine coefficients with absolute bounds      20

21   eight, the same node covers, thirty-three-action search menus and downward action       21

22   quantum 1/4096. The calibrated rule replaces the coefficientwise modulus by (16)        22

23   or (17), both in residual qualification and in the regularity allowance passed to the   23

24   previous fitted date. Both own-future continuations are constructed backwards. An       24

25   exact primal–dual gap of at most 1/65536 qualifies each new fit. The coefficient        25

26   proposal is quantized at forty bits; no hidden-feature optimizer or outcome-selected    26

27   basis is introduced.                                                                    27

28     A source manifest was committed before construction, and the complete ac- 28
29   tor identities were committed before generating 524,288 new four-coordinate             29

30   continuous-bin rows. The bin precision is forty bits. Two estimators, cash cost         30

31   and exact last-innovation conditional expectation, evaluate the same expected costs     31

32   under the original uniform initial law and continuous innovations. For each esti- 32
                                NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                        49

1    mator the frozen family contains nine marginal costs, thirty-six paired differences                       1

2    and two basis-by-qualification interactions. All ninety-four estimands share a single                     2

3    failure allowance bounded by 376 exp(−11) < 1/100. Shared paths and the two                               3

4    estimators do not multiply the number of independent rows.                                                4

5      The whole-bin enclosures are independently computed in outward binary64                                 5

6    arithmetic and directed-integer arithmetic. Their hull, intersected with the proved                       6

7    support, is used for inference. An exact rational implementation additionally checks                      7

8    512 midpoint trajectories per controller. All nine controllers, bins, endpoints, exact                    8

9    sample moments and disagreements are part of the retained protocol. This revision                         9

10   re-evaluates every bin and every inference quantity; it neither regenerates those                         10

11   observations as a second sample nor substitutes replay time for the original service                      11

12   clock.                                                                                                    12

1                          O.2. Precision of the economic comparison                          1

2                                                                                             2
       The target was a paired total width of 1/256, fixed before seeing the new rows.
3                                                                                             3
     Appendix AS reports every interval and its width components, including every
4                                                                                             4
     marginal interval whether or not its width falls below that paired target. Vari-
5                                                                                             5
     ance reduction by conditional integration affects the variance component, not the
6                                                                                             6
     bounded-support penalty or the acquisition contract. For example, in the fine cali-
7                                                                                             7
     brated basis comparison the conditional interval’s width consists of approximately
8                                                                                             8
     0.001008605 from the finite-support term, 0.000048152 from endpoint sampling-
9                                                                                             9
     variance allowances, and 2.69 × 10−11 from the numerical enclosure. The support
10                                                                                            10
     term is therefore the principal remaining contribution in that comparison.
11                                                                                            11
       The numerical component includes the implemented handling of continuous-bin
12                                                                                            12
     width, acquisition ambiguity and arithmetic propagation. It is not represented as
13                                                                                            13
     a pure floating-point error estimate. Likewise, a small numerical component does
14                                                                                            14
     not justify dropping the finite-sample support term. These diagnostics explain the
15                                                                                            15
     achieved precision without inferring significance from raw mean differences or treat-
16                                                                                            16
     ing the inherited R67 policy-cost rows as observations of these newly constructed
17                                                                                            17
     actors.
18                                                                                            18

19                                                                                            19

20                                                                                            20
                     O.3. From a fitted readout to a qualified optimizer class
21                                                                                            21

22     For each new terminal-label qualification problem the action-switch catalogue          22

23   covers every first-date observation cell and all thirty-two alternative robust actions. 23
24   The 20,480 rational dual bounds are independently recomputed using the antideriva- 24
25   tive and box-infimum calculations of Appendix AR; no switch optimization is rerun        25

26   to replace an unfavorable certificate.                                                   26

27     At zero extra objective allowance, the two fine-resolution constructions and coarse    27

28   Bernstein have complete first-date action stability. Every exact minimizer of each of    28

29   these fixed-label qualification problems therefore gives its attained complete policy,   29

30   not merely a similar fitted value. The original-law intervals in Table O.V extend        30

31   to these optimizer classes without additional probability expenditure. The same          31

32   conclusion holds for the positive dyadic sublevels displayed in Table O.IV.              32

1                           O.4. Controller choice and complete costs                           1

2                                                                                               2
       Both cash and conditional marginal and pair intervals can be used together on
3                                                                                               3
     their original simultaneous event. Exact shortest-path closure gives consistent cost
4                                                                                               4
     potentials and the pure-choice regret bounds of the inherited decision theorem. The
5                                                                                               5
     four-term interactions remain separately reported; sharpness of the pair-constraint
6                                                                                               6
     calculation is not asserted after adding those extra restrictions. The complete nine-
7                                                                                               7
     controller closure and attaining potentials are deposited in the revision audit.
8                                                                                               8
       For the fine calibrated controllers, the positive ReLU-minus-Bernstein interval
9                                                                                               9
     determines a nonempty region of additional Bernstein charges for which its lower
10                                                                                              10
     policy cost outweighs its extra charge. It also determines the opposite region in
11                                                                                              11
     which ReLU is preferred after that charge. All such statements are conditional on
12                                                                                              12
     an explicitly supplied conversion of computation, installation and deployment
13                                                                                              13
     into normalized cost units. The exact boundary values may be tightened by the
14                                                                                              14
     common pair-constraint closure; no new simulation is needed for that deterministic
15                                                                                              15
     calculation.
16                                                                                              16
       The construction work is recorded rather than presumed equal. Legacy clocks
17                                                                                              17
     include a label-loss ablation that the calibrated services do not execute. Their differ-
18                                                                                              18
     ence therefore does not identify an end-to-end speed gain from calibration. Exact
19                                                                                              19
     action and expected-feature evaluation counts are reported alongside the clocks,
20                                                                                              20
     and the original cost-inference, barrier and publication services retain separate mea-
21                                                                                              21
     sured costs. The present result supplies an identified policy-cost and conditional
22                                                                                              22
     purchase comparison in the original economy. It does not extrapolate a two-date
23                                                                                              23
     fitted-continuation calculation into a measured long-horizon speed result for the
24                                                                                              24
     recursively queried vector tree.

     APPENDIX P: E CONOMIC STOPPING REGIONS IN THE INVESTMENT ECONOMY
27                                                                                              27

28     The four calibrated constructions in Section O permit an exact application of            28

29   the stopping theorem on 0 ≤ ϵ ≤ 2−12 . The full 20,480-switch catalogue leaves             29

30   976 actions for economic integration. All four declared dyadic levels are retained. 30
31   Two independent arithmetic implementations reevaluate every paired box and                 31

32   reproduce the frozen records exactly. The tolerance calculation has 118 model strata;      32

1    a separate enumeration checks all 12,732 pair-stratum tests. Appendix AU records                          1

2    this computation and the earlier interrupted execution separately.                                        2

4    [0.000496, 0.002503], with endpoints rounded outward. The corresponding binary- 4
5    mass band is [−0.421558, 0.424526]. Thus the same attained-policy evidence iden- 5
6    tifies a Bernstein saving throughout the approximately optimized classes once                             6

7    unresolved actions are priced individually. Every additional Bernstein deployment                         7

8    charge below 0.000496 preserves the positive uniform net gain in normalized model                         8

9    units. This is not a calibrated installation fee.                                                         9

26     The exact equal-tolerance boundary for that ordering is approximately                                   26

27   0.0000674917368 and is excluded: a switch enters at equality. Its exact rational                          27

28   value, not the decimal approximation, governs a stopping rule. Cost equivalence                           28

29   within 1/256 is a different question. Coarse Bernstein and fine ReLU satisfy this                         29

30   equivalence throughout [0, 2−12 ], with final class-cost band [−0.001622, 0.003879]. 30
31   Both statements survive nonzero optimization error; neither is a universal                                31

32   representation ordering.                                                                                  32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                            55

1      All four classes retain a positive cost saving against the zero controller through- 1
2    out the integrated tolerance domain. Coarse ReLU nevertheless has inconclusive            2

3    pairwise class comparisons already at zero excess. It and all opposite-ordering tests     3

4    are not removed. The new frontier reuses the existing simultaneous cost event             4

5    and supplies deterministic coverage for a continuum of verified stopping outputs. 5
6    It creates neither new observations nor an estimate of fitting-success frequencies. 6
7    Complete phase work, all discrete refinement bands, all eighteen frontier targets         7

8    and both fixed-reference comparisons are retained in the appendix.                        8

       APPENDIX Q: E CONOMIC VALUE OF COMMON - READOUT QUALIFICATION
11                                                                                             11

12     The new calculation refines the economic accuracy of the original attained NBO          12

13   controllers. It does not fit another controller or change a conditional cost observation. 13
14   The four calibrated readouts, their frozen labels, terminal actors, robust 33-action      14

15   menus, acquisition grids and original continuous laws are those of the preceding          15

16   factorial investment study. The original actionwise integrations and simultaneous         16

17   attained-policy intervals remain the paid inputs.                                         17

18                                                                                             18

19                                                                                             19
                           Q.1. A complete, fixed qualification design
20                                                                                             20

21     At acquisition sizes eight and sixteen, every alternative admitted by the reference- 21
22   only barrier at excess objective at most 2−12 receives a full-menu qualification          22

23   problem. This gives 976 problems: 832 for coarse ReLU, nine for coarse Bernstein,         23

24   78 for fine ReLU and 57 for fine Bernstein. Each event includes the other 32 feasible     24

25   menu actions, with a common coefficient vector and the same objective.                    25

26     The joint-event design permits at most 512 pairs per model. When at most 128            26

27   alternatives remain at the largest allowance, all remaining alternatives seed the pair    27

28   catalogue. Otherwise the minimum-lower and maximum-upper conditional-cost                 28

29   alternatives in each cell seed it. Cross-cell pairs are ranked by the product of their    29

30   maximum absolute cost endpoints, with lexicographic tie handling, and the first           30

31   512 are retained. The complete roster contains 1572 pairs; the small Bernstein model      31

32   contributes all 36 of its possible pairs. This rule is deterministic postprocessing of    32
     56

1    an already covered cost event, not a newly randomized validation design. A small                            1

2    implementation pilot preceded the source lock and is disclosed separately.                                  2

3      All six declared allowances are evaluated: zero and 2−k for k = 16, 15, 14, 13, 12.                       3

4    The output preserves singleton bounds, pair bounds, positive box separations,                               4

5    retained alternatives, active conflicts and both economic duals. Every one of the 104                       5

6    infeasible optimization statuses has an independent positive rational separation                            6

7    certificate. A numerical status alone is never used to remove an event.                                     7

                  Q.3. Complete incremental work and independent arithmetic
11                                                                                             11

12     The recorded scientific interpreter, including imports, reads, all qualification and    12

13   assignment problems, durable outputs and shutdown, takes 104.699 seconds on               13

14   the stated ARM runner. Singleton, joint-event and assignment phases are recorded          14

15   separately. These measurements price the additional qualification calculation. They       15

16   are neither a cold-start cost for rebuilding the original controllers nor a matched       16

17   ReLU-versus-Bernstein timing experiment. Original fitting and integration clocks          17

18   remain unchanged and must be added when those inputs have not already been                18

19   purchased.                                                                                19

20     A separate rational implementation reconstructs the feature antiderivatives, all        20

21   winner comparisons, qualification supports, pair-event separations, conflict rosters      21

22   and 16548 assignment inequalities. It checks all 2,548 qualification certificates and     22

23   all 36 policy-class comparisons without invoking the production optimizer. Original       23

24   R77/R80 continuous-bin and actionwise kernel verification is separately replayed in       24

25   the full publication build. The distinction between new exact certificate verification,   25

26   inherited full-bin re-integration and independent cost sampling is explicit: this         26

27   study adds no new fitting attempt, integration bin or independent cost row.               27

28     The economic improvement is consequently a more informative decision from the           28

29   original NBO continuation and its previously qualified evidence. Full-menu winner         29

30   regions remove alternatives that cannot be selected; common-coefficient restrictions      30

31   remove jointly impossible selections; conditional integration prices what remains. 31
32   Each step has its own proof, numerical certificate and work account. The original         32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                            59

1    broader results and the difficult vector-horizon observations retain their separate      1

2    assumptions and evidence.                                                                2

3                                                                                             3

4                                                                                             4
         APPENDIX R: A FROZEN SHARED PROPOSAL ON UNSEEN WORKLOADS
5                                                                                             5

6      The joint-domain construction in Section A establishes a mathematical shared- 6
7    operator guarantee. A trained proposal’s incremental numerical value is a different      7

8    question. We test that value in the original investment model without changing its       8

9    continuous innovation law, feasible action cap or protected Bellman certificate. This    9

10   experiment adds a prospective comparison to the inherited recursive-query and            10

11   complete-policy studies; it does not substitute one of those objects for another.        11

12                                                                                            12

13                                                                                            13
                          R.1. Construction, selection and evaluation
14                                                                                            14

15     The protocol is fixed before fitting. The training stream contains 128 states in       15

16   each of dimensions two and eight, with two remaining dates. Protected adaptive           16

17   search at tolerance 1/256 supplies feasible action labels, normalized by the original    17

18   action cap. Both approximation families receive the same fourteen features: eight        18

19   zero-padded state coordinates, the overall, even-coordinate and odd-coordinate           19

20   means, the mean square, the mean adjacent product, and the dimension divided             20

21   by eight. The neural model is one width-32 ReLU network shared across both               21

22   dimensions. The nonneural comparator is one complete quadratic on the same               22

23   features. Each family has one attempted fit and fixed optimization settings, with no     23

24   hyperparameter search. All coefficients, training rows and fitting diagnostics are       24

25   retained before calibration.                                                             25

26     Calibration has 64 new states in each training dimension. The five services are        26

27   the adaptive baseline, bisection, a one-date analytic proposal, the shared ReLU          27

28   proposal and the shared quadratic proposal. Each runs at tolerance 1/64 using the        28

29   unchanged protected oracle. Selection minimizes their total action-value queries,        29

30   with the prespecified tie order. A terminal query is already included in that total      30

31   and is not added a second time. Calibration selects the adaptive baseline: its 990       31

32   queries compare with 999 for bisection, 1008 for the analytic proposal, 996 for ReLU     32
     60

1    and 999 for the quadratic proposal. This choice is committed before the evaluation       1

2    states are generated.                                                                    2

3      Evaluation contains 128 fresh states in each of dimensions two, four and eight. 3
4    Dimension four is absent from both training and calibration. All services receive        4

5    the identical states; three fresh-process repetitions preserve the complete timing       5

6    distribution. The saved coefficients and evaluation inputs also define the cross- 6
7    architecture replay. Repetitions and machine replay do not add independent states. 7
8    All streams are reproducible seeded numerical workloads. A seed alone is not an          8

9    assumption establishing population randomness; Proposition 6 and the coverage            9

10   theorem state the separate assumptions needed for probabilistic extrapolation.           10

11                                                                                            11

12                                                                                            12
                         R.2. Results and their economic interpretation
13                                                                                            13

14     The two fitting attempts return finite coefficients without a recorded warning. 14
15   All held-out services meet the common certificate tolerance. The adaptive service        15

16   attains the smallest total query count in each evaluation dimension; at dimension        16

17   eight it ties bisection and the quadratic proposal. Its empirical query regret against   17

18   the declared menu is therefore zero in each dimension. This is not a zero expected- 18
19   regret theorem and not a comparison with the original Bellman optimum.                   19

20     The ReLU proposal uses 987, 1017 and 987 queries in dimensions two, four and           20

21   eight, respectively, against 975, 993 and 969 for the adaptive service. The predictor    21

22   is genuinely shared and is evaluated on unseen states and an unseen dimension,           22

23   but it does not reduce verification work in this experiment. The protected first         23

24   partition already resolves many workloads before any model call. In the remaining        24

25   workloads, a model’s proposed query can fail to improve the protected baseline’s         25

26   allocation. All such calls and refinements are charged. This result identifies the       26

27   distinction between a valid common operator, a successfully returned fit and a           27

28   cost-saving proposal; none implies the next.                                             28

29     Complete process times include interpreter startup, coefficient loading, computa- 29
30   tion, serialization and shutdown. The numerical appendix reports every repetition        30

31   and peak resident memory on each recorded architecture, rather than only the             31

32   fastest execution. Logical dense-model arithmetic and disjoint oracle queries remain     32
                                NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                        61

6     APPENDIX S: D EPLOYMENT CHOICE UNDER QUALIFIED COST UNCERTAINTY                                      6

7      The attained continuation is an input to a complete economic decision, not merely                   7

8    to another approximation score. The same qualified costs can support a choice                         8

9    of acquisition precision across deployments. We retain the original NBO actors                        9

10   and draw a controller before observing the initial state or innovations. This imple- 10
11   ments a mixture of complete policies, not an average of their actions, coefficients                   11

12   or information sets. Its expected original-law cost is the corresponding mixture of                   12

13   the qualified costs. Minimax-regret allocation has an established decision-theoretic                  13

14   interpretation (Manski, 2004); the result below concerns the finite cost set supplied                 14

15   by NBO qualification, conditional on its simultaneous coverage event.                                 15

16     Let C and d( j, i ) be the consistent difference-constraint set and distances of Theo- 16
17   rem 35, stated and proved in the Numerical and Development Appendix. For a prob- 17
18   ability vector p ∈ ∆q and per-deployment charges w, set Hji (w) = d( j, i ) + wi − w j ,              18

19   for i, j = 1, . . . , q. The charges include all costs that actually vary linearly with de- 19
20   ployment shares; additional installation costs are treated separately.                                20

5    APPENDIX T: F ROM RETURNED POLICIES TO ECONOMIC RESOURCE DECISIONS                        5

6      An all-state upper bound and a comparison of actual policy costs answer different       6

7    economic questions. We now connect the policies returned by a construction frontier       7

8    to direct cost inference and then to a choice of implementation resources. The            8

9    construction data select the policies; independently generated evaluation paths           9

10   assess them. This separation permits a valid comparison of first-crossing policies        10

11   with different resolutions.                                                               11

12                                                                                             12

13                        T.1. Simultaneous inference after construction                       13

14                                                                                             14
       Let H denote all construction information: primitive inputs, numerical labels,
15                                                                                             15
     fitted continuations, returned policies and complete certificate ladders. Conditional
16                                                                                             16
     on H, fix a finite catalogue of implemented-policy contrasts. Each implementation
17                                                                                             17
     includes its observation rule, original witness or nearest-node selector, true-state
18                                                                                             18
     feasible repair and action quantization. For a contrast i, let Di be the difference in
19                                                                                             19
     expected discounted costs under a specified initial law. A common-innovation path
20                                                                                             20
     score evaluates the two policies on their own endogenous trajectories. It is not the
21                                                                                             21
     difference between two regret bounds.
22                                                                                             22
       Suppose the initial and innovation uniforms are partitioned into equal bins.
23                                                                                             23
     Independent uniform bin indices are drawn, and interval simulation encloses the
24                                                                                             24
     score for every continuous realization inside the drawn bins. All unresolved state,
25                                                                                             25
     selector and repair branches remain in the enclosure. If [ Ai , Bi ] is a deterministic
26                                                                                             26
     support interval, clip the path endpoints to that support. Write mi , mi for outward
27                                                                                             27
     sample-mean bounds on the lower and upper endpoint samples and vi,L , vi,U for
28                                                                                             28
     upper bounds on their unbiased sample variances. For n ≥ 2 define
29                                                                                             29

30
                                  √             7( Bi − Ai )ℓ                                  30
                     Ri ( v ) =       2vℓ/n +                 ,   ℓ ≥ log(4K/α),        (54)
31
                                                 3( n − 1)                                     31

32   where K bounds the number of sampled contrasts.                                           32

1      T HEOREM 24—Construction-conditional economic intervals: Under the stated                        1

2    independent-bin model, conditional on any valid construction history H, with probability           2

3    at least 1 − α all catalogue contrasts satisfy                                                     3

4                                                                                                       4
                 Di ∈ [li , ui ] := [max{ Ai , mi − Ri (vi,L )}, min{ Bi , mi + Ri (vi,U )}].   (55)
5                                                                                                       5

6    The same coverage holds unconditionally. Subsequent selection among these covered con- 6
7    trasts, or evaluation at any deterministic implementation-price vector, needs no additional        7

8    union bound. This conclusion applies only to policies and initial laws included in the             8

9    catalogue; it does not create direct-cost observations for every unstudied tolerance or initial    9

10   state.                                                                                             10

     The proof is in Supplement, Section 2.10.
12                                                                                                      12
       In particular, selecting the first policy to meet a specified certificate is permitted
13                                                                                                      13
     when that selection uses construction records rather than the subsequent evaluation
14                                                                                                      14
     paths. Repeated construction clocks are not independent draws from a neural-
15                                                                                                      15
     optimizer population. The statistical randomness here belongs to the evaluation
16                                                                                                      16
     design. A fixed pseudorandom stream implements a reproducible experiment; it is
17                                                                                                      17
     not itself a proof of the independent-bin assumption.
18                                                                                                      18

19                                                                                                      19
                                   T.2. Replacement-fee identification
20                                                                                                      20

21
       Write D = J W − J F . If witness is installed, replacing it by FVI changes total cost            21

22
     by k − D, where k ≥ 0 is the replacement fee. If FVI is installed, the change is k + D. 22

23
     Thus the two directional gains before fees are D and − D.                                          23

24     C OROLLARY 25—The break-even fee frontier: On an event where D ∈ [l, u], the                     24

25   two directional gains belong to [l, u] and [−u, −l ]. The unknown absolute cost difference         25

26   satisfies                                                                                          26

27                                                                                                      27
                                  | D | ∈ [dist(0, [l, u]), max{|l |, |u|}].                    (56)
28                                                                                                      28

29   For every k ≥ max{0, u, −l } neither replacement strictly lowers expected cost. By contrast,       29

30   k ≥ max{0, l, −u} only removes a certified strictly profitable replacement. The two thresh- 30
31   olds generally differ. Strict inequalities in the first criterion certify that both replacements   31

32   strictly increase cost.                                                                            32
     66

2      The fee frontier is more informative than a conclusion at one generous charge. 2
3    The earlier 1/64 replacement decision is retained with its original interpretation           3

4    and confidence account. The new analysis reports the two directional gain inter- 4
5    vals and both thresholds instead of selecting a favorable fee after seeing the data. 5
6    There is no welfare calibration hidden in the units: a decision maker must sup- 6
7    ply the economically relevant charge, or use the full frontier to see what remains           7

                    APPENDIX U: C ERTIFICATE - GATED NEURAL QUERIES
21                                                                                                       21

22     A prediction of the optimal action and a prediction of a useful numerical query are               22

23   different objects. Inserting a learned action before examining an already available                 23

24   certificate charges an extra continuation query even when the two boundary actions                  24

25   suffice. We instead make the demand for another query a mathematical decision and                   25

26   let a trained NBO selector determine its location only after that demand has been                   26

27   established. The underlying continuation recursion and the economic optimum are                     27

28   unchanged.                                                                                          28

29     At a recursive state, start with the two feasible action endpoints. Compute the                   29

30   global lower bound from the parabolic minorants of Lemma 36, including the                          30

31   capacity remainder, and the smallest upper endpoint. Return immediately when                        31

32   their difference is at most the requested tolerance. Otherwise choose a partition                   32
     68

1    interval with smallest current minorant. A learned action is used only if it lies in            1

2    the central half of that interval; it then replaces the conventional proposed split. 2
3    Outside that region the safeguarded parabolic split is used. The rounded split must             3

4    pass the one-fifth interior check before evaluation. A prediction is evaluated at most          4

5    once at each recursive state and is reused only as a proposed location. It supplies             5

6    neither a value endpoint nor a lower bound.                                                     6

7                                                                                                    7

8
          T HEOREM 27—Gated prediction and complete-policy validity: Under the assump- 8
9
     tions of Theorem 37, the gated service returns the same original-optimum and actual-policy      9

10
     guarantees for every supplied prediction, independently of fitting error. In real arithmetic,   10

11
     with endpoint-plus-capacity width at most 5δ/8, a sufficient number of endpoint evalua- 11
12
     tions at a state is                                                                             12
                                                             q          
13                                 Ar (δ, x ) = 4 + 5c( x )       Hr /(3δ) .                         13

14                                                                                                   14

15   Each requested evaluation either reduces the unresolved action partition or terminates          15

16   the query; no initial interior evaluation is mandatory. If the boundary certificate suffices,   16

17   no predictor is evaluated at that state. If every prediction is declined at every recursive     17

18   query, the value endpoints and implemented actions coincide with those of the conventional      18

19   parabolic service using the same arithmetic and tie rules.                                      19

20                                                                                                   20
     The proof is in Supplement, Section 2.8.
21                                                                                                   21
       This theorem establishes robustness to the numerical content of a fitted prediction,
22                                                                                                   22
     not that learning is free or always useful. It also does not state that an arbitrary
23                                                                                                   23
     routed transcript has fewer queries than the adaptive conventional transcript. The
24                                                                                                   24
     executed comparison records both possibilities. Unlike a policy catalogue with a
25                                                                                                   25
     stored reference action at every state, neither service constructs a conventional state
26                                                                                                   26
     lattice before it can return a certified policy.
27                                                                                                   27

28                                                                                                   28
                           U.1. State dimension, working memory, and reliability
29                                                                                                   29

30     For the original scalar model, the primitive bounds dGr ≤ 27 and dMr ≤ 54 imply               30

31                                                                                                   31
                                3369             1319             βMr−1 d    135
32                         Hr ≤      ,      Λr ≤      ,                   ≤       .                  32
                                 256              128              6144     16384
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                            69

1    Thus the action and scalar-innovation budgets at fixed remaining horizon and              1

2    tolerance are independent of the number of state coordinates. Every primitive eval- 2
3    uation still requires O(d) arithmetic. A width-w one-hidden-layer ReLU prediction         3

4    on the recorded d + 5 features requires O(dw) work and stored parameters; a full          4

5    quadratic prediction on these features uses O(d2 ) terms. Neither cost is included        5

6    for free in the Bellman recursion.                                                        6

7      Let qr (δ) be the dyadic quadrature count and let Pr (d) be the cost of one predic- 7
8    tor evaluation. Apart from the separately identified exact-arithmetic recovery, a         8

9    conservative work recursion is                                                            9

10                                                                                             10

11                Wr (d, δ) ≤ CdAr (δ) + Pr (d) + Ar (δ)qr (δ)Wr−1 (d, δ/(4β)).                11

12                                                                                             12

13   The predictor term is absent on a boundary-certified query. This expression removes       13

14   the exponential state lattice, not the horizon or innovation-tree dependence. A           14

15   depth-first scalar implementation can retain the current action partition and one         15

16   innovation child at a time. The executed batched implementation instead reports its       16

17   maximum state batch and process peak memory. A zero count of stored state-lattice         17

18   nodes does not mean zero storage.                                                         18

19     The deterministic certificate is valid for any supplied finite network weights. A       19

20   failure to fit and return such weights is a separate service event. Our reliability esti- 20
21   mand is the return count and worst measured complete work over the preregistered          21

22   finite seed catalogue, with every repetition retained. Numerical workload paths           22

23   are not optimizer replications. The real-arithmetic cap and the exact-rational preci- 23
24   sion condition establish algorithmic termination at admissible tolerances; recorded       24

25   processor seconds are descriptive, not a hardware-uniform worst-case bound.               25

26                                                                                             26

27                                                                                             27
                          U.2. An explicit economic purchase criterion
28                                                                                             28

29     The decision maker buys a callable controller rather than a table of successful         29

30   sample decisions. Fix a physical capital numeraire for the normalized state, a            30

31   conversion m > 0 dollars per unit of the model’s loss index, a computation charge         31

32   p ≥ 0 dollars per measured second, and a fixed deployment fee f ≥ 0 dollars. For n        32
     70

1    uses under a specified initial-state law, the monetary objective is                      1

2                                                                                             2

3                             C(π ) = nm E[ J0π ( X0 )] + pW (π ) + f .                       3

4                                                                                             4
     These are declared preference and service-price parameters, not estimated economic
5                                                                                             5
     primitives. The policy theorem bounds the expected loss under the original continu-
6                                                                                             6
     ous law for every such initial-state law. The finite dyadic workload below measures
7                                                                                             7
     execution; its average cost is not substituted for that expectation.
8                                                                                             8
       If two policies have an independently valid expected-gain interval [l, u] for
9                                                                                             9
     E[ J0π0 − J0π1 ], and their incremental measured service charge is ∆ = p(W1 − W0 ) +
10                                                                                            10
     f 1 − f 0 , then the monetary net gain lies in
11                                                                                            11

12                                                                                            12
                                       [nml − ∆, nmu − ∆].
13                                                                                            13

14                                                                                            14
     This follows by multiplication by the positive numeraire conversion and subtraction
15                                                                                            15
     of the same service charge. A positive lower endpoint supports purchase; a negative
16                                                                                            16
     upper endpoint rules it out at the stated prices. Uniform regret certificates alone do
17                                                                                            17
     not supply a directional expected-gain interval. They instead support a worst-case
18                                                                                            18
     accuracy requirement imposed before selecting the least expensive eligible service.
19                                                                                            19
       Finally, a forecast of amortized query work is distinguishable from a measured
20                                                                                            20
     complete service. With a fixed model and exactly repeated query workload, if
21                                                                                            21
     training requires B operations and deployment requires DL rather than DC oper-
22                                                                                            22
     ations per workload, then learning uses fewer total operations after k repetitions
23                                                                                            23
     precisely when DL < DC and k > B/( DC − DL ). If DL ≥ DC and B ≥ 0, no repeti-
24                                                                                            24
     tion of that unchanged workload produces an operation saving. This arithmetic
25                                                                                            25
     criterion includes training and cannot be applied to unrelated future states without
26                                                                                            26
     a new assumption. The executed catalogue reports its own full process clocks, not a
27                                                                                            27
     favorable hypothetical amortization.

       For T dates and target ϵ, write ST = ∑t<T βt . The recorded service chooses a
3                                                                                                  3

4                                                                                                  4
     outward allowance. Under dGr ≤ 27, a uniform per-date acquisition allowance at
5                                                                                                  5
     forty bits is
6                                                                                                6
                                             297β
7                                   αb = 8 +      + 27 2−40 .                                      7
                                              16
8                                                                                                  8
     The service certifies (δ + αb )ST ≤ ϵ. Each descendant request is δ/(4β), rounded as
9                                                                                                  9
     in the actual code and included in its outward endpoint guard. At the smallest re-
10                                                                                                 10
     quest in the two- and three-date catalogues, the declared thirty-two-bit action quan-
11                                                                                                 11
     tum satisfies the exact-recovery condition δr > 8Λr 2−32 . The supplement checks
12                                                                                                 12
     this inequality with rationals for every horizon and target, rather than inferring it
13                                                                                                 13
     from a successful workload.
14                                                                                                 14
       For fixed remaining horizon and accuracy, the bounds on action endpoints and
15                                                                                                 15
     scalar-innovation bins are independent of state dimension in this normalized econ-
16                                                                                                 16
     omy. The factors dGr and dMr are bounded, while every transition and cost still
17                                                                                                 17
     reads d coordinates. A width-wθ one-layer ReLU predictor has O(dwθ ) parameters
18                                                                                                 18
     and evaluation work; a dense quadratic on the same features has O(d2 ) terms. These
19                                                                                                 19
     statements identify the absence of a (n + 1)d stored state table. They do not count
20                                                                                                 20
     an arbitrary-precision rational operation as a unit-cost floating operation and do
21                                                                                                 21
     not suppress the product of descendant query counts as horizon grows.
22                                                                                                 22
       The two fitted implementations and their conventional controls therefore share
23                                                                                                 23
     one original-optimum contract, but need not share a transcript, a returned policy
24                                                                                                 24
     or a runtime. The completed experiments charge their distinct transcripts. A finite-
25                                                                                                 25
     catalogue reliability statement concerns all specified seeds and repetitions, including
26                                                                                                 26
     fitting warnings. It is not a population statement about optimizer success.

14     Cost inference can be combined with a deployment clock only after identifying the               14

30                                                                                                     30

31                                                                                                     31

32                                      pS > f + vm( B − D )u.                                         32
