1    If work is instead bounded by valid intervals, replacing Wπ − Wν by its interval difference   1

2    preserves the statement.                                                                      2

3                                                                                                  3

4    Proof. See Supplement, Section 3.8.                                                           4

5      The executed reuse design has B = 64, m = 8, and one predetermined identity                 5

6    rejection, so its normal-policy multiplier is 504. This does not make an observed             6

7    prefix clock a completed service, or an observed timing range a confidence interval           7

8    for future hardware. The paired complete receipts and the conservative envelope               8

9    across the observed repetitions are both reported. The inputs v, p, f remain declared         9

10   decision parameters; the experiment estimates neither an installation fee nor the             10

11   monetary value of normalized investment losses. The policy-cost margin supplies               11

12   an economically interpretable eligibility condition, while the full adoption region           12

13   keeps computational savings and policy quality separate.                                      13

14                                                                                                 14

15           APPENDIX Y: T RAINING , REUSE , AND POLICY- COST PRECISION                            15

16                                                                                                 16
       The experiment links the constructive readout to the actual controlled economy.
17                                                                                                 17
     It compares representation and training loss at a common information budget,
18                                                                                                 18
     validates a frozen readout on independent contexts, measures a longer deployment
19                                                                                                 19
     window, and evaluates the returned controllers at a prespecified policy-cost margin.
20                                                                                                 20
     The original continuous laws, state-dependent investment capacity and Bellman
21                                                                                                 21
     target are unchanged.
22                                                                                                 22

23                                                                                                 23
                                Y.1. Design and information balance
24                                                                                                 24

25     The scalar tasks are (d, T, ε) = (2, 2, 1/128) and (8, 3, 1/64), with twenty-four- 25
26   bit state acquisition and thirty-two-bit downward action rounding. Four fitted                26

27   proposals receive the same forty-eight training states, approximate action labels and         27

28   verified boundary endpoints per nonterminal date. The certificate-aware hat–ReLU              28

29   and degree-four Bernstein readouts have exactly 5d box-constrained coefficients               29

30   and the same objective, penalty and optimizer budget. An action-label readout on              30

31   the same hat features separates the objective from the representation. A quadratic            31

32   label fit retains its explicitly different parameter count. Adaptive parabolic search         32
     78

1    and upper-chord minimization are prediction-free controls; unused training is not       1

2    charged to them.                                                                        2

3      The hat basis has an exact finite ReLU realization. It also has a conventional        3

4    piecewise-linear interpretation, so this comparison does not count two encodings of     4

5    that basis as independent algorithms. The Bernstein comparison holds the coefficient    5

6    count and purchased information fixed. All fitted proposals use the same final          6

7    certificate. Every returned certificate-aware iterate is quantized and receives an      7

8    exact rational value of the objective and box dual gap from Proposition 40. The         8

9    achieved gap is reported rather than assuming that an optimizer’s success flag          9

10   proves optimality.                                                                      10

11     The protocol fixes two fitting seeds and four process repetitions. It contains        11

12   eighty cold comparisons, sixteen independent-validation services, fifty-six reuse       12

13   services, two paired-inference services and thirty-four vector services: 188 complete   13

14   processes. All returned. This is finite-catalogue evidence for the complete verifier,   14

15   not a population claim about fitting success. Four clock repetitions are not four new   15

16   training samples. The construction, receipts and original source freeze predate this    16

17   manuscript integration; the publication replay adds no scientific observations.         17

18     The complete cold-start results are reported in Supplement Table AE.I. At a fixed     18

19   purchased-information and coefficient budget, the hat–ReLU readouts close 48 and        19

20   50 of 256 small-task validation contexts for the two seeds, compared with 25 and        20

21   21 for Bernstein. The achieved exact box dual gaps are at most 10−7 across the          21

22   certificate-aware catalogue. This is a method-specific improvement in the declared      22

23   context screen, not a uniform complete-runtime advantage. All methods and both          23

24   tasks remain in the supplementary validation table.                                     24

25     Cold-start work remains an exacting comparison. In the two-state task the median      25

26   complete hat–ReLU process takes about 0.465 seconds, against 0.365 for adaptive         26

27   search. In the eight-state task the respective medians are about 1.618 and 0.666        27

28   seconds; both use 2,594 policy-query evaluations in the displayed workload. These       28

29   counts exclude separately reported training queries. Thus the certified objective       29

30   is now an executed arm, but its execution is not an identified complete-work            30

31   improvement. The recorded fitting, prediction and descendant counts locate the          31

32   cost rather than attributing every successful return to a successful predictor.         32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                           79

1                              Y.2. Prospective validation and reuse                          1

2                                                                                             2

3                                                                                             3

4                                                                                             4

5      Each fitted readout is frozen before a new predictor-independent context stream        5

6    drawn uniformly from the sixteen-bit dyadic state grid is generated. There are 256       6

7    contexts per nonterminal date. The validation calculation executes the declared          7

8    complete-action mesh recovery whenever the pre-query screen fails. Its clipped loss      8

9    bounds the additional root queries on every recorded context. The supplementary          9

10   table gives the achieved loss, actual recovery queries, simultaneous validation          10

11   upper bound and descendant counts for every task, date, method and seed. These           11

12   6,144 method–context–date evaluations validate the stated context law and fallback;      12

13   they are not relabeled as independent on-policy recursive states.                        13

14     Each reuse process trains and serializes once and then serves sixty-four predeter- 14
15   mined blocks of eight trajectories. At block index 16 it deliberately receives a stale   15

16   acquisition identifier and uses the adaptive benchmark. Loading, hashing, identity       16

17   validation, rejection, queries, cash-path enclosures, durable files and process shut- 17
18   down are included. A stable observed crossing must begin by block index 48 and           18

19   remain below the matched adaptive prefix through index 63. This requirement is           19

20   fixed before execution.                                                                  20

21     Three of the eight hat–ReLU processes in the two-state task satisfy that stable- 21
22   prefix criterion; four have smaller final complete times than their matched adaptive     22

23   process. The Bernstein and quadratic rules also exhibit small-task crossings. None       23

24   of the learned processes wins the eight-state reuse comparison. In the small task,       24

25   the adaptive complete times range from about 3.17 to 7.13 seconds, while hat–ReLU        25

26   ranges from about 3.32 to 5.07 seconds. Consequently, a favorable matched crossing       26

27   cannot be interpreted as uniform amortized superiority. The operation counts and         27

28   every unfavorable repetition remain in the record.                                       28

29     The deployment theorem states what additional context-law and descendant-cost          29

30   information would be sufficient for a prospective work bound. The experiment             30

31   does not substitute its identity check for a distributional drift bound. Likewise, the   31

32   sixty-four-block window is not extrapolated to an unobserved stopping horizon.           32

17                                                                                                                17
                                Y.3. A cost comparison at the declared precision
18                                                                                                                18

19     The central inference change is the Bellman-centered observation of Theorem 1. 19
20   It estimates the actual expected cost difference by evaluating optimal advantages                            20

21   along each actual policy trajectory. The conditional Bellman expectations are eval- 21
22   uated with a tighter tolerance and paid for separately. The estimator is not the                             22

23   difference between two policy-loss upper bounds, and its realized observation is                             23

24   not asserted equal to a same-shock cash-cost difference.                                                     24

25     For each scalar task the protocol fixes 1,024 new common-path rows, forty-eight- 25
26   bit continuous bins and evaluation tolerance ε/32. The policies are adaptive search,                         26

27   certificate-aware hat–ReLU, certificate-aware Bernstein, and the original R67 ReLU                           27

28   controller at its pinned fitting seed. That historical controller is loaded unchanged. 28
29   The predeclared loss margin is 1/256, with numerical and sampling half-width                                 29

30   allocations of 1/512 each. No new row is added after inspecting a sign or a failed                           30

31   comparison. There are twelve paired contrasts across the two tasks on one centered- 31
32   inference event with failure probability at most 0.01.                                                       32

21     All twelve centered intervals lie within [−1/256, 1/256]. They therefore establish                        21

22   two-sided equivalence at the declared normalized-loss margin, including com- 22
23   parisons involving the unchanged historical neural controller. Every interval still                         23

24   contains zero: strict cost superiority is not identified. Both precision allocations are                    24

25   met. This replaces the earlier inability to assess the declared margin by a positive,                       25

26   original-law eligibility result, without changing the economic estimand or assuming                         26

27   a fitted value is exact.                                                                                    27

     82

1    improvement for the same expected-cost target, not an identified neural speed          1

2    advantage. The complete shared inference processes cost about 9.03 and 408.63          2

3    seconds, respectively. Conditional Bellman integration, rather than state-bin width,   3

4    dominates their numerical enclosure budget.                                            4

5      The two controllers’ common initial-value terms cancel in expectation. All com- 5
6    patible true states within a sampled bin remain covered. No new inference trajectory   6

7    in this catalogue has an unresolved acquisition branch. The raw conditional-value      7

8    width, propagated bin width, acquisition allowance and final arithmetic diagnos- 8
9    tics are reported separately; they are not assumed statistically independent, and      9

10   pre-intersection component widths are not falsely presented as an additive decom- 10
11   position of the tighter final interval.                                                11

13                                                                                          13
                            Y.4. Two controls and the economic service
14                                                                                          14

15     The vector study compares simplicial search, largest-edge uniform refinement,        15

16   a trained two-output hat readout and a trained two-output quadratic proposal at        16

17   two state dimensions and horizons two and three. Both learned rules purchase the       17

18   same twelve teacher states per nonterminal date. Their training objective is action- 18
19   label loss, not the scalar convex certificate loss. All receive the same triangular    19

20   lower certificate and feasible rounding rule under two independent continuous          20

21   innovations. The horizon-four hat proposal transfers its three-date model and is       21

22   identified as a transfer, not as a four-date fitted model.                             22

23     Supplement Table AE.II reports every two-control workload.                           23

24     The vector records contain actual continuous-bin policy paths, complete clocks,      24

25   peak resident memory and descendant counts. Eight paths per ordinary service,          25

26   and one per horizon-four transfer service, are workload diagnostics rather than a      26

27   precision-designed population comparison. The experiment adds a trained vector         27

28   comparison, but it does not identify a vector expected-cost ranking or remove          28

29   continuation-tree growth. Derivative branch-and-bound is not an executed arm of        29

30   this catalogue.                                                                        30

31     For the scalar deployment decision, the release verifies that the cost-inference     31

32   readouts coincide with the relevant cold and reuse readouts at seed 6901. Param- 32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                          83

1    eter matching is necessary for pairing these records. Under the additional same- 1
2    numerical-policy premise, Proposition 32 gives their complete-service adoption          2

3    region, with normal-policy multiplier 504 and observed work differences. Cross- 3
4    platform prediction portability is separately discussed in the supplement. The          4

5    supplementary table reports the work savings and critical price ratios, including       5

6    cases in which savings are negative. The loss margin is a prospective numerical         6

7    design choice, not an estimated installation charge. Equivalence at that margin         7

8    makes policy quality an eligible comparison; adoption depends on declared prices        8

9    and complete work, including newly purchased qualification.                             9

10                                                                                           10

11                    Y.5. Earlier evidence and independent reconstruction                   11

12                                                                                           12
       The original R67 catalogue remains a separate, unchanged companion, together
13                                                                                           13
     with all preceding adverse studies. It records 212 returns, no neural complete-reuse
14                                                                                           14
     winner, unresolved ordinary cost intervals, forty-eight neural fitting warnings and a
15                                                                                           15
     severe horizon-four vector expansion. Those observations are not overwritten by the
16                                                                                           16
     new loss, new inference rows or different reuse window. The previous accumulation-
17                                                                                           17
     aliasing counterexample and corrected execution also remain unchanged.
18                                                                                           18
       The new publication audit verifies every R69 receipt and stored-file identity,
19                                                                                           19
     recomputes the achieved rational training certificates, checks every validation loss
20                                                                                           20
     and executes independently written rational path calculations. A second interval
21                                                                                           21
     implementation uses exact full-bin state and cost bounds, not only representative
22                                                                                           22
     points, for unambiguous acquired paths. Its coverage and every diagnostic dis-
23                                                                                           23
     agreement are recorded explicitly. This provides independent reconstruction of the
24                                                                                           24
     sampled cash-path calculation and independent final inference arithmetic. It is not
25                                                                                           25
     represented as an independent generation of every optimal-advantage endpoint or
26                                                                                           26
     of the largest vector Bellman descendant tree.

1      Each row has 256 new contexts; seed suffix 1 or 2 denotes 6901 or 6902. The upper bound is inde- 1
2    pendently recomputed from an exact sample sum and an outward radius. It applies to the declared   2

3    complete-action mesh recovery and context law, not automatically to an adaptive on-policy stream. 3
4    Recovery-query totals include descendants.                                                        4

5      The validation family contains twenty-four task–date–method–seed records with                   5

6    256 contexts per record. The logarithm allowance twelve covers the stated finite fam- 6
7    ily by a union bound. Reusing a context stream across methods induces dependence                  7

8    between estimators but does not invalidate that bound. The same stream is not                     8

9    counted as additional independent information for each method. The deterministic                  9

10   work inequality is checked on every context. The distributional upper endpoint is                 10

11   recomputed from the exact sample sum, an eighty-digit square root with an explicit                11

12   expansion, and outward conversion to the printed numerical endpoint.                              12

13     The full-action recovery mesh in this validation design has a declared root cap K. 13
14   Its actual descendant queries are separately recorded. These observations do not                  14

15   validate a different adaptive fallback by definition. In Theorem 31, a distributional             15

16   transfer allowance and a complete endpoint-work cap are additional premises. The                  16

17   theorem therefore handles predictable choices and dependent contexts without                      17

18   asserting that the recorded recursive state stream is independent.                                18

20                                                                                                     20
           APPENDIX AA: P RECISION AND THE CONTINUOUS - LAW ESTIMAND
21                                                                                                     21

22     The centered observation is the sum of optimal Bellman advantages along an                      22

23   actual policy trajectory. Its expectation equals policy loss. Subtracting two such                23

24   observations cancels the common initial optimal value in expectation. It does not                 24

25   cancel cash-flow risk path by path, and no empirical fitted value is substituted                  25

26   for the optimal value. Each conditional Bellman evaluation uses its own tolerance,                26

27   action covering and continuous-innovation integration account, whose work is                      27

28   charged to the inference service.                                                                 28

29     The twelve paired intervals form one finite family over the two declared scalar                 29

30   tasks. With logarithm allowance twelve, 2 · 12 exp(−12) < 0.01. For example, a ratio- 30
31   nal partial sum of the exponential series already proves exp(12) > 2400; no rounded               31

32   logarithm is needed for the coverage claim. The ordinary cash-cost estimator uses                 32

     Component columns are mean full widths before valid regret-range intersections. They do not add to

16                                                                                                           16

17   a separate empirical-Bernstein family on the same rows. A joint claim about both                        17

18   families requires the sum of their tail budgets, not an assumption that the estimators                  18

19   are independent.                                                                                        19

20     The mean conditional-value and state-bin component widths are recorded before                         20

21   intersecting with valid local regret ranges. The intersection can substantially reduce                  21

22   the resulting interval. Accordingly, those diagnostic widths need not sum to the final                  22

23   centered width, and they are not interpreted as independent errors. The state-bin                       23

24   column includes propagated state arithmetic. The actual final interval width is the                     24

25   mean final numerical width plus the two sampling radii, before any final support                        25

26   clipping.                                                                                               26

27     For every path, the numerical action used by the controller is fixed before the                       27

28   more accurate evaluation. Its feasibility is checked on the true-state box as well as on                28

29   the acquired state. An unresolved acquisition branch must receive a valid remaining                     29

30   support; it cannot be discarded. None is encountered in the new inference catalogue. 30
31   The absence of sampled ambiguity is an observation, not a theorem excluding                             31

32   acquisition boundaries from the model.                                                                  32
     88

1              APPENDIX AB: I NDEPENDENT FULL - BIN RECONSTRUCTION                           1

2                                                                                            2

3
       The new independent implementation imports no NBO numerical kernel. It                3

4
     uses exact rational intervals for the continuous initial and innovation bins, twenty- 4

5
     four-bit acquired-state comparisons, true-state feasibility, stage costs, transitions   5

6
     and terminal costs. It treats the two cumulative endpoints as separate immutable        6

7
     rational numbers. In a transition coordinate, the deterministic function                7

8                                                                                            8

9                                           1  u v u (1 − v )                                9
                              f (u, v) =      + + +
10                                         16 2 8      16                                    10

11                                                                                           11

12   has derivatives 1/2 + (1 − v)/16 ≥ 0 and 1/8 − u/16 ≥ 0 on the state cube. Thus         12

13   evaluation at the lower–lower and upper–upper corners bounds its range without          13

14   invoking the production interval library. Quadratic costs use exact interval squares;   14

15   shortage uses the monotone positive-part map followed by an interval square. Both       15

16   shocks keep their original widths and marginal laws.                                    16

17     The implementation checks that its complete bin enclosure lies inside the archived    17

18   cash-cost enclosure. A second, independently written rational midpoint calculation      18

19   checks acquisition and cumulative cost at a represented point. The midpoint test        19

20   is not substituted for the full-bin test. Across 3,706 physical path archives there     20

21   are 38,722 recorded path rows; 736 distinct payloads contain 14,242 unique rows. 21
22   Repeated timing copies are hash-checked and compared, but their duplicate rational      22

23   evaluation is not counted again. The independent full-bin replay covers all 14,242      23

24   unique rows, with no unresolved branch in this executed catalogue.                      24

25     A separate exact-sum and high-precision calculation checks every final centered       25

26   confidence interval from its stored advantage endpoints. This independently verifies    26

27   final inference arithmetic, not the generation of those optimal-advantage endpoints. 27
28   The regression suite compares primitive identities and small complete value prob- 28
29   lems with separate rational formulas. It does not independently regenerate the          29

30   largest multivariate Bellman descendant tree. The release states these different        30

31   coverage levels rather than assigning an undifferentiated independent-verification      31

32   label to the whole program.                                                             32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                         89

1                    AB.1. Prediction portability and retained disagreements                 1

2                                                                                            2
       An initial strict replay stopped because one Bernstein validation prediction was
3                                                                                            3
     not bitwise equal to a recomputed value. That failed attempt, its original audit
4                                                                                            4
     source and traceback are retained on their own branch and in the publication
5                                                                                            5
     inputs. The failure does not by itself identify an invalid Bellman bound. Nor does
6                                                                                            6
     a successful rerun on another processor prove the earlier numerical result bitwise
7                                                                                            7
     reproducible.
8
       The current audit checks the saved actual action on its declared lattice and recom- 8
9                                                                                            9
     putes its strong-convexity chord with exact rational arithmetic. A discrepancy with
10                                                                                           10
     a recomputed readout is additionally checked against the declared action-rounding
11
     and prediction allowances, not accepted by an arbitrary numerical closeness thresh- 11
12
     old. The original scientific endpoints, actions, models and source freeze are un- 12
13                                                                                           13
     changed. Numerical parameter identity, callable-policy identity and successful
14                                                                                           14
     certificate verification are distinct statements.
15                                                                                           15
       In particular, equality of saved readout arrays is necessary but not sufficient for
16
     transferring a cost estimate to an implementation on a different arithmetic runtime. 16
17                                                                                           17
     The complete-service purchase region assumes the same numerical callable policy or
18                                                                                           18
     an additional valid implementation-transfer bound. Matching processor metadata
19                                                                                           19
     or passing a finite replay is not silently promoted to an all-state policy-identity
20                                                                                           20
     theorem.

12                                                                                                            12

13     Seed 6901; zero incremental installation fee and common or previously paid qualification. The loss     13

14   upper is 504 times the corresponding policy-cost upper endpoint. A displayed positive-saving threshold   14

15   describes the adoption region only when the numerical callable policy is the one evaluated by inference. 15

16   Saved parameter equality is checked, but cross-platform numerical equality is an additional requirement. 16

17   A dash means no positive work saving in that matched observation. Newly purchased qualification          17

18   work must be added before claiming a first-purchase saving. Prices are declared inputs, not estimated    18

19   currency values.                                                                                         19

20                                                                                                            20

21     Qualification is not free. The displayed reuse thresholds condition on a common                        21

22   or previously paid qualification calculation. The process receipts cover the complete                    22

23   reuse service, not the separate policy-cost inference experiment. For a first purchase,                  23

24   let Cq be additional qualification work not paid by the benchmark alternative. In                        24

25   Proposition 32, use Wπ = Wπrun + Cq and Wν = Wνrun ; the observed saving becomes                         25

26   S − Cq . This is a change of the explicit accounting boundary, not an additional theo- 26
27   rem assumption about free inference. Charging the entire observed shared inference                       27

28   process gives Cq approximately 9.03 seconds in the small task and 408.63 seconds in                      28

29   the larger task. Every displayed matched saving then becomes negative. Thus the                          29

30   current receipts do not identify a positive qualification-plus-reuse work saving un- 30
31   der that conservative first-purchase boundary. An allocated marginal inference time                      31

32   or a longer repayment horizon was not separately executed. The machine-readable                          32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                          91

1    table retains both boundaries, and the general price region remains valid with the      1

2    actual charged work and installation fee.                                               2

3      The multiplier 504 follows from sixty-four blocks of eight trajectories with one      3

4    predetermined fallback block. In that block the service uses the benchmark itself,      4

5    giving zero expected policy-cost difference. In the other blocks it uses the fixed      5

6    candidate. The cost interval can therefore be scaled by 504 under the policy-identity   6

7    premise of Proposition 32. A positive work saving is an observed matched process        7

8    difference, not a confidence bound for future latency. The complete timing range also   8

9    records all unfavorable repetitions and does not authorize selection of a favorable     9

10   timing result as a population estimate.                                                 10

11     The parent receipt includes child launch, imports, fitting or loading, all policy     11

12   and path calculations, serialization, child exit and log flushing. Parent receipt       12

13   bookkeeping belongs to the group clock. Training, inference and publication replay      13

14   have different boundaries and are not assigned zero cost when excluded from a           14

15   displayed sub-account. The machine record includes the permitted CPU, thread            15

16   counts, available frequency and governor data. Processor frequency was not locked       16

17   by the experiment. Fine timing differences consequently remain descriptive even         17

18   when operation counts reproduce exactly.                                                18

19     The original normalized investment units are retained. The loss margin is fixed by    19

20   the numerical protocol; the value of a loss unit, compute price and installation fee    20

21   are purchaser inputs. This produces a transparent conditional adoption region, not      21

22   an estimated monetary welfare result. Its noninferiority and equivalence statements     22

23   concern the actual expected-cost target and do not imply strict neural superiority.     23

32   ple. The broader NBO development remains available under its original diffusion,        32

1    recursive-preference, temporal-self and game hypotheses; those hypotheses are not                        1

3      The new frozen R69 catalogue is integrated once. Publication replay, repeated                          3

4    clocks, repeated random streams, duplicate path payloads and regenerated tables                          4

5    do not increase its number of independent economic observations. The final source                        5

6    and content map records the location and hash of each retained item. Compilation,                        6

       Stored is the maximum number of retained witnesses. Stale counts discarded records at the declared   21

22
     context invalidation. Fill and lookup are internal subclocks, not the complete process boundary. The   22

23
     lookup account includes record-load validation. Repeated storage, hashing, output and shutdown are     23

24
     additionally included in complete receipts.                                                            24

25                                                                                                          25
                              AF.1. Complete timing and retained records
26                                                                                                          26

27     The process receipt includes creation, Python imports, original Bellman fills, all                   27

28   recursive descendants, cache retrieval, rational checks, repeated serialization, final                 28

29   output and process exit. The driver writes a separate receipt after that boundary. 29
30   Summing child block clocks is not the complete process time. Every block’s body                        30

31   prefix excludes imports and has its own exact definition in the source. The complete                   31

32   receipts, rather than those prefixes, determine the reported timing comparison.                        32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                            97

1      No processor frequency lock is claimed. The start and end records include the           1

2    selected CPU, frequency fields where the environment exposes them, interpreter            2

3    version and platform. A cyclic mode order limits a simple fixed-order confound            3

4    but does not create randomized hardware-frequency inference. Close clock differ- 4
5    ences require more evidence than the prescribed finite repetitions. Bellman action        5

6    counts are consequently reported separately from seconds. They are invariant de- 6
7    scriptive counts for these transcripts, not predictions of another implementation’s       7

8    throughput.                                                                               8

9      The 128-record cap bounds logical retained witnesses. It does not bound total           9

10   process resident memory: a fresh recursive fill can retain batched descendants,           10

11   interpreter state, interval work arrays and output buffers. The largest process           11

12   memory remains in the table. The executed cache operates at workload roots only. 12
13   The four-date pressure is not hidden by counting a later root hit as though the first     13

14   fill used no descendants.                                                                 14

15                                                                                             15

16                                                                                             16
                         AF.2. Independent checks and trusted premises
17                                                                                             17

18     The additional admission audit matches all 708 returns to the prescribed state,         18

19   control dimension, horizon, tolerance and context epoch. Cache reloads in this            19

20   catalogue use the same process and numerical model. Arbitrarily supplied external         20

21   intervals are not made valid by deserialization or a file hash.                           21

22     For a returned hit, the independent checker locates the earlier paid source record      22

23   with the same context, horizon, source state, action and endpoints. It recomputes         23

24   the original regularity constants, state distance, action displacement and interval       24

25   expansion in exact rational arithmetic. It checks the returned action’s lattice and       25

26   capacity and that the serialized binary endpoints contain the exact expanded in- 26
27   terval. A narrow stored interval alone would not prove that a source fill encloses        27

28   the original value: the inherited verifier and its primitive proof remain explicit        28

29   premises.                                                                                 29

30     For conditional ReLU integration, the second implementation reconstructs the            30

31   original projected drift and shocks independently. It does not call the divided-power     31

32   formula. It clips the support rectangle against the affine neuron’s positive half-plane   32
     98

1    and uses oriented area and first moments. Degenerate projections reduce to one- 1
2    dimensional integration or a deterministic positive part. All rational discrepancies    2

3    are fatal to the new arithmetic audit and are retained as evidence if encountered.      3

4      The earlier R70 independent cash-path audit and final inference arithmetic are        4

5    unchanged. The historical full descendant trees are not certified by pretending that    5

6    a file hash is a mathematical proof. Neither the new rational transfer checks nor the   6

7    polygon integrations independently regenerate all those trees. The release states       7

8    this boundary in its machine-readable audit and response.                               8

9                                                                                            9

10                                                                                           10
                     AF.3. Development probe and publication preservation
11                                                                                           11

12     Before the new source commitment, a vector development probe queried (0.4, 0.6)       12

13   at tolerance 0.04 and horizons two, three and four. The first two returned. The probe   13

14   was stopped at its 35-second process limit while computing horizon four. Its log is     14

15   retained separately and is not merged with the later 900-second scientific catalogue    15

16   or used as a favorable timing observation.                                              16

17     Every R70 active section is assigned to exactly one current document and checked      17

18   byte for byte. All inherited formal statements and proofs are thereby retained,         18

19   not reconstructed from a favorable summary. The complete old main article and           19

20   supplement remain companion PDFs. New tables are derived from the committed             20

21   receipts and independent audit, and all four current documents are rebuilt from a       21

22   clean source directory. Visual inspection is documented against the exact PDF hashes    22

23   actually inspected. Compilation and tests do not constitute external mathematical       23

24   acceptance.                                                                             24

28     The fixed design was committed before any new construction service ran. Its four      28

30   bases. The terminal continuation is the original g; each preceding date is fitted       30

31   against the continuation actually returned at the following date. The stage label is    31

32   an interval containing the full continuous-action Bellman infimum. The coefficient      32

20     The ReLU basis consists of the constant and, in each coordinate, the identity and                       20

21   positive parts at 1/4, 1/2 and 3/4. The conventional basis consists of the constant                       21

22   and the four nonconstant degree-four Bernstein polynomials per coordinate. Their                          22

23   valid feature moduli are respectively one and four. Having the same number of                             23

24   coefficients does not make their function classes or approximation moduli identical. 24

26   rule; the label-loss ablation within each stage shares those exact labels and features. 26

29   mal value minus the exact dual lower bound, not the solver’s reported duality                             29

30   tolerance. Constant recentering changes the continuation gauge without changing                           30

9              APPENDIX AH: E XACT SEQUENTIAL - LEDGER REGRESSIONS                           9

10                                                                                           10

11     The new regression suite checks conditional means, range reservation, target          11

12   changes and failed numerical episodes. Its adaptive binary-tree diagnostic enumer- 12
13   ates all 212 = 4096 leaves and every prefix, with exact probabilities and changing      13

14   predictable ranges. The event count is a finite-model check of the implemented          14

15   boundary, not an empirical substitute for the supermartingale proof. Rational loga- 15
16   rithm bounds and coefficient duals are also tested independently of floating solver     16

17   stopping flags.                                                                         17

18     A support failure and a numerical failure are deliberately different branches. 18
19   An empty intersection or a changed fixed target is rejected. A numerical failure        19

20   records the prequalified full support, advances the prefix and charges its work. The    20

21   interface cannot prove that a caller fixed the support before observing the episode;    21

22   that temporal and probabilistic premise remains part of the algorithm’s contract. A     22

23   cache carried across episodes is included in the target definition or is handled as     23

24   varying conditional cost.                                                               24

25     All prior scientific arrays and sources remain unchanged. The ordinary-source         25

26   build runs the inherited and current tests and recomputes the published tables. 26
27   The clean archive includes the transitive R62 input sources, including the original     27

28   service kernel omitted by the interrupted R72 archive. The reproduction report          28

29   identifies the suites run inside that archive and compares actual PDF text and          29

30   page counts after deleting generated PDFs and tables. Visual inspection is recorded     30

31   separately against the output hashes. None of those publication checks is an external   31

32   mathematical acceptance decision.                                                       32

       A second way to control the descendant cost is to reuse a calculation already
3                                                                                              3
     purchased. Exact memoization only recognizes the same state. The following rule
4                                                                                              4
     transfers a lower Bellman bound and its feasible upper witness to a nearby state,
5                                                                                              5
     after repairing capacity and accounting for the full displacement. It is compatible
6                                                                                              6
     with either a neural or a conventional generator of the original certified record.

           APPENDIX AJ: C ONTINUATION EVALUATION AND PAID QUERY REUSE
26                                                                                                     26

27     The evidence separates three questions: whether a certified readout returns an                  27

28   admissible policy, whether its actual expected cost meets a declared margin, and                  28

29   whether a purchased calculation can be reused profitably. The preceding training                  29

30   and inference catalogue remains unchanged. The new execution fixes localized                      30

31   query workloads and exact conditional-moment checks before running them. It                       31

32   changes neither the economic primitives nor the continuous feasible-action target.                32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                             105

1                     AJ.1. Retained trained policies and economic precision                       1

2                                                                                                  2
       The retained 188-process catalogue is reproduced in Appendix, Section Y. Hat–
3                                                                                                  3
     ReLU and Bernstein readouts purchase the same teacher information and have
4                                                                                                  4
     exactly 5d coefficients; the achieved objective and box dual gaps are recomputed
5                                                                                                  5
     rationally. Independent validation and sixty-four-block reuse receive separate work
6                                                                                                  6
     accounts. Both their favorable and unfavorable repetitions remain visible. In partic-
7                                                                                                  7
     ular, the eight-state task does not identify a learned complete-work winner.
8                                                                                                  8
       The direct policy-cost experiment fixes its sample count and numerical pre-
9                                                                                                  9
     cision before execution. All twelve original-law paired intervals lie within the
10                                                                                                 10
     declared equivalence region [−1/256, 1/256], and all contain zero. The two
11                                                                                                 11
     adaptive-minus-hat–ReLU intervals are approximately [−0.00068263, 0.00070764]
12                                                                                                 12
     and [−0.00160291, 0.00160253]. Their shared inference services cost about 9.03 and
13                                                                                                 13
     408.63 seconds. These are informative quality comparisons with expensive qualifi-
14                                                                                                 14
     cation, not an identified neural cost ranking or a free inference improvement. The
15                                                                                                 15
     complete precision and adoption tables are retained in the appendix.
16                                                                                                 16
       The frozen-continuation theorem supplies a different way to evaluate that
17                                                                                                 17
     expected-cost estimand. Its moment component is executed here; a new trained
18                                                                                                 18
     critic meeting the old equivalence margin at lower complete qualification cost is
19                                                                                                 19
     not presumed. The earlier optimal-advantage observations are not counted again
20                                                                                                 20
     as samples of the new formula.
21                                                                                                 21

22                                                                                                 22
                            AJ.2. A fixed original-law query catalogue
23                                                                                                 23

24     The new design has five tasks: two scalar-control tasks at (d, T ) = (2, 2), (8, 3)         24

25   and three two-control tasks at (d, T ) = (2, 2), (2, 3), (2, 4). Each task has three rules:   25

26   fresh calculation, exact-key reuse and geometric witness transport. The source                26

27   commitment fixes the task list, tolerances, repetition counts, 128-record capacity,           27

28   invalidation schedule, workload order and 900-second per-process limit. There are             28

29   36 prescribed complete processes. None trains a new action predictor or loads a               29

30   precomputed conventional policy.                                                              30

31     A workload starts at alternating coordinates 0.4, 0.6. Odd requests repeat that             31

32   state. Predetermined even requests perturb one coordinate by 2−20 . Except in the             32
     106

1    four-date stress case, a late distant request has every coordinate equal to 0.75, and a   1

2    specified earlier block invalidates the context. The first four tasks have 32, 16, 24     2

3    and 8 requests; the four-date task has four. The first three tasks have three timing      3

4    repetitions, the three-date vector task two, and the four-date task one. Rules are        4

5    executed in a fixed cyclic order across repetitions.                                      5

6      Every miss calls the unchanged original recursive verifier at the declared toler- 6
7    ance. Every hit uses the exact state-transfer and capacity-repair test. The cache is      7

8    serialized, hashed, reloaded and validated after every block. The receipt begins          8

9    before process creation and ends after exit, including imports, all descendant work,      9

10   file output and shutdown; writing the parent receipt itself is separate publication       10

11   overhead. A single CPU is pinned. Available start and end frequency information is        11

12   recorded, but frequency is not locked. Repetitions support descriptive comparisons,       12

13   not a hardware-uniform or population timing theorem.                                      13

14     The table counts Bellman action evaluations at all descendants, not only workload       14

15   roots. Geometric hits at a nonidentical state distinguish transport from exact memo- 15
16   ization. Their capacity-repair count is separately recorded, including downward           16

17   repairs that change the returned action. The distant request and context invalidation     17

18   test whether a cheaper rule refuses an unjustified reuse rather than forcing a hit. An    18

19   unchanged numerical law with a new context identifier still triggers the declared         19

20   conservative invalidation.                                                                20

21     The four-date task retains the first expensive tree. A hit avoids that tree on          21

22   a later request, whereas a miss pays for it again. This is a local repeated-query         22

23   improvement under the original optimum contract, not a disappearance of the long- 23
24   horizon first-fill cost. The trace records every source witness, state displacement,      24

25   repaired action and outward endpoint. The absence of a state lattice does not justify     25

26   omitting either the descendant count or resident memory.                                  26

27     The full repetition and storage ledger is in Appendix, Section AF. Prefix clocks        27

28   there begin after module loading and are labeled as body clocks; they are not             28

29   substituted for complete process receipts. The bounded cache and requested fill           29

30   tolerance do not satisfy the stronger retained-cover premises of Proposition 34, so       30

31   that proposition’s uniform packing count is not presented as an empirical result.         31

                     AJ.3. Independent conditional-moment and transport checks

27   (2, 1), (8, 1), (2, 2), (8, 2), and 64 state–action contexts per specification. Each context              27

28   has sixteen rational neurons. The original bilinear state drift, action exposure and                      28

29   uniform innovation projections are evaluated exactly. An independent checker                              29

30   clips the corresponding innovation rectangle and integrates the affine neuron                             30

31   through polygon area and first moments. It imports no production moment, cache                            31

32   or interval-query routine.                                                                                32

11                   AJ.4. Constructed continuations and attained qualification                            11

12                                                                                                         12
       The readout theorem is executed in four predeclared backward constructions in
13                                                                                                         13
     the original two-state, two-date, scalar-control economy. Two midpoint covers, with
14                                                                                                         14
     eight or sixteen cells per coordinate, are crossed with additive shallow ReLU and
15                                                                                                         15
     degree-four Bernstein features. Each basis has nine coefficients bounded by eight.
16                                                                                                         16
     At each date the future is the continuation actually constructed at the next date.
17                                                                                                         17
     Exact conditional moments supply thirty-three continuous feasible action values per
18                                                                                                         18

13   Width and Equiv. are exact-endpoint decisions at the separate predeclared thresholds, each 1/256. Posi- 13

14   tive zero-minus-controller means a gain from replacing the zero incumbent. Common-path dependence      14

15   is retained; the twenty intervals are not twenty independent samples.                                  15

16     The width and equivalence columns are different decisions. A precisely positive                      16

17   gain over the installed zero-investment controller fails equivalence because it iden- 17
18   tifies a material improvement, not because its computation failed. Conversely, a                       18

19   small point estimate with an interval spanning the chosen margin cannot justify                        19

20   equivalence. All decisions use unrounded rational endpoints. The printed inter- 20
21   vals are rounded outwards, so a reader need not infer a sign from a decimal that                       21

22   understates uncertainty.                                                                               22

23                                                                                                          23

24                                                                                                          24
                          AK.1. Acquisition, bin integration and arithmetic
25                                                                                                          25

26     Each path row contains two initial-state and two innovation bin indices. A forty- 26
27   bit bin represents its full original-law continuous cell. The implementation does not                  27

28   replace the conditional distribution within the bin by a midpoint. At each date, a                     28

29   rectangle enclosing the true state can intersect several acquired cells. The selected                  29

30   action range then includes every cell in that rectangle. Closed upper boundaries                       30

31   are included conservatively. The endpoint calculation propagates that entire range                     31

32   through the stage cost and next transition.                                                            32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                         113

1      A zero action and every stored constructed action are feasible on the full cell. In   1

2    particular, the action quantum is checked against the minimum state-dependent           2

3    capacity on the cell before costs are evaluated. State uncertainty is therefore not     3

4    silently removed by using the lower-cell center as the true state. Acquisition am- 4
5    biguity, continuous-bin variation and arithmetic expansion all contribute to the        5

6    retained numerical width. They can interact, so a decomposition into three sepa- 6
7    rately additive causal effects is not inferred from a single interval run. The exact    7

8    aggregate numerical term is reported alongside independent ambiguity counts and         8

9    the two arithmetic implementations’ endpoint differences.                               9

1    Unrounded components sum exactly to the recorded width. The numerical term is the mean retained full- 1
2    bin endpoint width, including acquisition and arithmetic effects; these are not asserted to be separately   2

3    additive causes. Variance terms use exact endpoint sample variances and upward rational square roots.       3

4      The first evaluator uses centered polynomial expressions with binary64 outward                            4

5    operations. The second expands the cost and transition polynomials and uses inte- 5
6    ger floor and ceiling on a 2−48 grid. It obtains acquired-action extrema by directly                        6

7    enumerating each distinct intersected rectangle rather than reading the first evalua- 7
8    tor’s range tables. Thus it shares neither floating arithmetic nor cost, transition or                      8

9    lookup routines with the production evaluator. The stored endpoints use the hull,                           9

10   not the more optimistic intersection, of these two evaluations. Intersection with the                       10

11   global primitive support is justified separately.                                                           11

12     Every new full-bin path is recomputed by both implementations during replay. 12
13   A further exact-rational midpoint calculation checks 512 predetermined rows for                             13

14   each policy, including both estimators. Synthetic boundary tests exercise bins at the                       14

15   state and innovation extremes and across acquired-cell boundaries. These point- 15
16   wise tests supplement the interval-propagation proof; they are not called full-bin                          16

17   verification by themselves. The historical aliasing counterexample and its corrected                        17

18   R67 execution remain unchanged.                                                                             18

19                                                                                                               19

20                                                                                                               20
                                        AK.2. Statistical allocation
21                                                                                                               21

22     There are 30 estimands and 60 one-sided endpoint events in one fixed family. The                          22

23   empirical-Bernstein radii use the common log allowance ten. The union bound is at                           23

24   most 120e−10 < 1/100, verified independently by a rational lower partial sum for                            24

25   e10 . All endpoint sums and sums of squares are integer calculations, so no floating                        25

26   sample-variance cancellation enters the confidence endpoints. Upward rational                               26

27   square roots enlarge rather than shrink the radii.                                                          27

28     The new family is distinct from the earlier R69 optimal-advantage-centered                                28

29   inference family. Combining conclusions from different families requires adding                             29

30   their error allowances. The R73 construction records contain no new independent                             30

31   cost rows, and replay of the R77 rows adds none. The sample stream, row count,                              31

32   bin precision, policy hashes, estimands and margin are fixed before execution. A                            32
                               NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                     115

1    failure of the declared width target is retained; it does not authorize more samples                    1

2    after inspecting the signs. No optional-stopping interpretation is assigned to this                     2

3    fixed-sample experiment.                                                                                3

21     The parent process. The complete five-controller service took 105.049 seconds                         21

22   from process launch through shutdown and log flushing. Exact statistical postpro- 22
23   cessing took 8.204 seconds; process peak resident memory was 189128 KiB. Each                           23

24   arithmetic implementation evaluated 2621440 complete policy trajectories, and the                       24

25   additional rational midpoint check covered 2560 predetermined policy trajectories. 25

27   observed where available, not claimed locked.                                                           27

28     The acquired-action rectangle tables are paid preprocessing, not a free neural                        28

29   operation. Their simple exhaustive construction is practical at the declared small                      29

30   covers but is not claimed to scale linearly in state dimension. The independent inte- 30
31   ger implementation deliberately pays for another complete calculation. The parent                       31

32   service also pays for reading and hashing controller records, both evaluations, exact                   32
     116

1    midpoint checks, statistical inference, compression, durable output and shutdown. 1
2    These costs qualify the returned policies; they do not retime the original backward             2

3    construction services or establish a new cold-start generator ranking.                          3

4      Corollary 22 provides a priced decision for every declared deployment count                   4

5    and monetary scale. In particular, the gain interval in Table N.I yields an entire              5

6    replacement-fee frontier, rather than a favorable isolated fee. The zero-investment             6

7    incumbent is a feasible installed policy in the original economy. An improvement                7

8    over it does not establish dominance over an adaptive Bellman search or over every              8

9    conventional basis. The matched feature comparison and the retained conventional- 9
10   query catalogue must be read separately.                                                        10

11     The experiment’s units are those of the theoretical investment loss. A currency               11

12   calibration, an empirically estimated installation fee and a deployment-frequency               12

13   model would be additional economic assumptions. The mathematical adoption                       13

14   interval states their role explicitly and remains valid for all such nonnegative prices         14

15   on the same coverage event. It therefore supports a conditional purchase calculation            15

16   without manufacturing an empirical welfare estimate.                                            16

       The preceding experiment qualifies the same complete controllers returned by
20                                                                                                   20
     the NBO fits. A decision maker must also choose between them. Marginal upper
21                                                                                                   21
     costs give the valid rule in Corollary 22, but discard the more precise paired observa-
22                                                                                                   22
     tions. We retain all available comparisons on their original simultaneous event. No
23                                                                                                   23
     additional simulation, independence assumption between estimators, or allocation
24                                                                                                   24
     of statistical error is required.
25                                                                                                   25
       Let Ji be the expected original-law cost of constructed controller i, for i = 1, . . . , q.
26                                                                                                   26
     Introduce an anchor θ0 = 0. Every reported marginal interval [ Li , Ui ] supplies
27                                                                                                   27
     constraints θi ≤ Ui and −θi ≤ − Li . Every reported paired interval [lij , uij ] supplies
28                                                                                                   28

29                                θi − θ j ≤ uij ,   θ j − θi ≤ −lij .                               29

31   Use both estimators when their intervals belong to the joint family of Theorem 21. 31
32   Denote the resulting bounded set of cost vectors by C . An edge a → b of weight c               32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                  117

1    represents θb − θ a ≤ c, and d( a, b) is its shortest-path distance. These are classical             1

2    difference constraints; the computational reduction is not a new graph algorithm                     2

3    (Floyd, 1962). Its role here is to turn paid NBO cost observations into an attainable,               3

4    price-uniform choice certificate.                                                                    4

7    C ̸= ∅. For every i, j, the tight upper bound implied by the retained inequalities is                7

                                         max(θi − θ j ) = d( j, i ).                              (70)
9                                         θ ∈C                                                            9

     For any declared resource charges w1 , . . . , wq ≥ 0, put

                                  Γi (w) = max {d( j, i ) + wi − w j }.                           (71)    12
                                             1≤ j ≤ q

     Then Γi (w) is exactly the largest possible cost regret of controller i over C . A minimizer bi of
15                                                                                                        15

16                                                                                                        16

20   The inequality holds for all charges on that same event, including charges chosen after              20

21   inspecting the intervals. Controller i is optimal for every cost vector in C exactly when            21

22   Γi (w) = 0.                                                                                          22

23                                                                                                        23

24     The proof in the Numerical and Development Appendix constructs a feasible cost                     24

25   vector attaining each bound. Sharpness refers to the retained interval inequalities,                 25

26   not to all information in the raw paths or to a statistically optimal procedure. The                 26

27   comparator is the qualified finite catalogue, not the original continuous Bellman op- 27
28   timum; the earlier original-optimum guarantees remain necessary. A negative con- 28
29   straint cycle means inconsistent inputs and stops selection rather than authorizing                  29

30   a silently repaired interval. Closure uses O(q3 ) rational additions and comparisons                 30

31   and O(q2 ) storage; rational bit complexity and all prior qualification work remain                  31

32   additional costs.                                                                                    32

       Let [l, u] be the closed interval for JReLU,8 − JReLU,16 . Suppose only the finer con-
14                                                                                              14

15                                                                                              15

22   The fine controller is universally optimal for f ≤ l; the coarse one is universally        22

23   optimal for f ≥ u. Between those thresholds, the regret-minimizing choice changes          23

24   at (l + u)/2. Here [l, u] ⊂ [0.0002277, 0.0011901] and the exact switch is approxi- 24
25   mately 0.00070894. Exact endpoints, all adjacent gaps and attaining cost vectors           25

26   are deposited. Transitive closure does not further tighten the paired bounds in this       26

27   particular catalogue; no such improvement is claimed.                                      27

28     For m deployments and monetary scale v > 0, replace f by the incremental                 28

29   total charge divided by mv. This gives a complete conditional fee region without           29

30   assigning a monetary value to normalized losses or inferring a deployment horizon          30

31   from the sample count. Acquiring finer information is an economic choice only after        31

32   its actual incremental costs are specified.                                                32

       P ROOF OF T HEOREM 35:                If θ ∈ C , summing its constraints along a path from j

     to i gives θi − θ j at most the path’s length. Hence it is at most d( j, i ). Feasibility rules
5                                                                                                      5
     out a negative cycle, because summing the constraints around a cycle has left side
6                                                                                                      6
     zero. Every vertex has a finite path to and from the anchor, by the finite marginal
7                                                                                                      7
     bounds. All shortest distances are therefore finite and d( j, j) = 0.

     For each edge a → b of weight c, the shortest-path triangle inequality gives d( j, b) ≤           12
                         ( j)     ( j)               ( j)

      ( j)  ( j)

     θi − θ j = d( j, i ). This proves (70). The same construction proves that a graph with            14

                        θi + wi − min(θ j + w j ) = max{θi − θ j + wi − w j }.

21                                                                                                     21
     over j. The preceding attainment result gives exactly (71). The term j = i is zero, so
22
     Γi (w) ≥ 0. For a maximizing j, the vector θ ( j) attains this regret. Minimizing over            22

23                                                                                                     23
     i proves the claimed deterministic minimax property. On the coverage event the
24                                                                                                     24
     true cost vector belongs to C ; inserting it proves the statistical conclusion without
25                                                                                                     25

26                                                                                                     26
     which establishes price-uniformity. Finally, nonnegative regret is identically zero
27                                                                                                     27
     exactly when its maximum is zero.                                                      Q.E.D.
