29     The ordinary Floyd recurrence evaluates the all-pairs distances with (q + 1)3 can- 29
30   didate relaxations. The implementation uses exact fractions, including the rational               30

31   confidence endpoints, rather than shortest paths through rounded printed entries. 31
32   Its independent cross-check runs a separate single-source Bellman–Ford recurrence                 32
     120

1    from every vertex. Each returned attaining vector is checked against every original                       1

2    edge and against every claimed attained difference. Negative self-loops and longer                        2

3    negative cycles are regression cases; an inconsistent graph cannot pass by resetting                      3

4    its diagonal.                                                                                             4

5                                                                                                              5

6                        AM.1. Refinement charges and the finite comparison                                    6

7                                                                                                              7
       Let the fine and coarse ReLU controllers be denoted by A and B, with JB − J A ∈
8                                                                                                              8
     [l, u], where these endpoints are the tight implications of C . Suppose all policies
9                                                                                                              9
     except A have a common charge and A has an additional charge f . The retained
10                                                                                                             10
     inequalities identify B as less costly than every other controller in the catalogue, so
11
     those controllers cannot be a best-cost comparator or a regret-minimizing choice. 11
12                                                                                                             12
     Equation (71) reduces to
13                                                                                                             13

14                      Γ A ( f ) = max{0, f − l },         Γ B ( f ) = max{0, u − f }.                        14

15                                                                                                             15
     For f ≤ l, only A can have positive strict cost advantages against all alternatives;
16                                                                                                             16
     its worst-case regret is zero. For f ≥ u, B has zero worst-case regret. On [l, u],
17                                                                                                             17
     the difference of the two regrets is 2 f − l − u, proving the switching formula. At a
18                                                                                                             18
     boundary, universal weak optimality, rather than strict optimality, is the appropriate
19                                                                                                             19
     statement. The independently checked exact fee probes include both boundaries,
20                                                                                                             20
     the switching point, and points outside the ambiguity interval.

8     A positive lower gain licenses any smaller incremental total charge in normalized cost units. For m      8
          deployments and monetary scale v, multiply the endpoints by mv. Construction, acquisition,
9                                                                                                              9
     qualification and installation charges remain explicit; seconds are not assigned an unobserved monetary
10                                                                                                             10
                                                     price.
11                                                                                                             11

12                                                                                                             12

13     Both original cash and conditional-estimator intervals qualify the same expected                        13

14   costs and are covered by the same thirty-estimand event. Intersecting their implica- 14
15   tions therefore needs no independence between estimators and no new multiplicity                          15

16   allowance. It does not convert two estimators on one stream into two independent                          16

17   datasets. The exact constraint ledger, closure, attaining cost vectors, zero-charge                       17

18   regrets and fee probes are in the machine-readable decision record.                                       18

19     All ten unordered contrasts and five marginal costs from each estimator enter the                       19

20   computation. In this particular dataset, transitive paths do not improve the direct                       20

21   paired endpoint intersections. The new decision result is consequently not presented                      21

22   as a new precision experiment. It uses the already purchased joint information to                         22

23   establish an identified catalogue ordering and sharp conditional choice regions. A                        23

24   separate exact test provides a graph in which transitivity strictly tightens an indirect                  24

25   comparison; that test is a mathematical regression, not another observation from                          25

26   the investment economy.                                                                                   26

27     The qualification process is part of the cost of learning these regions. Its histor- 27
28   ical complete service clock is retained, not replaced by the much shorter graph- 28
29   processing clock. Controller construction, acquisition, qualification, deployment                         29

30   and installation charges are distinct. A common sunk cost cancels from compar- 30
31   isons only under a decision problem in which it is indeed common and sunk. The                            31

32   arbitrary-charge theorem does not authorize dropping a method-specific cost. Like- 32
     122

1    wise, the catalogue includes the actually attained ReLU and Bernstein actors; it does               1

2    not include the different recursive adaptive-search controller merely because that                  2

3    controller was evaluated in an earlier experiment.                                                  3

4               AN.1. Installed subsets rather than free simultaneous availability             4

5      Suppose a decision maker serves m ≥ 1 deployments and may install a nonempty            5

6    subset S at total normalized cost K (S). Write Ki = K ({i }), and assume K (S) ≥ Ki       6

7    for every i ∈ S. The benchmark installs the best single controller, paying its own K j . 7
8    For p supported on S, the exact worst-case average regret is                              8

9                                      (                           )                           9
                                                       K (S) − K j
10                    RS ( p, w) = max ∑ pi Hji (w) +                .                  (72)   10
                                     j    i
                                                           m
11                                                                                             11

12   Indeed, the installation term is independent of θ, and the same attaining potential       12

13   proves the formula. The assumption on K makes the cost of an allocation at least          13

14   the weighted average of its corresponding singleton costs, and hence at least their       14

15   minimum; the regret is nonnegative. For each subset, (72) is a linear program on          15

16   ∆S . Enumerating the nonempty subsets and retaining the least optimum yields the          16

17   exact installation-and-allocation decision. It requires at most 2q − 1 programs and       17

18   is not advertised as dimension-free optimization. Lower bounds from the dual of           18

19   every subset, together with an attaining primal allocation for the chosen subset,         19

20   certify a global solution. A controller outside the installed subset remains a possible   20

21   oracle comparator and must not be removed from the rows.                                  21

22                                                                                             22

23
                    AN.2. Two-controller formula and extra setup threshold                     23

24     With only the fine controller charged f , put a = f − l and b = u − f . Within the      24

25   ambiguity region both are positive and a + b = u − l. If p is the fine share, the two     25

26   worst-case comparator regrets are pa and (1 − p)b. Their maximum is minimized at          26

27   their intersection, giving p = b/( a + b) and value ab/( a + b). Outside that region      27

28   a uniformly optimal pure controller has zero regret. If l = u, the gain is known          28

29   exactly: use the fine controller below l, the coarse one above it, and any allocation     29

30   at equality; no division by a zero width is needed.                                       30

31     When both controllers incur additional cost κ only for 0 < p < 1, their optimal         31

32   nontrivial allocation is unchanged and its value is ab/( a + b) + κ. The best pure        32
     124

1    value is min{ a, b}. Subtracting gives the exact saving min{ a, b}2 /( a + b) and proves   1

2    (53). Equality gives a tie. A positive extra cost can make the pure rule optimal;          2

3    the deployment analysis never assumes that loading or operating an additional              3

4    controller has zero cost. Formula (72) handles unequal singleton installation charges      4

5    as well.                                                                                   5

6                                                                                               6

7                                                                                               7
                        AN.3. A deterministic allocation to a finite cohort
8                                                                                               8

9      Random assignment is unnecessary when m homogeneous deployments with                     9

10   the same expected cost law can be assigned in advance. Deploy A on exactly k               10

11   tasks and B on the others, without choosing tasks according to unqualified outcome         11

12   information. The average expected cost is the mixture with p = k/m; independence           12

13   between tasks is not needed for this expectation identity. For l < f < u, a minimizing     13

14   integer k is one of ⌊mp∗A ⌋ and ⌈mp∗A ⌉. This follows because the piecewise-linear         14

15   regret decreases up to p∗A and increases afterwards. Nearest rounding also supplies        15

16                                                                                              16
                                                    max{ f − l, u − f }
17                         0 ≤ Vm ( f ) − V ( f ) ≤                     .                       17
                                                           2m
18                                                                                              18

19   An extra two-controller setup charge is included only for 0 < k < m; both pure             19

20   endpoints must then also be tested. Nonhomogeneous deployment laws require                 20

21   separately qualified cost vectors and are not covered by relabeling the existing           21

22   sample as a cohort.                                                                        22

23     The deposited implementation checks exact probability feasibility, all original cost     23

24   inequalities at each attaining potential, and equality between primal and dual pay- 24
25   offs. Two-policy formulas are checked against a separate exhaustive rational vertex        25

26   solver on the full five-controller catalogue. Finite-cohort choices are checked against    26

27   every integer allocation. The original thirty cost estimands, 524,288 evaluation rows,     27

28   fitted coefficients, scientific clocks and adverse selector comparisons remain un- 28
29   changed. The optimization tables contain no new simulation or timing evidence. 29
30   The sharpness claim is relative to the retained interval system; neither an exact          30

31   decision solution nor a positive finite comparison establishes a population-wide           31

32   neural ranking.                                                                            32

         APPENDIX AO: N EURAL B ELLMAN QUERIES WITHOUT A STATE LATTICE
15                                                                                                                 15

16     The preceding construction makes numerical error accountable, but a complete                                16

17   tensor reference can absorb the benefit of a fitted continuation. We therefore change                         17

18   the computational organization, not the economic problem. A Neural Bellman                                    18

19   selector proposes an action at the state actually acquired. A recursive interval query                        19

20   then bounds the original Bellman value at that state and checks the proposed                                  20

21   action against the entire continuous feasible interval. No conventional policy is                             21

22   precomputed at all states. The controller is the resulting callable algorithm, not the                        22

23   unverified proposal alone.                                                                                    23

24     The distinction matters for attribution. Learning may reduce the work needed                                24

25   to locate a useful action. It does not supply the inequalities used to certify that                           25

26   action. The adaptive conventional comparator receives the same inequalities and                               26

27   the same original-optimum target. The trained value-fit policies in the preceding                             27

28   study remain distinct from the action-query predictor introduced here.                                        28

29                                                                                                                 29

30

17     The finite-precision contract is explicit. The executed service uses the width guard,             17

18   a 512-point cap, and a 32-bit downward action lattice. A precision or cap failure                   18

19   withholds the floating certificate and invokes a separately counted exact-rational                  19

20   enumeration. This fallback constructs values only at the required descendants; it                   20

21   does not load a precomputed conventional policy. The rational fallback chooses                      21

22   dyadic action and quadrature counts until each cover or quadrature allowance is                     22

23   at most one quarter of the request, and requests child width at most one quarter                    23

24   divided by the discount. Provided δ > 8Λr 2−32 at every recursive level, its exact                  24

25   brackets have width at most three quarters of the request. This proves a finite                     25

26   return for every acquired state in the declared tasks, including states absent from                 26

27   the workload. Rational bit-operation cost is additional work and is not replaced                    27

28   by a unit-cost floating clock. The mathematical point budget is checked against                     28

29   the declared tolerance range; termination in an arbitrary floating implementation                   29

30   is not inferred merely from the real-arithmetic argument. Fitting convergence is                    30

31   unnecessary for validity: any finite prediction, and even its clipped replacement                   31

32   when numerically invalid, is only a proposed query.                                                 32

28                       AO.4. Work, prediction, and the economic object                      28

29                                                                                            29
       Let Ar (δ) be a bound on action endpoints at a query. Excluding training, a scalar
30                                                                                            30
     operation account obeys
31                                                                                            31

32            W1 (δ) = O(dA1 ),        Wr (δ) ≤ CdAr (δ) + Ar (δ)qr Wr−1 (δ/(4β)).            32
     130

1    The constants dGr , dMr are bounded independently of d, so the declared endpoint          1

2    and quadrature budgets need not grow exponentially with the state dimension. 2
3    Primitive evaluations still cost O(d). This removes the state lattice, not the hori- 3
4    zon or shock-tree cost. The executed vectorization batches a query’s innovation           4

5    descendants. Its largest live state batch is reported; it does not claim the smaller      5

6    memory of a fully streamed implementation. Increasing the horizon or the shock di- 6
7    mension may still be expensive. The new execution is scalar-control; the earlier full     7

8    two-control result is retained rather than relabeled as a query-service experiment.       8

9      A useful prediction can reduce the endpoint count or return its own action              9

10   from the initial partition. An inaccurate prediction can instead add work. This is a      10

11   prediction-dependent algorithm, not a theorem of neural dominance. Learned warm           11

12   starts have their own literature, including Sakaue and Oki (2023); their discrete- 12
13   convex setting differs from the original-law Bellman intervals here. Convex control       13

14   policies and learned optimization parameters are studied by Agrawal et al. (2020). 14
15   Our comparison identifies the extra proposal cost and the resulting query transcript      15

16   rather than claiming priority for learning a warm start.                                  16

17     The purchase is now a complete callable controller with a declared error contract. 17
18   Its training cost, every subsequent certified query, and its return-time cost belong to   18

19   the same service. A reduction in queried state storage is economically relevant for       19

20   repeated decisions only after these costs are counted. The original cost normalization    20

21   is maintained. Workload seconds and normalized expected-cost loss are reported            21

22   separately; a price per unit of computation can convert the former to the latter only     22

23   as an explicitly specified decision-maker parameter.                                      23

29     Proposition 28 separates witness accuracy from lower-certificate localization. Its      29

30   distance to an unknown minimizer is useful for analysis but is not a stopping             30

32   information already purchased. This gives a certificate before an additional Bell- 32

     certifies f ( a∆ ) − infa∈ A( x) f ( a) ≤ δ without another Bellman query. If the screen does not
21                                                                                                       21
     close, the protected refinement of Theorem 27 retains its validity and finite query bound.
22                                                                                                       22
     The complete acquired policy therefore retains the original-optimum account of Theorem 37.
23                                                                                                       23
       Equivalently, the union over partition intervals of the sublevel sets { a : C ( a) − L ≤ δ} is
24                                                                                                       24
     a computable certified action set. Each constituent is a quadratic sublevel interval intersected
25                                                                                                       25
     with its partition cell. Endpoints obtained numerically must be rounded inward, or the final
26                                                                                                       26
     action must be checked by (78).
27                                                                                                       27

28   Proof. See Supplement, Section 3.1.                                                                 28

29     The screen is shared by every comparator. In particular, minimizing the quadratic                 29

30   upper envelope is an available conventional proposal. Prediction is valuable only                   30

31   to the extent that its proposed location or evaluation cost improves the observed                   31

32   service. The executed neural rule screens its feasible prediction on the partition                  32
     132

1    interval containing it, even when that location is outside the protected part of the            1

2    currently critical interval. A failed screen does not authorize an unprotected split.           2

       The work loss can be trained without invoking a successful hidden-layer op-

13                                                                                             13

20                                                                                             20

9                                                                                                       9
       This is a fixed-feature neural construction, not a convergence assertion for unre-
10                                                                                                      10
     stricted nonconvex network fitting. The deposited rational implementation uses
11                                                                                                      11
     finite conditional-gradient steps and reports its achieved loss and dual gap. Its re-
12                                                                                                      12
     gression examples test the proposition; they are not new investment-policy observa-
13                                                                                                      13
     tions. The preceding R67 service comparison retained its preregistered action-label
14                                                                                                      14
     fits. The new comparison in Section Y separately executes this certificate-aware
15                                                                                                      15
     objective, with equal-information Bernstein and label-loss controls; no historical fit
16                                                                                                      16
     is relabeled as its minimizer. Prediction-dependent optimization has an established
17                                                                                                      17
     literature (Sakaue and Oki, 2023, Sambharya et al., 2023); the additional object here
18                                                                                                      18
     is a computable original-law Bellman certificate that can license an action before
19                                                                                                      19
     another query and supplies a directly auditable training criterion.

       The absence of a state lattice does not remove action or innovation dimensions. We

26                                                                                                      26
     model and its experiments are unchanged.

10   a lower minorant of the true Bellman objective. Taking the smallest valid triangle lower           10

11   endpoint and the best feasible-action upper endpoint gives a lower–upper certificate for           11

12   the full continuous simplex. Include the omitted capacity strip and actual action-rounding         12

13   allowances. Returning when this gap is at most δ gives the same acquired-policy loss               13

14   recursion as Theorem 37.                                                                           14

                                 AR.2. First-disagreement extension
18                                                                                                     18

19
       Let two finite-horizon policies have a common terminal cost and common tran- 19

20
     sition primitives. Suppose they choose the same action outside measurable sets                    20

21
     Et at each date. Assume a common-innovation realization of the actual kernels is                  21

22
     available, so trajectories agree while states and actions agree. Let the tail cash cost           22

23
     from date t lie in [mt , Mt ] under either policy, uniformly over feasible paths, and             23

24
     write Ht = Mt − mt . Put b0 = 1 and bt = ∏ j<t β j .                                              24

25                                                                                                     25
       P ROPOSITION 42—First-disagreement cost bound: If π
                                                         b is the reference policy and
26                                                                                                     26
     Dt is the event that the coupled policies first choose different actions at date t, then
27                                                                                                     27
                                     T −1                 T −1
28                     | J π − J πb | ≤ ∑ bt Ht Pr( Dt ) ≤ ∑ bt Ht Pr( Xt ∈ Et ).               (89)   28
                                     t =0                 t =0       π
                                                                     b
29                                                                                                     29

30   The first events are disjoint. Any independent bound on the entire cash-cost difference may       30

31   be intersected with this bound. To apply it to an optimization class, the action agreement        31

32   and bad sets must hold uniformly for every policy in that class.                                  32
     138

1      P ROOF : On Dt , the histories, states and costs before t coincide. The absolute          1

2    difference of the remaining discounted costs is at most bt Ht . On the event of             2

3    no disagreement the costs coincide throughout. Summing over the disjoint first- 3
4    disagreement events and taking expectations proves the first inequality. Up to the          4

5    first disagreement, the common state is the state of the reference policy driven by         5

6    the same innovations. A first disagreement at date t requires this state to lie in Et ,     6

7    proving the second inequality.                                                   Q.E.D.     7

8                                                                                                8
       This proposition is a cost-transfer identity, not a substitute for constructing longer-
9                                                                                                9
     horizon stable sublevels. In particular, changing a terminal readout changes labels
10                                                                                               10
     upstream. Independently small optimization gaps at different fitted label prob-
11                                                                                               11
     lems do not automatically establish the uniform action-agreement premise. In the
12                                                                                               12
     executed two-date experiment the last-date actor depends only on the common
13                                                                                               13
     terminal payoff; Theorem 12 therefore needs the first-date switch problems alone.
14                                                                                               14

15                                                                                               15
                          AR.3. Confidence and economic interpretation
16                                                                                               16

17     The original cost event concerns a finite list of frozen attained policies. On that       17

18   event all deterministic stability enlargements are valid simultaneously, including          18

19   for a coefficient vector selected after seeing the records but still belonging to a         19

20   certified fixed-label sublevel. The probability statement does not change because           20

21   the optimizer class is uncountable. A new unqualified model or newly fitted label           21

22   problem is not covered by that argument.                                                    22

23     A failed action-stability certificate is a numerical statement about the available        23

24   lower bound, not a witness of policy variation. Conversely, policy invariance of            24

25   an exact optimization class does not assert that an arbitrary practical optimizer           25

26   reaches that class. The deposited feasible iterate and dual gap describe what the           26

27   construction attained; the switch lower bounds describe what every member of a              27

28   stated sublevel must do. These are distinct quantifiers, and neither is inferred from       28

29   a collection of fitting seeds.                                                              29

30     For the two calibrated n = 16 controllers let [l, u] be the positive interval for         30

31   ReLU cost minus Bernstein cost. Let κ be the additional per-deployment charge for           31

32   Bernstein, after all actual installation and computation charges have been converted        32
                                NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                     139

1    into the same normalized cost units. The corresponding net difference is [l − κ, u − κ ]. 1
2    Bernstein is strictly less costly for every κ < l; ReLU is strictly less costly for every                 2

3    κ > u; neither strict ordering is certified in the intermediate region. This is an exact                  3

4    conditional price region, not a monetary calibration. It remains valid for the fully                      4

5    action-stable optimizer classes, and it does not use construction times with different                    5

6    service boundaries as interchangeable monetary prices.                                                    6

7                                                                                                              7

       The following tables report both estimators, all marginal costs, paired contrasts
10                                                                                                             10
     and predeclared interactions. A paired-width target is not a marginal-cost target.
11                                                                                                             11
     The two estimators share the same underlying rows and target the same expected
12                                                                                                             12
     costs; no independent sample size is gained by computing both. The interval width
13                                                                                                             13
     is decomposed into the two endpoint sampling-variance allowances, the bounded-
14                                                                                                             14
     support term and the mean numerical enclosure width. Arithmetic, continuous-bin
15                                                                                                             15
     width, and any acquisition branching enter the latter through actual endpoint
16                                                                                                             16
     propagation. The implementation does not claim a uniquely identifiable additive
17                                                                                                             17
     attribution of that combined numerical width to each intermediate operation.

         APPENDIX AT: A CTIONWISE ECONOMIC TRANSFER AND ITS COMPLETE
27                                                                                            27
                                    NUMERICAL ACCOUNT
28                                                                                            28

29     Theorem 13 separates two numerical tasks. The action-switch dual identifies            29

30   actions that cannot be selected by a qualified readout. Original-law integration         30

31   prices the actions not excluded by that dual. Neither calculation substitutes a fitted   31

32   critic for the cost of the implemented continuation policy.                              32
     144

1                    AT.1. Conditional contrasts from the original primitives                      1

2                                                                                                  2
       For a fixed initial cell C, reference action a, and alternative action b, couple
3                                                                                                  3
     the two trajectories with the same first innovation W. Write Y b , Y a for their next
4                                                                                                  4
     states and B = τ1 (Y b ), A = τ1 (Y a ) for the fixed last-date actor. The action pair need
5                                                                                                  5
     not agree on the two next states. Let m(y, d) denote the original transition with
6                                                                                                  6
     innovation zero and action d. Its opposite-signed last innovation leaves the state
7                                                                                                  7
     sum unchanged, so the shortage term in the terminal cost is unaffected. The terminal
8                                                                                                  8
     quadratic terms give
9                                                                                                  9

                                                                                          5
      E[ g(m(y, d) + (W ′ , −W ′ )) | y, d] = g(m(y, d)) + 5E[(W ′ )2 ] = g(m(y, d)) +
10                                                                                                 10
                                                                                              .
11                                                                                       3072      11
                                                                                            (90)
12                                                                                                 12
     The correction is the same for both trajectories and cancels in their difference. It
13                                                                                                 13
     cannot be omitted from an individual conditional cost. Feasibility and the inherited
14                                                                                                 14
     transition bounds ensure that the actual states remain in [0, 1]2 ; intersecting an
15                                                                                                 15
     enclosing box with this domain does not clip the economic transition law.
16                                                                                                 16
       Consequently the paired conditional cash cost is
17                                                                                                 17

18                    ZCb ( X, W ) = c0 ( X, b) − c0 ( X, a)                                       18

                                        15

                                      + {c1 (Y b , B) − c1 (Y a , A)}                              20
                                        16

                                      +     { g(m(Y b , B)) − g(m(Y a , A))}.               (91)
22                                      256                                                        22

     Its expectation conditional on C is ∆C (b). The first difference cancels the common           23

24
     state cost exactly. The remaining terms retain a separately propagated state differ- 24
25                                                                                                 25
     ence rather than subtracting independently widened state boxes.
26
       For example, δ = Y b − Y a = ((b − a)/2, (b − a)/4). The bilinear difference satisfies      26

27                                                                                                 27

28                                 Y1b Y2b − Y1a Y2a = δ1 Y2b + Y1a δ2 .                           28

29                                                                                                 29

30   This identity gives the difference of the next mean states, including the separate            30

31   term B − A. The square identity u2 − v2 = (u − v)(u + v) and the quartic identity             31

32   u4 − v4 = (u − v)(u + v)(u2 + v2 ) preserve analogous cancellation in costs. For the          32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                  145

1    shortage term, whenever an interval encloses d = u − v,                                              1

                        u+ − v+ ∈ ([u]+ − [v]+ ) ∩ [min{0, d}, max{0, d}].
3                                                                                                         3

4
     Multiplication by an enclosure of u+ + v+ encloses the difference of their squares. 4
5                                                                                                         5
     An empty intersection stops verification.

7      P ROPOSITION 43—Complete-bin enclosure: Partition each initial cell into r2 equal                  7

8    rectangles and the first innovation into q equal bins, with dyadic r, q. Evaluate (91) on            8

9    every complete subbox by outward interval operations. On each next-state rectangle, enclose          9

10   the fixed actor by the minimum and maximum of its action indices over every intersected              10

11   acquired cell, including boundary cells. Let [ls , us ] be the resulting paired interval on subbox   11

12   s, intersected with the valid signed support [− H, H ]. Then                                         12

13                                                                                                        13
                                      1                  1
                                      2 ∑ s
14
                                           l ≤ ∆C (b ) ≤ 2 ∑ us .                                         14
                                     r q s              r q s
15                                                                                                        15
     The hull of two independently valid interval implementations is valid. Intersecting indepen-
16                                                                                                        16
     dently valid integrated bands across refinement levels is valid as well.
17                                                                                                        17

18                                                                                                        18
       P ROOF : The transition and centered identities are algebraic identities for every
19                                                                                                        19
     state and innovation in the subbox. The rectangle extrema contain the actual actor
20                                                                                                        20
     action even when the rectangle crosses an actor discontinuity. Interval inclusion,
21                                                                                                        21
     propagated through each arithmetic operation and the valid intersections, therefore
22                                                                                                        22
     contains (91) pointwise. Equation (90) accounts for the remaining innovation under
23                                                                                                        23
     its actual continuous marginal law. All subboxes have exact conditional probability
24                                                                                                        24
     1/(r2 q), so integration preserves the endpoint inequalities. Taking a hull preserves
25
     inclusion, as does intersecting two bands that separately contain the same integral. 25
26                                                                                                        26
                                                                                               Q.E.D.
27                                                                                                        27

28     The two deposited implementations do not import each other’s interval, transi- 28
29   tion, cost, partition or actor routines. The binary64 implementation uses outward                    29

30   next-representable-number operations and a precomputed rectangle-extremum                            30

31   table. The integer implementation uses Python-integer endpoints in units 2−48 , 31
32   directed integer division, expanded cost polynomials and direct enumeration of                       32
     146

1    distinct acquisition rectangles. Their endpoint streams must overlap on every sub- 1
2    box. A separately written rational trajectory calculation checks four deterministic                  2

3    midpoint trajectories per action and rung. Those checks are regressions; the proof                   3

4    concerns every point in each box, not only its midpoint.                                             4

5      The complete records retain exact integer endpoint sums, their common denomi- 5
6    nator, the digest of all four independently computed endpoint streams, the largest                   6

7    endpoint discrepancy and the number of processed boxes. They do not claim to                         7

8    deposit an additional random sample or to count the two arithmetic implementa- 8
9    tions as independent statistical observations. Full replay recomputes those endpoint                 9

10   streams, not only the saved sums.                                                                    10

11                                                                                                        11

12                           AT.2. Refinement and resource requirements                                   12

13     P ROPOSITION 44—Refinement with a discontinuous acquired actor: In the origi- 13
14   nal two-date model, fix the finite acquired actor, initial cell and feasible first-action pair. In   14

15   exact interval arithmetic, the integrated width in Proposition 43 tends to zero as r, q → ∞          15

16   along dyadic partitions. The same conclusion holds with outward arithmetic whose uni- 16
17   form propagated padding tends to zero. The executed fixed-precision calculation retains its          17

18   nonzero numerical width rather than invoking this limiting statement.                                18

19     If P alternatives remain at the largest qualification tolerance, a level uses exactly Pr2 q        19

20   paired subboxes in each implementation. Its binary64 actor tables occupy O(n4 ) entries and          20

21   the direct preprocessing used here takes at most O(n6 ) operations. After that preprocessing         21

22   each rectangle query is constant work. The independent enumerator instead stores the n2              22

23   actor indices, groups queried rectangles by sorting their codes, and can inspect up to n2            23

24   indices per distinct rectangle. Its conservative worst-case work is therefore                        24

25                                                                                                      25

26                                     O Pr2 q [n2 + log(r2 q)] .                                         26

27                                                                                                        27
     Processing one alternative at a time uses O(n4 + r2 q) numerical working storage, apart
28                                                                                                        28
     from retained integrated records.
29                                                                                                        29

30     P ROOF : For a fixed first action, each next-state coordinate is a polynomial of                   30

31   the initial state plus either W or −W. For any terminal acquisition boundary Yj =                    31

32   k/n, its preimage has conditional innovation measure zero for every initial state,                   32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                              147

1    because the coefficient of W is nonzero. There are finitely many boundaries and two           1

2    coupled trajectories. Fubini’s theorem therefore gives a measure-zero union of their          2

3    preimages in the integration domain.                                                          3

4      At every other interior point, sufficiently fine boxes intersect only the fixed             4

5    actor cell containing each next state. On such boxes, (91) is a finite composition of         5

6    continuous polynomial and positive-part operations with constant actor actions. 6
7    Its exact interval width tends to zero. The support intersection bounds all widths            7

8    by 2H. Boundary sets of the initial partition also have measure zero. Dominated               8

9    convergence now gives convergence of the integrated width. Vanishing outward                  9

10   padding preserves the argument. It does not justify removing the actual fixed- 10
11   precision remainder from a finite calculation.                                                11

12     There are r2 q subboxes per action and P retained actions. The direct binary64              12

13   precomputation enumerates O(n4 ) rectangles and scans at most n2 entries each. A              13

14   stored rectangle extremum then uses its four indices. For m = r2 q boxes, the inde- 14
15   pendent algorithm spends O(m log m) work grouping codes and at most O(mn2 )                   15

16   work enumerating their actor indices. Finally, keeping only one alternative’s in- 16
17   terval arrays at a time gives the stated working storage. Summing the displayed               17

18   counts over every executed rung, rather than only the last one, gives the complete            18

19   refinement work.                                                                   Q.E.D.     19

20                                                                                                 20
       These are counts for the actual two-date procedure. They are not a reduction of
21                                                                                                 21
     the retained vector recursion’s horizon growth, and table bytes are not substituted
22                                                                                                 22
     for process peak memory. The completed R83 recovery records its verification
23                                                                                                 23
     clock and peak resident memory separately from the interrupted R82 phase clocks.
24                                                                                                 24
     Development pilots informed the dyadic resolution before the source freeze; the
25                                                                                                 25
     computation is described as deterministic verification rather than a prospectively
26                                                                                                 26
     selected new economic experiment.
27                                                                                                 27

28                                                                                                 28
                    AT.3. A classwide adoption decision on the original event
29                                                                                                 29
                                                          πj
30     Let the signed optimizer-sublevel interval for J        − J πk be [ L, U ] after applying   30

31   (26). If deployment of class k rather than class j has an additional total charge τ, the      31

32   net advantage of k belongs to [ L − τ, U − τ ] for every allowed pair of readouts on the      32
     148

1    same original event. Thus τ < L supports k uniformly and τ > U supports the oppo- 1
2    site decision uniformly. The intermediate region remains unresolved. This statement       2

3    prices construction, verification and installation only when their conversion into        3

4    economic cost units is explicitly supplied. A measured second of verification is not      4

5    itself such a price.                                                                      5

6      The extension uses a deterministic outer set of possible acquired actions. It permits   6

7    the qualification tolerance or the comparison to be selected after observing the          7

8    original simultaneous cost event, provided the original attained controllers and          8

9    event are not changed and the stated deterministic enclosures hold. There is no new       9

10   probability expenditure over an uncountable set of readouts. Conversely, a newly          10

11   fitted hidden-feature model, a changed terminal actor, altered transition law or new      11

12   statistical estimand is not covered merely because its displayed objective resembles      12

13   the one here.                                                                             13

14                                                                                             14

15                              AT.4. Complete refinement catalogue                            15

16     The tables retain all four calibrated constructions, all three objective allowances     16

17   and all four refinement rungs. ReLU and Bernstein are each evaluated at both              17

18
     resolutions, including the coarse ReLU construction whose exact-optimizer action          18
     certificate remains partly unresolved. The actionwise radii are compared with their
19                                                                                             19
     binary uncertified-mass bounds. Every calibrated pair and both own-controller
20                                                                                             20
     references are retained in the machine-readable records; no unresolved or adverse
21   entry is removed.                                                                         21

1              APPENDIX AU: E XACT INVERSION , BOUNDARY CHECKS AND                                1

2                               SOURCE - QUALIFIED RECOVERY                                       2

3                                                                                                 3
                     AU.1. A finite algorithm for the entire tolerance interval
4                                                                                                 4
       For one construction, let P be the number of integrated nonreference actions and
5                                                                                                 5
     N the number of acquisition cells. Sort the action events by DCb − U. Events with
6                                                                                                 6
     nonpositive threshold are active at zero. Maintain the lowest lower endpoint and
7                                                                                                 7
     highest upper endpoint on each cell, initially including the exact reference interval
8                                                                                                 8
     [0, 0]. When an event is activated, update those two extrema and their probability-
9                                                                                                 9
     weighted sums. Equal thresholds are processed together. The resulting records
10                                                                                                10
     contain every knot, the two class endpoints, the number of admitted alternatives,
11                                                                                                11
     and the open or closed status of the stratum’s right endpoint.
12                                                                                                12

13
       P ROPOSITION 45—Exact finite inversion: Suppose the switch bounds, attained ob- 13
14                                                                                                14
     jectives, cell masses and integrated endpoints are rational. The complete class band is
15                                                                                                15
     computed with O( P log P + N ) comparisons and arithmetic operations after integration,
16
     using O( P + N ) storage. Let K j , Kk denote the numbers of tolerance strata for two con- 16
17                                                                                                17
     structions. For a fixed replacement charge or equivalence margin, the sufficient region in
18                                                                                                18
     Theorem 14 can be represented by at most K j horizontal strips and found using at most
19                                                                                                19
     K j + Kk endpoint-predicate evaluations. The same bound applies to a certified ordering in
20                                                                                                20
     the opposite direction.
21                                                                                                21

22     P ROOF : Sorting costs O( P log P) comparisons. Each event changes at most two             22

23   cell extrema and two weighted sums; reference initialization takes O( N ) work. 23
24   Storing events, cells and resulting strata uses the stated memory. These counts              24

25   exclude the already paid action integration, and rational bit complexity depends on          25

26   the input denominators.                                                                      26

27     Order both stratum lists from smaller to larger tolerance. Start at the largest            27

28   second-coordinate stratum. For the current first-coordinate stratum, decrease the            28

29   second index until the desired endpoint test holds or the index is exhausted. Record         29

30   the resulting maximal admissible second-coordinate prefix. Move to the next first- 30
31   coordinate stratum and continue from the same second index. Monotonicity implies             31

32   that this index never needs to increase. Each successful test advances the first             32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                           155

1    coordinate, and each failed test decreases the second coordinate. There are at most       1

2    K j + Kk tests. Each recorded strip has the first stratum’s exact endpoint convention     2

3    and the upper boundary convention of its last admitted second stratum. Exhaustion         3

4    leaves no later feasible strip. Thus the algorithm returns precisely the largest region   4

5    certified by these fixed endpoint tests, not merely an inner grid of isolated tolerance   5

6    points.                                                                         Q.E.D.    6

7                                                                                              7
       The implementation also enumerates the finite product grid independently and
8                                                                                              8
     checks every returned and excluded stratum pair. Separately, it rebuilds the possible-
9                                                                                              9
     action set directly from the original switch predicates at every knot, every adjacent-
10                                                                                             10
     stratum midpoint and the final endpoint. This check does not reuse the incremental
11                                                                                             11
     cell-extremum update. It includes negative and repeated switch thresholds, a switch
12                                                                                             12
     at the maximum allowed tolerance, and strict sign or equivalence tests at equality.
13                                                                                             13
       An economic margin and an objective excess are not interchangeable numbers.
14                                                                                             14
     The plotted or tabulated tolerance region is an implication from one to the other
15                                                                                             15
     for the specified construction. It neither assumes that the nonlinear ReLU and
16                                                                                             16
     Bernstein classes coincide nor attributes a larger allowable objective excess to
17                                                                                             17
     universally better numerical conditioning. A valid stopping certificate is sufficient
18                                                                                             18
     for an individual output; it is not a probability that a randomly initialized algorithm
19                                                                                             19
     attains that certificate within a given time.
20                                                                                             20

21                                                                                             21
                         AU.2. Finite numerical and statistical domains
22                                                                                             22

23     The integrated catalogue is complete up to e j = 1/4096. Actions whose switch           23

24   lower bound exceeds Uj + e j cannot be selected within that objective sublevel and        24

25   require no economic integration for this result. The calculation does not extrapolate     25

26   the band beyond e j . Each class has its original labels, feature map, coefficient box,   26

27   acquisition grid, robust action menu and fixed terminal actor. Changing any of these      27

28   objects requires new qualification and, where relevant, a new integration account.        28

29     There is no new empirical-Bernstein event in this inversion. The original nine- 29
30   controller simultaneous event supplies the attained marginal and paired costs. 30
31   The actionwise bounds extend it deterministically to all admissible readouts and          31

32   tolerances. A data-selected comparison or stopping tolerance is covered because           32
     156

1    the event already implies every stated comparison. An unlisted controller or an           1

2    additional statistical estimand does not acquire coverage merely by receiving a           2

3    similar display name.                                                                     3

4      For a common tolerance the sufficient region is an initial interval, possibly empty     4

5    or the entire declared domain. When a switch destroys the endpoint test, its exact        5

6    knot is the supremum and is excluded. Decimal summaries must therefore not be             6

7    used as executable stopping thresholds. The deposited rational boundaries and             7

8    their inclusion flags are authoritative. A deployment fee is handled by comparing         8

9    the lower gain endpoint with the supplied fee, not by converting a measured second        9

10   into a monetary amount without a declared exchange rate.                                  10

11                                                                                             11

12                       AU.3. Recovery of the incomplete predecessor                          12

13     The earlier actionwise run completed the four model files but stopped while             13

14   looking up a legacy-controller contrast. Its string operation removed the prefix          14

15   calibrated- without restoring legacy-. The resulting name was absent from                 15

16   the frozen policy roster. The failure log, source freeze and four completed model         16

17   files are retained unchanged. The present recovery reproduces that exception as a         17

18   negative regression and resolves the comparison through an explicit roster-checked        18

19   identity map, with construction and callable-actor hashes bound to the existing cost      19

20   record.                                                                                   20

21     The source-qualified replay recomputes every paired subbox with both original           21

22   independent arithmetic implementations and requires exact agreement of the re- 22
23   sulting model records. It then computes the previously missing 72 paired and 24           23

24   own-controller transfers and the new full tolerance regions. It does not relabel the      24

25   failed run as successful, invent its missing final service clock, refit a controller or   25

26   add a cost-observation row. The old logged integration-phase durations and the            26

27   new complete replay durations are reported separately. All inherited full-bin cost        27

28   calculations and exact action-switch duals remain part of the release verification.       28

29                                                                                             29

30                         AU.4. Complete tolerance-frontier results                           30

31     The six calibrated pairs each receive three endpoint tests: positive difference,        31

32   negative difference and equivalence within 1/256. Empty regions and the coarse            32

     APPENDIX AV: E CONOMIC COMPARISONS BEYOND EXACT ACTION STABILITY
10                                                                                                            10

11     We apply Theorem 13 to the same four calibrated constructions in Section O. 11
12   No controller is refitted, and the original-law cost observations are unchanged. 12
13   The additional computation evaluates the economic consequences of actions not                            13

14   excluded by the exact optimization barriers. It therefore answers a different relia- 14
15   bility question from whether one numerical fitting attempt returned its displayed                        15

16   coefficient vector.                                                                                      16

17     The objective allowances are 0, 1/65536 and 1/4096. At the largest allowance,                          17

18   the complete 20,480-switch catalogue leaves 976 alternative actions for economic                         18

19   evaluation: 832 for coarse ReLU, nine for coarse Bernstein, 78 for fine ReLU                             19

20   and 57 for fine Bernstein. Each is evaluated on all four dyadic levels (r, q) =                          20

21   (2, 8), (4, 16), (8, 32), (16, 64), where r subdivides each initial-cell coordinate and                  21

22   q subdivides the first innovation. No action is discarded because its cost effect is                     22

23   adverse or its switch bound is uninformative. Both whole-bin arithmetic implemen- 23
24   tations evaluate every level, and the final innovation retains its exact conditional                     24

25   expectation.                                                                                             25

26     At the standard objective allowance 2−16 , the ReLU-minus-Bernstein class                              26

27   interval is [0.000496, 0.002503], compared with the binary-mass interval                                 27

28   [−0.421558, 0.424526]. The positive actionwise interval applies uniformly to                             28

29   the stated approximate-optimizer classes. No new attained-policy sample is used.                         29

30     The comparison concerns all readouts in the two attained-objective sublevels,                          30

31   including every 1/65536-optimal readout of their respective fixed-label problems. 31
32   It does not require the same fitted coefficients or complete action agreement. By                        32
                               NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                       159

8    ReLU minus Bernstein costs for all qualified readout pairs with fixed labels and terminal actors. Every   8
          earlier dyadic level is retained. Cost endpoints are rounded outward; allowances are exact.
9                                                                                                              9

10                                                                                                             10

11   contrast, an arbitrary fitting attempt with no verified objective guarantee does not                      11

12   enter the class merely because it returns a feasible policy. The original fallback and                    12

13   fitting-warning results retain their separate interpretation.                                             13

14     For an additional Bernstein deployment charge τ, subtracting τ from the two                             14

15   endpoints gives the uniform net-benefit interval for choosing Bernstein over ReLU. 15
16   A charge below a positive lower endpoint is covered by the classwide policy-cost                          16

17   saving; a charge above the upper endpoint favors the opposite decision. The inter- 17
18   mediate region is unresolved. The comparison uses normalized model costs and                              18

19   an explicitly supplied charge, rather than an invented processor price or empirical                       19

20   installation calibration.                                                                                 20

21     The complete numerical appendix includes all 48 model–allowance–level bands,                            21

22   all 72 pairwise transfers and all 24 final-level comparisons with the zero or cor- 22
23   responding legacy controller. It retains the coarse ReLU sublevels and the larger- 23
24   allowance outcomes alongside the fine-grid comparison. The cellwise extrema are                           24

25   outer bounds: separately possible actions need not be jointly selectable by a single                      25

26   coefficient vector. Their residual width is not characterized as a population fitting                     26

27   error.                                                                                                    27

28     The completed recovery reevaluates 18,270,720 paired boxes per arithmetic imple- 28
29   mentation and verifies 15,616 exact rational midpoint differences. Its independently                      29

30   recorded time through the verification calculations is 515.246 seconds; peak resident                     30

31   memory is 88,652 KiB. The failed R82 run retains its own partial integration-phase                        31

32   clocks and exception record. This recovery is not a new policy-cost sample. The                           32
     160

1    completed R83 recovery accounts for all refinement levels, both arithmetic im- 1
2    plementations and rational checks; its output-accounting boundary is recorded                   2

3    explicitly. Subsequent replay has separate clocks. The interrupted R82 phase clocks             3

4    are preserved, not replaced or promoted to a missing complete-service clock. The                4

5    computation adds neither an independent cost-observation row nor a new success- 5
6    frequency sample. Development pilots informed the deterministic refinement reso- 6
7    lution before its source freeze; it is not presented as a newly randomized prospective          7

8    economic experiment. The unchanged prospective R80 statistical family and the                   8

9    new deterministic integration together support the classwide statements on their                9

10   original event.                                                                                 10

12     APPENDIX AW: W INNER AND COMMON - READOUT SUPPORT CERTIFICATES                                12

13     The coefficient vector is shared across the acquisition cells. This appendix gives            13

14   the exact certificates used to retain that restriction in the main economic bound. All          14

15   coefficients in the executed fixed-feature problem are rational. Floating optimization          15

16   proposes multipliers; the formulas below, evaluated in rational arithmetic, decide              16

17   whether those proposals are admissible.                                                         17

18                                                                                                   18

19                         AW.1. A full-menu lower qualification bound                               19

       Write α = 2ρ, let ϕi be the frozen feature rows, and let r j be the rows defining the

     calibrated derivative bound. The objective is
