                                                j − ξ j )r j + ∑ νk dk ,
                                                      −                                              29

30                           i                 j                 k                                   30

31                     D = ∑ pi yi+ − ∑ qi yi− + αG + ∑ νk ck − ∑ Bm |sm |.                          31

32                           i          i                 k          m                               32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                           161

1    Then D is a lower bound on the infimum of F over the event and the coefficient box. 1
2      To see this, each of the first two maxima dominates its corresponding probability- 2
3    weighted average. The derivative term dominates the signed derivative combination          3

4    because its total nonnegative multiplier mass is at most α. For a feasible event,          4

5    adding ∑k νk (dT
                    k θ + ck ) cannot increase the resulting lower expression. Minimizing       5

6    its linear part over |θm | ≤ Bm gives − ∑m Bm |sm |. This proves the bound without         6

7    strong duality, a constraint qualification or a successful numerical minimizer. Taking     7

8    the maximum with an inherited lower bound preserves validity.                              8

9      The numerical implementation scales the event rows before proposing multipliers. 9
10   It then undoes that scaling exactly. Probability multipliers are projected to a rational   10

11   simplex; derivative multipliers are made nonnegative and rescaled only when their          11

12   total mass exceeds the exact derivative budget. Event multipliers are nonnega- 12
13   tive rationals obtained from the proposed binary values and the exact row scales. 13
14   The independent checker reconstructs every feature integral from the separate an- 14
15   tiderivative implementation, every menu comparison, and the final coefficient-box          15

16   support value. It does not call the production optimizer or production certificate         16

17   routine.                                                                                   17

18                                                                                              18

19                        AW.2. Empty events require a separate proof                           19

20     For nonnegative weights λk , set                                                         20

21                                                                                              21

22                              η = ∑ λk ck − ∑ Bm ∑ λk dkm .                                   22
                                     k         m       k
23                                                                                              23

24   If η > 0, the event is empty. Indeed, the left side is the minimum over the box of         24

25   ∑ k λ k ( dT
                k θ + ck ), whereas an event-feasible point would make this sum nonpositive. 25
26   The executed slack problem additionally imposes ∑k λk ≤ 1 when proposing the               26

27   weights; this normalization is convenient but is not needed for the implication once       27

28   strict positivity has been checked exactly.                                                28

29     An infeasible numerical solver status is not a proof of η > 0. When no positive          29

30   exact separation is obtained, the event receives only its valid ordinary objective         30

31   lower bound and remains possible whenever that bound does not exceed the                   31

32   stated sublevel. The numerical catalogue preserves every such solver status. In the        32
     162

1    executed study, all 104 nonsuccess statuses correspond to positive independently          1

2    checked separation certificates, rather than to silently discarded observations or        2

3    failed fitting attempts.                                                                  3

4                                                                                              4

5                           AW.3. Exact economic assignment duals                              5

6      For a chosen tolerance, the pair-conflict relaxation has nonnegative variables          6

7    zi , one unit of mass per acquisition cell, and zi + z j ≤ 1 for each verified incom- 7
8    patible pair. Given nonnegative rational pair multipliers λij and weights wi , the        8

9    construction                                                                              9
                                                                    
10                                                                                           10

11                        αC = max 0, max wi −
                                   i in C            ∑          λ ij
                                                                                              11
                                                j:{i,j} is a cut
                                                                        
12                                                                                             12

13   satisfies every assignment-dual inequality. Its value is ∑C αC + ∑{i,j} λij . The zero    13

14   reference action explains why nonnegative αC is a harmless conservative choice. 14
15   The lower cost endpoint uses the negative conditional lower-cost weights and has a        15

16   separately verified dual.                                                                 16

17     The pair multipliers are rounded to nonnegative dyadic rationals. Every remain- 17
18   ing residual is paid by the exact cell maximum; no floating primal optimum is used        18

19   as a certified upper bound. Setting all pair multipliers to zero recovers the full-menu   19

20   cellwise extrema. Whenever a proposed dual is less useful, that valid fallback is         20

21   retained. The independent checker verifies every variable’s covering inequality, all      21

22   signs, all objective sums, and every final interval transfer.                             22

23                                                                                             23

24
                                AW.4. Why both refinements matter                              24

25     For three action scores 0, −1, −2, the second action defeats the reference score        25

26   zero but cannot win the full menu. A reference-only barrier can therefore remain          26

27   inconclusive even though the complete winner event is empty. This is a distinction        27

28   about the implemented argmin, not a change in the economic action set.                    28

29     For the shared-coefficient restriction, take θ ∈ [−1, 1] and two cells. An adverse      29

30   action in the first cell requires θ ≥ 1; one in the second requires θ ≤ −1. Both          30

31   events are separately feasible but their intersection is empty. If each adverse action    31

32   contributes 1/2 to aggregate cost, independent cell bounds allow a cost increase          32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                         163

1    of one. The compatible bound is 1/2, and it is attained by one endpoint of the          1

2    parameter interval. This supplies a strict example for the improvement claimed by       2

3    the theorem.                                                                            3

4      Pair restrictions need not characterize every feasible policy. With three unit- 4
5    weight alternatives and all three pair conflicts, an assignment relaxation permits      5

6    mass 1/2 on each alternative, even though a zero–one assignment can select at most      6

7    one. The corresponding fractional support can therefore be larger than the true         7

8    worst cost. More generally, pairwise intersection does not imply joint intersection     8

9    of all winner regions. The manuscript consequently calls the computed object an         9

10   enclosure, not the exact attainable policy set or the exact worst optimizer output. 10
11   Additional certified joint events may strengthen it without invalidating any existing   11

12   bound.                                                                                  12

13                                                                                           13

14                                                                                           14
                         AW.5. Work, storage and continuation scope
15                                                                                           15

16     Let P be the number of admitted alternatives, K the number of declared joint          16

17   events, m the maximum menu size, d the readout dimension, N the number of cells,        17

18   and E the number of retained pair cuts at a tolerance. A singleton event adds at most   18

19   m − 1 rows and a pair event at most 2(m − 1) rows to the same fixed qualification       19

20   problem. The experiment executes exactly P + K qualification problems. It does          20

21   not claim a dimension-independent bound on an LP solver’s arithmetic or bit             21

22   complexity.                                                                             22

23     Once multipliers have been obtained, checking a qualification certificate takes       23

24   a number of rational operations linear in its feature, derivative and event coeffi- 24
25   cients. Checking one assignment dual takes O( P + N + E) rational operations and        25

26   storage linear in its retained variables, cells and cuts. Constructing and solving      26

27   the assignment LP is separate work. The executed pair budget limits qualification       27

28   work without assuming that unchecked intersections are feasible or infeasible. Pre- 28
29   viously purchased conditional integration remains an explicit prerequisite, not free    29

30   computation.                                                                            30

31     The identity transferring conditional contrasts to aggregate policy cost holds        31

32   because the policies share the stated future controller and only their first acquired   32
     164

1    action varies. A longer horizon can use the same identity when its entire fixed- 1
2    continuation conditional cost is enclosed. Jointly refitting that future controller   2

3    changes the estimand and requires another qualification argument. The results         3

4    therefore do not claim that the present pair graph certifies arbitrary changes at     4

5    every date or regenerates the large vector-control descendant tree.                   5

26   distinct from a signed ranking; both can hold for a small nonzero cost difference. The displayed six- 26
27   decimal endpoints are descriptive and do not decide a boundary test. Each interval is displayed on       27

28   two lines, with its lower endpoint above its upper endpoint; no endpoint digit or catalogue row          28

29   is omitted.   Every alternative previously admitted up to 2−12 is included in the                        29

30   singleton roster. The fixed pair-selection rule, including its deterministic cost-based                  30

31   ordering and tie handling, is part of the source lock. Its execution contributes no                      31

32   additional independent cost row or fit. The 24 model–tolerance records and 36 class                      32

1                            AY.2. Selected-service price and fee breakpoints                                    1

2                                                                                                                2
       Every comparison is selected minus comparator. If the upper cost endpoint is u,
3                                                                                                                3
     then the guaranteed extra-fee ceiling at zero resource price is −λu. With resource
4                                                                                                                4
     prices it becomes −λu − p · (wbj − wk ). Negative ceilings are retained: they do not
5                                                                                                                5
     support a positive fee based solely on policy quality. A declared normalized loss
6                                                                                                                6
     margin does not calibrate λ or a monetary fee.

                         AY.3. Diagnostics and independently checked kernels
26                                                                                                               26

27     The new exact fixtures compare path enumeration with forward occupation re- 27
28   cursion, test the candidate-occupation counterexample, retain ties, reject impossible                       28

29   shared-parameter assignments, and exhibit a fractional outer bound that is valid but                        29

30   unattained. Separate tests check the ReLU/min-plus identity, label-noise enclosure,                         30

31   persistent-exploration recursion, sufficient screen closure, correct lower-versus- 31
32   upper work comparison, dated allocation, and price-region boundaries.                                       32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                           175

1      The frozen evaluation records zero new fits. Predictor warning rates, fallback          1

2    frequencies and zero-progress query probes are not identified by that fact. Earlier       2

3    fitting warnings and their affected services remain in the inherited experiment           3

4    records. The R70 audit covers 188 service processes and records its prediction- 4
5    replay differences; it is not a 336-service manifest. The source-specific diagnostic      5

6    index records these distinctions instead of assigning unsupported aggregate counts        6

7    to the current evaluation.                                                                7

8      The large inherited vector descendant tree, a derivative branch-and-bound com- 8
9    parator, energy calibration, and a population experiment for a newly trained cross- 9
10   regime proposal require their own numerical evidence. Exact finite-state fixtures,        10

11   whole-bin evaluation of the nine attained controllers, or a successful PDF build do       11

12   not silently certify those different objects. This evidence distinction does not remove   12

13   the original algorithms, economic applications or full-horizon theorems from the          13

14   manuscript.                                                                               14

15                                                                                             15

16                                                                                             16
                                   AY.4. Publication identity
17                                                                                             17

18     Current document hashes, source hashes, test counts, exact replay status and            18

19   clean reconstruction status are recorded under the current revision’s audit directory. 19
20   A publication gate rejects absent current PDFs, bibliographies or source-matched          20

21   checks. Visual inspection is a separately signed-off observation against actual PDF       21

22   hashes; neither a compiler exit code nor an inherited release record is such an           22

23   observation. The full preservation edition retains every preceding active substantive     23

24   section. Its actual page count is reported rather than concealed by reducing type         24

25   size or deleting mathematical content.                                                    25

       The singular-transition example of Section L separates a genuine original-law
8                                                                                                           8

9                                                                                                           9
     charge converge with the approximation distance, although the total-variation
10                                                                                                          10
     discrepancy does not. All calculations below are exact rational arithmetic.

23     A separate three-date, two-state fixture enumerates all 64 deterministic com- 23
24   plete binary policies. Independent backward dynamic programming evaluates                              24

25   each under the original transition kernel and computes its original optimum. The                       25

26   proposed certificate is formed from a different numerical kernel, arbitrary stored                     26

10                                                                                             10
       The current reproduction command requires the ordinary article, technical sup-
11                                                                                             11
     plement, numerical appendix and response sources, rather than substituting an
12                                                                                             12
     older compiled article. It checks the preserved active input list, reconstructs the re-
13                                                                                             13
     quired tables, executes the selected mathematical and numerical checks, and records
14                                                                                             14
     their exact source and output identities. Each executed check has its own exit sta-
15                                                                                             15
     tus and log. A clean typesetting reconstruction removes inherited auxiliary and
16                                                                                             16
     bibliography outputs. The final release gate additionally requires a rendered-page
17                                                                                             17
     inspection record tied to the actual four PDF hashes.
18                                                                                             18
       A compiled candidate, a passed arithmetic test and a visually inspected package
19                                                                                             19
     are distinct delivery states. The current manifest determines which states have
20                                                                                             20
     actually been attained. The full matched large-vector comparator study, measured
21                                                                                             21
     energy and application-specific monetary calibration remain separate evidence

     obligations; they are not inferred from the new transport theorem or a successful
23                                                                                             23
     build. The original diffusion, recursive-preference, temporal-self and game results

20     The adversarial tests also retain atoms at a quantile, zero-probability outcomes,                     20

21   exact ties, malformed probability inputs, infeasible actions, negative or inconsis- 21
22   tent allowances, terminal error and correct date weights. A one-date singular-law                       22

23   example attains the signed discrepancy charge while its symmetric counterpart is                        23

24   twice as large. A two-date example rejects cancelling a discrepancy that is action                      24

25   independent only within each state. A four-outcome example separates static from                        25

26   nested tail risk. The tail-transport factor is attained as the risk level approaches                    26

27   one. These tests guard the original economic objective rather than selecting a more                     27

28   convenient replacement.                                                                                 28

5           APPENDIX BD: I NDEPENDENT DIAGNOSTICS FOR CENTERED RISK                             5

6                                        QUALIFICATION                                          6

7                                                                                               7
       This section reports deterministic diagnostics of Section 7. They use finite rational
8                                                                                               8
     models with three states, two feasible actions and original nested average value at
9                                                                                               9
     risk. They are not additional investment observations, new fitting attempts, or a
10                                                                                              10
     rerun of the inherited largest-vector problem. The production calculation uses the
11                                                                                              11
     vertices of a capped probability simplex. The independent evaluator minimizes the
12                                                                                              12
     scalar threshold representation of average value at risk and performs a separately
13                                                                                              13
     coded original-value recursion. It does not import the centered-risk implementation.
14                                                                                              14
       The declared horizons are 2, 3, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, risk levels are 0, 1/2, 3/4, and the
15                                                                                              15
     four shared continuation parameters are −1, 0, 1, 2. At date t, state s ∈ {0, 1, 2} and
16                                                                                              16
     action a ∈ {0, 1}, transition weights are
17                                                                                              17

18          (1 + (t + s + a) mod 3,     1 + (2s + a) mod 4,      1 + (t + 2a) mod 3),           18

19                                                                                              19

20
     normalized by their sum. Costs are ((s + 2a + t) mod 5 − 2)/7 + a/5, the discount          20

21
     is 3/4, and the terminal vector is (0, 1/3, −1/4). These fully specified diagnostic        21

22
     primitives avoid a hidden change in objective between implementations. They are            22

23
     separate from the paper’s economic calibration.                                            23

24
       A single parameter determines the stored circuit throughout a configuration:             24

25                                                     θ+1                                      25
                          f t,θ (s) = ( T − t)θ/20 +       (s/2 − 1/2)+
26                                                      7                                       26

27                                      t mod 3                                                 27
                                    +           (1/2 − s/2)+ .
28
                                           11                                                   28

29   The implemented policy is greedy for this circuit under the original one-step eval- 29
30   uation, with smaller-action tie breaking. No training claim is attached to these           30

31   prescribed circuits. Residual intervals use radii 0, 1/64, 1/8 times (1 + s)/3; termi- 31
32   nal intervals use the same statewise radius. The exact configuration records retain        32
     186

1    the complete input, every centered residual and risk penalty, lower and upper                              1

2    potentials, a feasible maximizing risk occupation, and all strict kernel-exclusion                         2

3    certificates. Unpruned potential bounds are computed before any exclusion, pre- 3
4    venting circular error boxes.                                                                              4

           0.75    Quadratic − Spline                  [-0.029729, 0.029734]       0.022864     Yes
14                                                                                                               14
           0.75    ReLU − Spline                       [-0.029037, 0.030625]       0.022963     Yes
15                                                                                                               15
     The unit is an independent initial state under the declared dyadic uniform distribution. There are
16                                                                                                               16
     twelve fixed comparisons in one family. Equivalence uses the prespecified margin 1/16 in normalized
17   cost units. Every interval includes zero: none identifies strict superiority. The calibration selector is   17

18   online risk-DP at both risk levels.                                                                         18

19                                                                                                               19
     after evaluation. The fixed sample size, margin and confidence rule are those in the
20                                                                                                               20
     pre-evaluation protocol.
21                                                                                                               21

22     Numerical components. The original innovation is uniform on [−1/32, 1/32]. The                            22

23   one-date original terminal risk is integrated analytically and enclosed outwards. 23
24   First-date integration uses full-bin bounds. A half-bin contributes the explicit con- 24
25   tinuation allowance 15/(32n); a complete terminal controller contributes at most                            25

26   2−20 before first-date discounting. State observation and terminal optimization are                         26

27   bounded together by 2−21 + 30 2−40 < 2−20 . Arithmetic rounding is propagated                               27

28   through the interval operations rather than replaced by an unverified final decimal                         28

29   tolerance. These allowances overlap with conservative bin bounds in the code and                            29

30   must not be added again to the final cost interval. State covers qualify residuals;                         30

31   they do not change the transition law. There is no numerical entropic evaluation in                         31

32   this experiment.                                                                                            32
                               NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                                       189

12   The first 32 prespecified rows of every arm are retained, with 128 bins each. Full includes unpruned      12
     bound construction and sorting. Pruned additionally pays for the strict proof and retained-bin sorting.
13                                                                                                             13
     Both return identical upper-tail values. Repeated sorts are timing diagnostics, not new economic
14                                                                                                             14
     observations; these measurements show no end-to-end gain from screening.
15                                                                                                             15

16                                                                                                             16

17                                                                                                             17

18                                                                                                             18

19                                                                                                             19

20     What the independent implementation checks. A separately written Fraction im- 20
21   plementation reconstructs the original quadratic shock polynomial from primitive                          21

22   evaluations, computes AVaR through a primal threshold integral, and solves its                            22

23   own one-date action problems. It imports neither the production interval kernel nor                       23

24   the production rational terminal fallback. The audit checks the six serialized fits,                      24

25   eighteen exact feature evaluations, all 16,384 raw row identities and hashes, and                         25

26   twelve complete paired-inference replays. For sixteen prespecified policy fixtures, a                     26

27   finer 512-bin independent continuous-policy enclosure must be contained in the                            27

28   reported production enclosure, not merely intersect it. All these checks pass in the                      28

29   local execution. The inference replay shares the production interval formula and                          29

30   is identified as replay, not a second independently derived statistical implementa- 30
31   tion. This is kernel-level independent implementation, not external authorship or a                       31

32   second full learning and largest-vector stack.                                                            32

14                         BE.2. The full inherited tightening distribution                               14

15                                                                                                        15
       The new summary uses all 252 finite-risk configurations, including equality and
16                                                                                                        16
     no-cut configurations. Absolute improvements and relative-improvement quartiles
17                                                                                                        17
     are stored alongside every exact rational scalar and state-dependent bound. These
18                                                                                                        18
     calculations reorganize previously generated mathematical evidence and create no
19                                                                                                        19
     new fits or independent observations. The absorbing example continues to mean
20                                                                                                        20
     zero policy loss with a nonzero continuation bias; it is not an exact continuation fit.
21                                                                                                        21
     The existing rational bit-growth records remain separate from a measured exact-
22                                                                                                        22
     versus-interval arithmetic crossover, which has not been run here.

24                                                                                                        24
                           BE.3. Source, execution and release boundaries
25                                                                                                        25

26     The controlling advisory report is the R92 review deposited on 13 October 2026. 26
27   Its reviewed manuscript is the complete R92 article. The R94 development branch is                   27

28   a descendant of that manuscript; its paper entry point had not yet incorporated its                  28

29   new continuous-risk implementation. R95 retains the original title and author, adds                  29

30   the continuous primitive proof and executed comparison, and relocates fifteen com- 30
31   plete historical main-text sections to this appendix. Each moved file is byte-identical              31

32   to the R92 source. The only inherited scientific-section prose amendment clarifies                   32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: APPENDIX                          191

1    that the earlier experiments use expectation, while the new explicitly specified ex- 1
2    periment uses nested AVaR. No original mathematical claim or adverse observation         2

3    is removed.                                                                              3

4      The cloud R94 attempt stopped before scientific execution when creation of its         4

5    source branch was rejected. The available source-and-evidence artifact therefore         5

6    contains sources but no completed primary run. R95 executed those sources locally. 6
7    A serialized actor and calibration hash was written before evaluation; it was not        7

8    remotely timestamped or published before that local evaluation. The source checks        8

9    in the evaluator reject changed models or changed frozen numerical files. Two tool- 9
10   interrupted local starts are retained as interrupted attempts, and the completed run     10

11   is identified separately. Repeated evaluations of the same pseudorandom stream do        11

12   not increase the number of independent observations.                                     12

13     New-science tests, inherited-test replay, independent continuous-kernel valida- 13
14   tion, clean typesetting and actual-PDF inspection are separate release gates. The        14

15   downloaded R92 publication artifact intentionally omits several older numerical          15

16   datasets and external historical code directories. Missing-fixture errors in replay of   16

17   those inherited suites are recorded rather than skipped or reclassified as passed. The   17

18   full repository materialization procedure preserves those historical dependencies;       18

19   the local export is not evidence that their full replay has passed. The publication      19

20   continuation restores the original repository dependencies and records its new           20

21   source-bound replay and delivery separately under audit/remote95. Historical             21

22   local-only receipts remain unchanged. An ARM64 execution, energy measurement             22

23   or full largest-vector replication is reported only with its own record.                 23

24                                                                                            24

30   every generated table to that record. Subsequent manuscript edits do not change the      30

31   source attribution of previously generated scientific tables. Repeated architecture      31

32   runs use the same actors and states and do not create independent observations.          32
